# Decisions

Newest first. One dated paragraph per decision: context, decision, alternatives rejected.

## 2026-11-27 — M10 WP2: initial cap 100 KB, a per-route table in `check:bundle`, one initial file, the lethal solver stays eager

- **Context:** WP0's baseline scores Lighthouse performance 100 and accessibility 100 on all six routes at the fixed throttle (RTT 40 ms, 10 Mbit/s, CPU 4x), so there was no score to fix. WP0's JSX in `App` and `Footer` made Rolldown emit `react/jsx-runtime` as a second initial chunk (84.3 KB gz in 2 files, against 83.5 KB in 1).
- **Decision: the initial cap is 100 KB gzipped** (was 300 KB; PROMPT.md's "under 300 KB" is still met). Measured 83.7 KB in 1 file.
- **Decision: one initial file, by a chunking rule.** `app/vite.config.ts` `build.rolldownOptions.output.codeSplitting.groups: [{ name: 'index', tags: ['$initial'] }]` puts everything the entry reaches statically in one chunk. Tried first: a group on `node_modules/react` (worse: 3 files, a 0.1 KB runtime chunk appears). `app/test/route-chunks.test.ts` asserts the initial closure is one file (it fails with the rule removed: 2 files). Before and after, Lighthouse medians of 3 runs: transfer 1 KB lower on every route, LCP and FCP within 1 to 2 ms on five routes and noise on `/meta` (777 before, 744 after; its run-to-run spread is 30 ms), score 100 both.
- **Decision: `check:bundle` prints a per-route table** (`scripts/bundle-routes.ts`, pure and unit-tested; `scripts/bundle-budget.ts` the CLI): a lazy page's chunk plus the chunks it imports statically that the initial bundle does not hold; the two workers are reported (117.6 and 118.0 KB gz), not capped. `ROUTE_CAPS` holds Play only, at 215 KB (measured 194.7 KB, +10%, rounded up); the lead may change it. Routes without a cap print only.
- **Decision: the lethal solver stays in the shared ai chunk; no lazy import.** Measured by source-map attribution: `ai/src/search/lethal/*` is 34 KB raw of the 196 KB ai chunk (about 11 KB gz as an isolated stream, and `bound.ts` and `profile.ts` are 14.6 KB of that and are used on the main thread by the coach card and `defenceBound`, so they could not move). What could move (`search.ts`, `lethal.ts`, `analyse.ts`, `public.ts`, used by `coach/blunder.ts`) is 18.6 KB raw, about 5 KB gz. Whole ai chunk compile on this machine: 1.7 ms (V8 `vm.Script`, median of 15), so about 7 ms at 4x, of which the movable part is under 2 ms; Lighthouse on `/play` at the fixed throttle: TBT 0 ms, LCP 685 ms, performance 100 (the requirement for moving it was TBT over 200 ms at 4x). The route test "over 1.1 times the median route" is true of Play (194.7 KB against a median of about 72.8) but because of Game, the engine and the review flow, not the solver. A lazy import would add a status line and a failure path to the coach for about 2.5% of the route. Revisit if the nightly's `/play` TBT passes 100 ms.
- **Decision: no font change.** The two `@fontsource` imports pull ten `@font-face` rules but only the latin subsets are fetched (unicode-range); Lighthouse FCP is the boot line's 203 ms. Trimming subsets would cut the service worker's 308 KB precache by about 190 KB at the risk of missing glyphs in card text; not done without a failing score. A `<link rel="preload">` for the two latin files needs a `transformIndexHtml` plugin in `vite.config.ts`; not needed at LCP 440 ms on `/`.
- **Decision: the rules digest is already lazy.** `src/rules/data.ts` (185 KB raw, 60 KB gz) is a dynamic entry that no chunk imports statically; `route-chunks.test.ts` asserts it and that Home, the Learn hub, About and Rules do not reach it.

## 2026-11-27 — M10 WP6: release shell; licences before the build; CSP by a post-build step

- **Context:** WP6 owns About, the error boundary, the offline line, the 404, icons, licences, CSP, docs and the deploy check. The lead limited `app/vite.config.ts` to `versionInfo` (WP2 edits the build options in parallel).
- **Decision: `third-party.txt` is generated by `prebuild` into `app/public/` (git-ignored), not after the build.** The service worker's file list is read in `closeBundle`, before any `postbuild`; a file in `public/` is copied into `dist` first, so it is precached and the deploy check can fetch it. The script (`scripts/licences.ts`) walks `dependencies` through `node_modules` instead of `npm ls` (a fixture tree tests it; no process spawn). It also fails on a known licence with no licence text. `@optcg/*` workspaces are walked but not listed. Today: 5 packages, MIT and OFL-1.1.
- **Decision: the CSP is written into `dist/index.html` by `postbuild` (`scripts/csp.ts`)**, built from `IMAGE_SOURCES` (enabled hosts only), as the first element of `<head>`. It is not a Vite plugin, because of the vite.config limit. The service worker fetches `index.html` from the network, so the policy reaches the cached copy. `style-src` has `'unsafe-inline'` (React style attributes, the boot style); scripts have none. The e2e runs the home page, a Rookie turn (AI worker), the builder, a drill and the text-face fallback with no violation.
- **Decision: the error boundary resets on a route change** by reading the path itself (`ErrorBoundary` wraps a class `Boundary` with `resetKey`), so `App.tsx` needed no change. Unit tests call the class's static methods and render the failure page without a DOM; the real throw (a route chunk replaced by a throwing module), focus, header, store and clipboard are Playwright.
- **Decision: version details come from `version.json` at run time**, which the service worker does not precache (`shellFiles` excludes it, deliberately: it changes every build). Offline, About says the details could not be loaded. Not changed here (sw.ts and `shellFiles` are not WP6's).
- **Decision: the About link in Settings is a section, and the Offline line is mounted there**; Home's mount is a one-file patch for the lead (`routes/Home.tsx` is not WP6's).
- **Decision: README keeps its Legal paragraph and gains the footer's notice sentence above it**, so the three notice strings are equal (a test compares them). The licence reads "to be chosen by the owner" everywhere; no LICENSE file and no `license` field. Version 1.0.0 is the build's reported release only; no tag.

## 2026-11-27 — M10 WP0: shared wiring once, the Lighthouse and frame harnesses, pinned versions

- **Context:** WP1, WP2, WP3, WP4, WP6 and WP7 run in parallel and must share no file; WP0 owns every shared seam.
- **Decision: `App.tsx` mounts `Guard` once per route.** `Guard` is one error boundary (`ErrorBoundary`, scope = the route's name), then an optional `StoreGate` (given the route's path), then the route's `Suspense`; `Home` and the not-found page go through the same table. The shell has its own boundary. Gated now (WP1 fills the gate): Play, Tutorial, Decks, Settings, Reviews (list and review), the lesson pages, and the M9 pages drills, drill-start, drill-run and progress (`GATED_M9`). Ungated: Home, Cards, About, Meta, Learn hub, Rules. `StoreGate` takes `{ children, fallback?, path? }`; `path` lets WP1 pick the heading without editing `App.tsx`. `ErrorBoundary` takes `{ children, scope? }`. Both are stubs that render their children, so WP1 and WP6 change one file each. Folding the repeated `Suspense` into `Guard` saved bytes in the entry chunk (the table had 14 copies).
- **Decision: the frame recorder's statistics are a separate pure file** (`app/e2e/support/perf-stats.ts`, re-exported by `perf.ts`), so a vitest unit test imports them without loading `@playwright/test`.
- **Decision: `serve-dist --spa` for Lighthouse.** GitHub Pages answers a client-side route with status 404 and the 404.html body; Lighthouse refuses to audit a document with a 4xx status. The Lighthouse run therefore serves `index.html` with 200 for an extensionless unknown path. The bytes are the same as on Pages; the default (no flag) keeps Pages' behaviour.
- **Decision: pinned versions** `lighthouse` 13.6.0 (published 2026-10-27, the latest stable at least two weeks old) and `chrome-launcher` 1.2.3 (2026-11-05), both exact in root `devDependencies`.
- **Decision: package scripts for packages not yet built guard on the file** (`licences`, `a11y:sheet`, `test:visual`, `test:audit`) and print "not built yet", exiting 0, so no later WP edits `package.json`. They look for `scripts/licences.ts`, `scripts/a11y-sheet.ts`, `app/e2e/visual/` and `app/e2e/m10-audit.spec.ts`: a WP that names its file otherwise must change the script's path.
- **Decision: `index.html` boot style.** Dark-theme values only (the app has no JS theme switch yet; `data-theme="light"` is opt-in), duplicated from `tokens.css` and checked by `app/test/head.test.ts`.

## 2026-11-27 — M10 plan: measured performance with fixed throttles and nightly bounds, store off the critical path, an audited WCAG claim, a release shell, pixel baselines on Linux

- **Context:** PLAN row 21 (20 h). Done criteria: Lighthouse performance and accessibility at least 90; a WCAG 2.2 AA audit with manual NVDA and VoiceOver passes; a 60 fps trace during card animations on a throttled profile; the deployed URL in STATUS.md. Carried: BACKLOG §2 and §12, the reviews' severity 2 and 1 carries, the Warlord wait on the worker, the shared ai chunk, owner session 3. Nothing had been measured: no Lighthouse run, no frame trace, no favicon, manifest, About page, privacy statement, licence, error boundary or changelog existed. The carried items and the criteria are about 34 h; the plan cuts to 20 and names what it cuts.
- **Decision (lead): performance is measured with a fixed throttle, reported whatever the verdict, and asserted only in `perf.yml` (nightly and dispatch).** Lighthouse (the `lighthouse` package, desktop form factor, 4× CPU, 40 ms RTT, 10 Mbit/s, median of 3, 5 at release, against a gzip static server that mimics Pages) asserts performance ≥ 85 and accessibility ≥ 90 as the bound and prints the requirement (90); the release gate asserts the requirement itself on the release candidate. Frames: `requestAnimationFrame` deltas and long tasks at 4× CPU through CDP, a Chrome trace per scenario as an artifact, requirement p95 ≤ 20 ms and p99 ≤ 34 ms, bound 1.5×. This is testing.md §2 applied: a mechanism per push (the gate, the warm-up message, the boundary), the number nightly. Rejected: `@lhci/cli` (its assertion model is a point threshold and its default uploads to public storage; the programmatic API gives the same scores with our reporting); Lighthouse's default mobile profile (the board shows a size screen below its minimum by design, so it would measure that screen; one mobile run is recorded for information); asserting a score per push (a loaded runner moves it).
- **Decision (lead): the store opens in parallel and gated routes show their heading and a status until it is ready.** BACKLOG §12. Home, Cards, Meta, Rules and About paint at once; Play, Tutorial, Decks, Reviews, Progress, Settings and Drills wait behind `StoreGate`, which renders the page heading (so focus and title are right) and a polite "Opening your saved data…". The 3 s cap and the memory fallback stay. Rejected: keeping the await (first paint hostage to IndexedDB); rendering every route immediately with an empty store (a game saved to memory then migrated, a tutorial credit lost).
- **Decision (lead): bundle budgets tighten and become per route.** Initial JS cap 300 → 100 KB gz (83.5 now); a per-route table in `check:bundle`; Game's cap set from the measurement × 1.1. The lethal solver (about 30 KB of the 196 KB shared ai chunk) becomes a dynamic import at the coach's first open only if the measurement shows the Game route or TBT over line; otherwise it stays and the cost is recorded. Rejected: a blanket lazy solver (a loading state in the coach for a number nobody measured); capping workers (off the main thread; reported).
- **Decision (lead): the WCAG 2.2 AA claim is audited in three layers and worded for what was done.** Automated axe over a route manifest at both sizes (a test fails when a route has no entry), the 200% and 400% zoom, text-spacing, forced-colour, reduced-motion and colour-vision checks, focus-not-obscured and target-size sweeps; a manual row for every A and AA criterion with its evidence; NVDA scripted (Chrome, Firefox) and VoiceOver by the owner from one generated sheet. STATUS says exactly that and that no outside screen-reader user has used the app unless S3's invitee did (accessibility R10). Rejected: axe alone (it cannot judge reading order, names in context or announcements); a hand-copied sheet (drifts: generated from the harness steps and tested); claiming conformance without the owner's VoiceOver pass (the claim waits for it).
- **Decision (lead): the AI wait is measured in parts before anything is changed; the worker is warmed at Setup.** The Warlord's ms-mode wait was 1.8 to 4.1 s against a 1,500 ms budget and a 1.8 s target; the split (transfer, setup, search) decides, the worker is spawned and its session built when Setup mounts and a one-iteration plan warms it, and the `peekNext` wait stops counting as board time (M5a-20). The nightly keeps 2,700 ms (1,800 × 1.5). If the target is still missed the number is reported and BACKLOG gets it. Rejected: raising the budget (it is the product's strength), a larger bound (a regression would pass), warming on page load (an idle worker for visitors who never play).
- **Decision (lead): release is 1.0.0 with a shell the product lacked.** About (version from `version.json`, credits with each source's terms, third-party licences generated at build and failing it on an unknown or copyleft licence, the privacy statement), an error boundary that never touches saved data, an offline line, a 404 with focus, our own favicon and manifest, CHANGELOG, README, a post-deploy check that the live `version.json` matches the build. A CSP meta in the production build is attempted and dropped if the product's own origins cannot satisfy it. The privacy statement is proved by an e2e that lists every request origin and cookie. The `LICENSE` is added only when the owner chooses (proposal: MIT for our code, with a notice that card data, names and art are not ours). Rejected: waiting for a custom domain and headers (Pages cannot set them; the meta tag is the available form); an analytics-free "crash report" upload (nothing leaves the browser).
- **Decision (lead): visual regression is pixel comparison on Linux, with art blocked.** 14 screens at both sizes (the PROMPT's five first), baselines produced only by a dispatch workflow on the CI image and committed after the lead reads them against principles.md, a `visual` job per push (a pinned renderer is deterministic) that moves to the nightly if two pushes in a row fail on renderer noise. Every art host is aborted so no Bandai art is compared or committed. Rejected: macOS baselines (anti-aliasing differs); comparing real art (hotlinked and watermarked; also forbidden to commit); a lower threshold to hide noise.
- **Decision (lead): the carried items are cut as the plan's table says.** Fixed: the builder keyboard (M6-3, N-2: keyboard operability is the audit's business), M6-11, N-4, the midline overlap, the Setup layout. BACKLOG: M6-14 (the one item BACKLOG §2 says M10 must close: it is features, with no done-criteria or WCAG link, and the M6 reviewer accepted the deferral; the owner may swap it for WP8's remainder), M3-18 chips and drag (2.5.7 and 2.5.8 are met), M3-23 leftovers, L-15, M4b and M5a nits other than the two named, M5b seed debt and bias-kind merge, LF-R8 and W8-N1 (engine events and recorded flows), M8-2 and the M9 carries. Honest size: about 34 h carried against 20, and the review's fix round and S3's findings are on top.
- **Cut order:** CSP; reading the colour-vision screenshots; WP7's two copy items; the offline line on Home; the optional S3 seed; WP8 down to the PROMPT's five screens; the Lighthouse mobile run. **Never cut:** the two measurement harnesses and their reported numbers, zero-violation axe over every route, the audit document and the screen-reader sheet, the store off the critical path, About with credits and privacy, the error boundary, the S3 kit, the independent review. If the baseline fails the criteria by more than WP1 to WP3 can fix, the hours move from WP7 and WP8; the criteria are not lowered.
- **Not built (recorded):** a native screen-reader user's test (owner's invitee, if any); real-GPU frame numbers in CI (the owner's `--headed` run is the reference); a custom domain and response headers; an in-app bug reporter; a second language.
- **Decision (lead, amending the draft): visual regression is nightly.** the pixel comparison runs in the nightly (and on dispatch), not per push: renderer noise on a pixel diff is exactly the non-defect failure docs/runbooks/testing.md keeps out of per-push suites. Per push keeps the saved screenshots and axe. A failed nightly diff uploads the diff images.

## 2026-11-27 — Tests: deterministic per push, timing measured nightly, rate checks by confidence interval

- **Context:** CI run 45927059028 (main e8a76f3b) failed `warlord.spec.ts:116` ("turn 6: waiting on the worker": a millisecond bound on a shared runner; the nightly 45618763945 failed the same way). Nightly 45821285318 failed `game-plan-g3` (OP15-058 seed 2, 0.740 against 0.80) and 45618763945 failed `strength` (OP17-058 seed 1, 0.737 against 0.80 over 19 situations): a threshold over a small sample that any code change reshuffles. Under load `budget.test.ts` and `why-differential.test.ts` failed and passed alone (ms p95 bounds; a 120 s timeout set at 3x the idle time). Also seen in the last 60 runs and already fixed on main: `noop-activation` timing out at 120 s (b96b5f9f), `fonts.spec` first-render race (6440923c), `review-m8-states` axe name mismatch (later commits).
- **Decision:** the rule is in `docs/runbooks/testing.md`. Per push a test never asserts wall-clock time: a budget is tested as a budget (iterations, or an injected clock), UI pacing on fake timers, waits are hang guards, `vi.setConfig({ testTimeout })` is scaled by `TEST_TIMEOUT_SCALE` (default 2) in `test-setup/timeouts.ts`. Millisecond product requirements are kept and measured nightly (`npm run test:timing`, `E2E_FULL=1`), at the product figure x 1.5, with observed numbers written to `test-results/` (`timing-budget.json`, `timing-hint.json`, `ai-turns-<project>.jsonl`). A rate over sampled games is `expectRateAtLeast` / `expectRateAtMost` / `expectTalliesMeetThresholds` (`ai/test/support/rate.ts`, `stats.ts`, own tests in `ai/test/stats.test.ts`): it fails only when the one-sided 99% Clopper-Pearson bound is wholly on the wrong side of the target; messages print rate, n, bound and target.
- **Before and after, test by test:**
  - `budget.test.ts`: wall-clock p95 per push; now per push rollouts <= iteration budget x1.2 (Pirate also on a binding 40/8 budget) and an injected-clock check on the ms budgets (10 ms a read, <= x1.2 + one tick); real ms nightly (`TIMING=1`) at x1.5 (was x1.2).
  - `hint.test.ts` budget: wall-clock p95 per push; now injected clock within 2,500 of 2,000; real p95 <= 3,000 ms nightly.
  - `usability-s2.test.ts` Yamato hint on `{ ms: 2000 }`: now injected clock per push, real clock nightly.
  - `warlord.spec.ts`, `pirate.spec.ts`: ms bounds asserted per push; now asserted only with `E2E_FULL`, wait on worker at product figure x1.5 (Pirate 1,500 ms, Warlord 2,700, Emperor 7,500; before 1,000 / 4,000 / 8,500); per push they play and report the turns. New `app/test/ai-pacing.test.ts` holds §5.2 deterministically (real `GameController`, fake timers: the late 13-move turn 9,160 virtual ms, bound 9,700, 10,280 with compression off; early Warlord turns 2,040 / 4,240 / 3,880 against 8,000).
  - `review-m8-gate.spec.ts`: 60 s bound per push; now hang guard per push, 90 s nightly (added to the nightly Playwright line). `review-gate.test.ts`: on demand only; now in the nightly, 90 s bound, marker fractions by interval.
  - `strength.test.ts`, `strength-warlord.test.ts`, `game-plan-g1/g2/g3` tallies, `blunder-corpus`, `target-corpus`: point-estimate thresholds; now interval checks. Honest cost: a check can only see a shortfall n can distinguish (12 games against 75%: 4 wins or fewer fails; 200 against 95%: 181 or fewer), so the per-push 12 to 20 game gates catch gross breaks only and the nightly 100/200-game series carry the power.
  - `store-import` (5 s parse), `decklist` (50 ms): ms bounds dropped for correctness assertions; `rules-showme`/`tutorial` `until` 8 s to 25 s, `rewind`/`sw` waits widened, as hang guards.
  - Left alone, already deterministic: `polite-queue`, `tutorial-timing`, `review-pipeline`, `lethal-bound`, engine property tests.
- **Fail-on-regression evidence (mutation applied, run, reverted):**
  - *Search spends only half its budget accounting (2x slower)*, `mcts.ts` and `pirate.ts`: per-push `budget.test.ts` failed 6 of 6 (e.g. warlord plan rollouts [8000 x6] against 4,800; injected clock 5,010 of 2,500 ms; emperor 20,000 against 12,000; pirate [1010, 840, 780] against 600). Nightly `TIMING=1`: warlord plan p95 5,156 ms against 3,975 and emperor 12,305 against 9,450 failed; Pirate's real-ms plan is 38 ms of a 500 ms budget (its search ends structurally), so the nightly cannot see it and the per-push check does.
  - *Playbook bias inverted* (OP17-058 bias seed 1 bonus -50): `strength.test.ts` game-plan run failed with "OP17-058 seed 1 ... observed 0/10 = 0.0%; 99.0% one-sided upper bound 36.9% ... against a target of at least 80.0%". Removing the bias instead left the rate at 23/27: search alone finds that line, so that bias is not load-bearing for the assertion.
  - *AI turn compression disabled* (`aiDwell` always 400 ms): `ai-pacing.test.ts` failed with "turn 8: animation and holds (13 moves): expected 10280 to be less than or equal to 9700"; `pacing.test.ts` fails too.
  - Stats helper: `stats.test.ts` proves by exact enumeration that a true rate on the line fails at most 1% of samples, that 77/104 and 14/19 against 80% (the two nightly failures) pass and 40/104, 5/19 fail.
  - *Corpus ceilings*: lowering `TARGET_GAP` to 0.05 and `GAP_THRESHOLD` to 0.02 left both corpora passing under the old and the new assertion alike (other gates in the detectors bind), so those mutations prove nothing about the ceilings; the at-most logic is covered by `stats.test.ts` (40/300 against 1% fails, 5/300 passes). Not mutation-proven; recorded as left.
- **Rejected:** retries or longer waits on the pacing spec (they hide a real hang and keep a stopwatch in a per-push test); widening nightly bounds past x1.5 (a runner that exceeds them is reported, with numbers); a global fake `performance.now` for the e2e (the worker runs on its own clock).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP8: the tutorial-keyboard flake was a real race in the board's "G then H"; guide source links are 24 px targets; the M9 e2e share one axe helper and one screenshot folder

- **Context:** `tutorial-keyboard.spec.ts` failed under load at both sizes and passed alone. Step 5 ("Being attacked") pressed G, H, Space, P, Enter and nothing was staged.
- **Decision (the flake):** the board's key handler (`Game.tsx`) is re-subscribed in a passive effect after each render and reads `goto` from React state, so a G followed by H before that effect ran saw `goto = false` and dropped the H (H alone is a no-op there). Under load the gap between two Playwright key presses exceeds a render. The chord now reads a ref set in the same task as the G (`gotoRef`), with the state kept only for the "Go to…" hint. A test dispatches G and H in one task: it fails on the old handler and passes on the new one (`m9-flows.spec.ts`, "G then H in the same task"). This is a user-facing bug too (a fast typist or a key-repeat tool). A second race was in the test: `data-kind="block"` is true an instant before the lit hand is drawn, so G then H found no hand and focus fell to the decision heading; the spec now waits for the lit hand card (what a person sees first). 10 of 10 pass at 5 workers with 30 busy loops, 2 of 10 failed before. No retry or sleep was added.
- **Decision (guide links, review M9-R2):** `.meta-foot a` is an inline-block with `min-height: 24px` and block padding; every one of the 12 guides, the tiles and the grid now run through axe at both sizes (it failed on 7 guides before: `target-size`, 16 px Sources links).
- **Decision (e2e):** `m9-support.ts` holds the one axe helper (WCAG 2.2 AA + best practice) and writes every screenshot to `.cache/m9/<project>-<name>.png`; the three earlier M9 specs use it. `planted.ts` gains a `hindsight` game (E21 with their Kid in hand: Hindsight flag, certainty needed-hidden-info, Practice keeps the real hand and shows the label); the History count is 8 rows. The DON!! and mulligan planted games already existed from M8. A fresh-profile import uses `browser.newContext` with the project's viewport and base URL.
- **Decision (a11y script `m9`):** the 10-second warning is scripted on the counter drill at 50% (15 s an item): the mulligan's base time is 10 s, so its "10 seconds left" can never fire (the timer says "Over time" first). The `a11y-nvda` workflow gains `script=m9` (and `all` includes it).
- **Rejected:** retries or longer waits on the flaky spec (they hide a dropped key); a global key-queue in `Game.tsx` (more machinery than one ref).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 fix round: deck-out says RL-26, a counter's judge and text agree, one timed rule, the mulligan bank is committed

- **Context:** the independent review of M9 (`docs/reviews/m9-review.md`) failed it on three severity 3 findings and found nine more. Everything but M9-R2 (guide target size: WP8) is fixed here; the per-finding record, tests and fail-on-revert evidence are in that file's "Fix round".
- **Decision (R1, deck-out):** the demo starts with 1 card in the deck and ends the turn; the Draw Phase draws it, the deck is 0, and the loss is the one RL-26 describes (a rule process the moment the deck reaches 0, with 5 Life in play and the draw having worked). The test asserts the claim on the engine (deck 0, hand 1, Life 5, result) and that a deck of 2 survives the same line.
- **Decision (R3, counter verdict and text):** a take item's alternative counter is worded "Weaker: add X (10000 vs 8000), which stops it but spends 2 cards to save 1 Life", not "Also fine". Chosen over judging the counter acceptable (the lesson of the class is that cards are a resource, and the detector's `no-counter-on-attack` judge is also what a played review's Practice uses) and over dropping the alternative (the comparison is the teaching, UX-R27). The text and `examples.json` (E19) change with it; a test over the whole counter bank shows no choice the text accepts is one the judge rejects, and the reverse.
- **Decision (R4, one timed rule):** `DrillSession.timedOk` (additive, optional) is the number of right answers inside the limit that applied to each; `timedPasses` (`progress/exit.ts`) is the one rule, used by the summary and by Progress (`drillPassedAt(.., true)`). A session without `timedOk` (written before this, or untimed) stays valid and earns no timed badge: `factor <= 1` alone never did earn it honestly. The limit is recorded per answer (`AnswerResult.limit`), so an answer is measured against the factor it ran under. The time allowed is fixed once the first answer is given (the select is disabled with a spoken reason). Pause stays: time never ends an item, and a player who is interrupted should not lose the set; but an item with a pause has no limit and does not count towards the badge, and the timer line says so. Rejected: removing Pause (an interruption would end the timed attempt), capping paused time (a number with no basis).
- **Decision (R5, mulligan label and the Rocks rule):** the label is "From the Rocks guide (sourced baseline, inference)" when the guide's rule is tagged inference (`isInference`: its `source` says so), "(sourced)" only for a rule that is not (OP13-004 and OP15-058). The Rocks playbook now follows the guide's own Mulligan line (hands of only 6+ cost cards, or no 1 to 3 cost Character going first): `MulliganRule.redrawIfAllCostAtLeast` (additive) makes every other hand a keep, so going first an early play without the backup piece is a keep (the keepable-hand rule, which the guide says is inference, redrew it). The Pirate AI reads the same function, so its Rocks mulligan follows the same line. The review flag's label is unchanged ("(sourced)"; its e2e asserts it) and is left for WP8 or a later pass.
- **Decision (R6, empty-hand premise):** every counter answer and every DON!! answer gets the evidence line "They had no cards in hand, so nothing hidden adds to this. With cards in their hand, count what a Rush Character or an Event could add first." unless it says so already (the should-counter text does). The take-at-high-Life rule (`counter-wasted-b`, which includes `counter-20`) is worded "With nothing in their hand, ...". `counter-20` is kept, not dropped: with an empty hand the claim is true and now says so.
- **Decision (R7, the DON!! menu):** a fifth line, "play what can be played, then give the rest to an attacker" (`spend-give`), is offered when it differs from the lines already there (up to 5 lines; the hold Leader never gets it, as it never gets give-everything). Lines tied for the least unused DON!! are all best (`best` is the set; the answer says "Also tied for best"), so Choose-a-line and board mode read one set, and board mode's `unused <= min` accepts exactly it. An item is admitted when some line other than "hold everything" is worse than the best (before, "unique minimum"). Bank regenerated (22 items, 16 ordinary and 6 hold; the line that holds DON!! comes first in `best`, so E07 explains it).
- **Decision (R8, drill evidence):** learning-ui §6.1 says "2 points if the last drill session was below the exit". Read literally, one wrong answer scores 2 and outranks flags in every one of ten games (at most 1.61). The intent is a failing drill, so the weight is `2 * min(1, n / of)`: full for a session of the exit's ten or more items (the same size `sessionPasses` asks), proportional below, and the card says "under the 8 of 10" only for a session of that size. The constant stays 2 and is pinned by a test.
- **Decision (R11/R12, one list):** the mulligan bank is committed (`app/src/drills/banks/mulligan.json`, `npm run drills:bank -- mulligan [--check]`, generated on the pinned lists) and each item carries the 50-card list its hand came from (`MulliganItem.deck`), so a weekly meta PR changes neither the shipped hands nor their odds text. The lethal answer text is still solved at run time against the live prior; `ai/test/drills-live-lists.test.ts` loads the drills' modules a second time with the live `meta-lists.json` in place of the pinned copy and requires the same headline, header and answer for every committed lethal item and a mulligan bank of at least 20 hands in both classes, so a drift that matters fails CI with the instruction to refresh the pin and regenerate. `sanitizeSession` rejects a session whose lesson code is not its drill's (L09 DON!!, L10 counter, L12 mulligan, L15 lethal).
- **Found, not fixed (R10):** 15 seeded Rookie games gave 66 flags and 13 items: most counter and DON!! flags cannot be rebuilt as puzzles (a counter step carries pending state `scenarioOf` refuses; DON!! and target are practised in place), and no any-hand lethal or counter flag came out of a real game, so the harder-hand path is exercised on the bank's records only. To BACKLOG: a modifier-free cut at the Main before a counter, a planted E01 game test through `puzzleResponse` and `materialise`, and a line in the flag list saying why a flag cannot be practised as a puzzle.
- **Rejected:** committing only the mulligan hands and leaving the text on the live list (the odds in the answer would move under saved sessions); making the weekly meta PR fail unless the pin is refreshed whenever any list changes (the test fails only when a drill's answer would differ).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP7: Practice for DON!!, target and mulligan flags; the `puzzle` message answers with `puzzleFromFlag`; the ai barrel is lean

- **Context:** the M8 review's Practice only knew three judges. WP4 built the controller's `don-ledger` and `target-best`, WP2 the flag-to-puzzle function; nothing connected them, and the drill shell's shared ai chunk had grown by 67 KB (232.9 KB raw against 165.4 KB before WP4) because the `@optcg/ai` barrel re-exported all drill code.
- **Decision (detectors):** `don-unused` flags carry `don-ledger` with `unusedMin = min(best candidate line at the turn's first Main, n - 1)` (`candidateLines`, WP3): the flag says some of the `n` unused DON!! had a use, so a candidate list that cannot beat the game never makes "End turn at once" a solution. `target` flags carry `target-best` with `expect.target` = the better target's ENGINE INSTANCE id (`actionFromView(state, me, best).target`), not the view handle the spec named: the controller compares it with `a.target` of an engine action, and a handle never equals one (a test restores with a handle and shows the better target then fails). `mulligan` flags carry `{ judge: 'mulligan', turn: 1 }`.
- **Decision (ids in a rebuilt board):** a flag's record ids mean nothing in a scenario rebuilt by `createFromScenario`, so `puzzleFromFlag` maps `expect.target` through its position map into `BoardItem.judgeExpect` (additive; `expect` was taken by `CounterItem`), and the intake caches that, not the flag's. In-place Practice (the record restored as it was) keeps the flag's own ids.
- **Decision (the `puzzle` message):** the request carries `flag` and `gameId` too (additive): a stored review has the flag, a job does not, and the flag's `restoreIndex` and certainty cannot be read from its id. The job answers from the request's record and cards, else its own run's. The review worker loads the cards' scripts before it replays a record. `puzzleResponse` (run.ts) never throws. Found on the way: the cards a game's record names do not include the filler card (`PUZZLE_FILLER_CARD`, OP01-081) their hidden Life and deck are rebuilt from, so every item failed with "cannot be rebuilt" until `cardIdsOf` added it.
- **Decision (Practice routing, ReviewFlow):** `win-this-turn`, `survive-turn` and `no-counter-on-attack` Practice asks the worker for the item and restores it with the drills' `materialise` (the game's AI tier kept): an any-hand flag at a Main shows "Their hand is the strongest the guide allows: 3 cards, up to +2000 each." (counts only, `harderHandHeader`); a Hindsight flag or a cut the engine cannot describe (every counter step) keeps the real hand and shows "Their real hand: one way to see it", also on the drill answer screen; if no item can be built the record is restored as before, with that label. `don-ledger` and `target-best` restore the record itself. A mulligan flag opens `/drills/mulligan/run?flag=…` (one item, no board). A drill answer on a flag item writes "Practised: solved / not yet" back to the stored review. `practiceOf` falls back by kind for a stored review without `practice`: DON!! (bar: none unused, since the count was never stored), mulligan; a target flag says "Analyse again to practise this." `DRILLS_COPY` is gone.
- **Decision (one exit rule):** the drill summary reads WP6's table (`progress/exit.ts`) through `ruleOf`, and "passed" is `sessionPasses` on the session it records (n = answers given), so it cannot disagree with Progress's credit; a test compares them for every drill at 8/10, 7/10, 16/20, 5/5 and more. The summary's timed badge still needs each answer within the time limit, which Progress cannot know (`DrillSession` stores no ms): Progress's "passed (timed)" credits any session at 100% or faster. Left as is; WP6 can record `within`.
- **Decision (bundle):** `@optcg/ai` (the package's `.`) is now `ai/src/app.ts`, the lean barrel with only the drills' types and no review runner; `@optcg/ai/drills` (banks, judges, seams) and `@optcg/ai/review` (`ReviewJob`, the registry, `reviewRecord`) are subpaths, imported by the drill pages' own dynamic import and by the review worker. `ai/src/index.ts` is all three, for the AI's own tests and tools. eslint allows the two subpaths as it allows `@optcg/ai/worker`. Shared ai chunk: 165.4 KB (30.9 gz) before WP4, 232.9 (52.5) after, 196.0 (40.9) now; Game 224.9 → 225.8, start-up 275.0 → 275.3 KB raw. The 30 KB that is still extra is the lethal solver (`bound`, `search`, `lethal`, `analyse`): two lazy chunks (the coach's blunder check and the drills) now share it, and the bundler hoists a module shared by two chunks into the ai chunk the Game route loads. Splitting it with `manualChunks` pulled the engine into one 330 KB chunk, so it was not done.
- **Rejected:** unusedMin straight from the candidate lines (it can exceed what the player left); asking the worker for Practice of every flag (DON!! and target need no rebuilt board, and in place their ids are the record's); bumping `ANALYSIS_VERSION` to re-run old reviews (the fallback by kind does what the spec asks without throwing away stored reviews).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP4: the drill shell rides in the board's decision panel; banks load per drill; the engine work stays in ai

- **Context:** four drills share one shell. Board items must be the real `Game`; full-state engine calls may not appear under `app/src` (eslint); the bank JSON (about 300 KB raw) must stay out of the start-up and Game chunks.
- **Decision (board items):** `materialise` restores a `GameController` from the item (`restore.puzzle`), the record built by `recordOfItem` in `ai/src/search/drills/item-seam.ts` (the `cutAtCounter` pattern; the item's `seat` is the human). The drill bar (item count, Show answer, Skip, Leave, timer, "No lethal") is passed to `Game` as a new `drillSlot`, which fills the decision panel's existing slot; `PuzzleConfig.submitLabel` renames End turn to "Submit turn". Two small additive edits outside WP4's file list (`Game.tsx`, `Decision.tsx`) carry this. The answer is a separate screen; the board is disposed when the judge decides.
- **Decision (judges):** `don-ledger` reads the player's own `donLedger` once, right after the End turn that ends the puzzle turn (the AI has not moved); solved when `unused <= expect.unusedMin`, and a bank item's bar is the least `unused` of its lines. `target-best` compares the first attack's target with `expect.target` (an instance id; for visible cards that is what an action holds, a flag's handle must be mapped by WP7). An end-of-turn effect order the player owes after Submit turn keeps the item open until answered.
- **Decision (bundle):** the app never uses `bankFor`: `banks.ts` imports each JSON with its own dynamic import and the mulligan bank is built from `mulliganBank(metaListSets(), cards)`; using `bankFor` put all three files into the ai chunk the Game loads (361 KB vs 232 KB raw).
- **Decision (flag items):** `flagBank` asks the review worker's `puzzle` message once per flag and caches the item, judge and the flag's own explanation under `drills.flagItems`; a flag with no puzzle is not cached (WP7 wires the worker). Mulligan flags are rebuilt in `mulliganItemFromFlag` (the player's own hand). An item cut from a game answers with its flag's explanation, has no "No lethal" button, and the lethal header is solved in `public` mode for the item's seat.
- **Decision (exit and timer):** the summary scales the lesson's exit to the set (ceil of 8/10, 7/10 for DON!!) and passes only a set of 10 or more; the timed badge needs a factor of 100% or less. Base seconds: 10, 60, 30, 45 (curriculum). K and R answer only while focus is on the two buttons (WCAG 2.1.4); N declares "No lethal" like the board's own single keys.
- **Cut:** the Settings → Drills timer row (not WP4's file), per-archetype two-archetype L12 counting beyond recording `archetype` (WP6 counts it).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP3: counter and DON!! banks come from the M8 detectors' own code, positions are seeded and tightened to the edge, the mulligan bank is built at call time

- **Context:** WP3 builds three drills (mulligan, counter-or-take, DON!! allocation) whose items must be `any-hand`, deterministic and re-verified. The M8 detectors read a *record* of a game; a bank item is a *position*. WP2's `scenarioOf`/`boards.ts` were not available, so the positions are built directly as seeded `ScenarioSpec`s.
- **Decision (classes come from the detectors):** the counter classifiers are the M8 code, extracted without changing behaviour: `counterWastedOf(s0, sC, played, me, cards)` (the body of the counter-wasted detector, cases a and b), `shouldCounterExplanation(...)` and `refutationCertainty` (should-counter), `explainMulligan(...)` (mulligan), `takeTheHit` exported. The M8 review tests and snapshots are unchanged. *should-counter* is admitted when `lethal` (`actual`) refutes their Main, the refutation holds with their deck unknown and their hand empty (the detector's own `any-hand` rule), taking the hit hands them a proven win, and some counters (searched breadth first over the sets in hand) keep the next Main refuted. *counter-wasted* is the detector run on the naive play (biggest counters first until the hit stops); only `any-hand` cases are admitted (case a always; case b by the public bound).
- **Decision (positions):** the attacker's hand is empty and every Life card is `INERT_LIFE` (OP01-081, a vanilla Character), so nothing hidden bears on any item. At one or two Life the defender's hand is cut to the cards the defence needs (`tightened`: each card dropped while the turn is still survivable): random positions almost never sit on the edge where a taken hit loses, and without this 150 seeds gave no should-counter item. A counter that opens a decision of its own (a cost to pay, OP04-016) is skipped by the naive play, because the detector's one-card-per-counter text would be wrong for it.
- **Decision (DON!! lines):** `candidateLines` offers spend (dearest plays), two cheaper plays, give all DON!! to the best attacker and attack, play one card and give 1 DON!! to the Leader before its attack, and hold. The opponent answers attacks plainly (no Block, no Counter) and the actions are in `lines[].actions`, so a line replays with `reduce` alone; after `endTurn` the player's own [End of Your Turn] "you may" is taken (E07's Usopp) so the ledger is the one the review reads. An item needs a unique minimum of unused DON!!; "give everything away" is the best line in most positions, so at most half the ordinary items have it as their best, and the OP13-001 hold items do not offer it (E07's lesson is that held is not idle).
- **Decision (mulligan bank):** built at call time from `meta-lists.json` and the card data (`mulliganBank`); nothing committed. `bank-mulligan.ts` is empty until the app calls `provideMulliganCards(cards)` (the app loads cards lazily, `ai/` cannot). 34 items from the 9 sourced guides (OP17-039 going second has no Redraw hand in 400 seeds: the guide keeps nearly everything going second).
- **Decision (bank size):** DON!! holds 22 (16 ordinary, 6 hold), not the plan's 18, to meet the 20-item criterion. Banks record `builtWith.scripts` = `DRILL_SCRIPTS_VERSION` (`wp3-1`); bump it whenever a generator changes what a seed builds. Counter and DON!! register in `scripts/drill-bank.ts` (`npm run drills:bank -- counter|don [--check]`, on the pinned lists); `scripts/drill-bank-mulligan.ts` only reports the call-time mulligan bank. Counter bank: 24 (12 should-counter, 6 counter-wasted a, 6 b), found in 88 seeds.
- **Rejected:** committing explanation text in the banks (it would go stale with the detectors; `explainCounter`/`explainDon`/`explainMulliganItem` rebuild it from the item); a hand-picked list of positions (a seed is reproducible and auditable); relaxing the any-hand rule to reach a class count (the counts below are what the rule admits).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP6: progress is sessions and marks; status, win rates and the recommendation are derived; import merges

- **Context:** WP6 builds `progress.v1`, the exit rules, the recommendation, `/progress`, `/learn` and the lesson page.
- **Decision (store):** `progressStore(kv)` writes through the page's synchronous `progress` cache when there is one (journalled, so a reload after a drill cannot lose a session), else the store's KvStore. Writes are serialised in one queue. `recordSession` stores the session and, when the lesson's exit is now met, the first `passedAt` (and `passedTimedAt` for sessions with `factor <= 1`); a worse later session never undoes it. Status is also derived from the sessions, so a pass survives the 200-session cap. WP4 calls `progressSink()` (`ProgressSink`).
- **Decision (exit):** a session meets "N of M" when it has at least M items and the same share correct (16 of 20 passes 8 of 10; a 5-item warm-up cannot). L12 needs two archetypes each passing. Only the drill clause of L09/L10 is measured; the "no flag in 2 of 3 games" clause is not (flags are Progress evidence, not an exit gate in M9). L08 is Rookie games, not Rewound, From review or Tutorial.
- **Decision (import):** `importWithProgress` (one-line change in `DataSection`) merges `progress.v1` with `mergeProgress` before calling the store's `importAll`, which would otherwise overwrite the key. `sanitizeProgress` leaves out a bad session and says so; the wrong envelope or over 2000 sessions refuses the key, not the file.
- **Decision (recommendation):** "reviewed" is a complete review. Concepts score per slug and one suggestion is kept per lesson. "N flags in your last M games": M runs back to the oldest game in the window with a flag of that concept. A lethal-tier line is supporting only.
- **Cut / not built:** estimated lesson times (no source for them); "hints used" (not recorded anywhere); the lethal "found alone" rate (a lethal found with no coach use leaves no log entry, so the page reports tiers needed and says the rate is not recorded); glossary links only on the first six terms.
- **Additive outside WP6 files:** `ExplanationView` gained optional `evidenceLabel` (two explanations on one page made two landmarks with one name, an axe failure).
- **Rejected:** copying games or flags into progress; replacing on import.

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP1: guides are parsed in the browser from the content files; two sources "disagree" when their records are not each other's reverse

- **Context:** the Meta Explorer (tiles, guide, 12 × 12 grid) reads the 12 guide files, `sources.md` and `meta-lists.json`. Three things in the content did not match the spec's picture: two guides' front-matter `archetypeId` differs from the id `meta-lists.json` and `DECKS` use (`OP09-062-robin` against `OP09-062-nico-robin`, `OP05-098-enel` against `OP05-098-enel-yellow`); the spec's disagreement example (Rocks 16-20 against Kaido's row) is one source read from both sides (Kaido's row has 20-16, the exact reverse), so it is not a disagreement; and some guide cells hold several records ("38-63 (bad) [SH-SAB]; 10-8 post-ban [SH-MUC]").
- **Decision:** (1) Every link built from a guide (Play this deck, Play against, Play this matchup, the mulligan trainer) uses the archetype id from `meta-lists.json` through `archetypeIdOf(leaderId)`, never the guide's front matter; the content files are not edited here (tracked for the content owner). (2) A pairing is `disagree` only when both guides give a record and the first is not the exact reverse of the second: Rocks 11-12 against Robin and Robin 35-39 against Rocks disagree, Rocks 16-20 and Kaido 20-16 do not. The popover then lists both guides' records, each with its owner ("Rocks W–L") and tags; the grid shows "≠" beside the marker. (3) `record` is the first W-L pair in the cell; the whole cell, tags removed, is `recordCell` and shown in the popover, so a second sourced record is read, not dropped. (4) The parser is pure and also exported for tests; the guides load through `import.meta.glob(?raw)` in the lazy meta chunk, and the grid/tile data and header come from `meta-lists.json` (events count parsed from `countingMethod`, so a refreshed file changes the header with no code change). (5) The tile's automation count is by copies of the first list's 50 cards: a card plays fully if its script exists or the card data says it needs none; until the cards load the line reads "Checking card automation…" rather than a guess. (6) Contract additions (additive): `GuideBlock` gains a `table` kind (Game plan, Defining cards), `Span` inference gains `note`, `GuideMatchup` gains `recordCell` and `recordSpans`, `GuideMatchup.owner` is the guide's leader id.
- **Rejected:** a build step that writes JSON (a second source of truth, decided in the M9 plan); using the guide's `archetypeId` in links (two of twelve would load no deck); treating any two records as a disagreement (25 pairings have a record in both guides and most re-report one source from the other side; only 4 of them, 8 cells, are different counts); a percentage or colour scale on cells (no data for it).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP2: the lethal bank, the harder hand and `scenarioOf`

- **Context:** WP2 builds the lethal drill's bank, the opponent hand a flag-derived puzzle may show, and the engine function that turns a played state into a scenario. Three facts of the code shaped it: the solver gives a `bound` refutation the certainty `needed-hidden-info` (it assumes their worst hand, so a weak real hand might lose to it), the tests run on the pinned meta lists (`PINNED_META`) while `tsx` reads the live file, and a plan that wins "against any answer" keeps attacking after a Counter, so its action list is longer than the line a play-through shows.
- **Decision (none items):** a "provably none" item is admitted only with reason `attackers` (too few attackers could ever land the hits: true for every hand). The spec also named `bound`, but that refutation is not any-hand in the solver, so no item uses it; a search refutation and `unknown` are discarded too. The any-hand rule was not relaxed to reach a count.
- **Decision (lethal items):** `proven`, `any-hand`, `oblivious` (one fixed plan wins whatever they answer), then a second check: `lethal actual` on the full state with the harder hand must prove it too (the dominance check; an earlier run dropped one candidate whose search hit the cap). Adaptive proofs (a plan that depends on their answers) are left out: the answer text is a list of moves.
- **Decision (bank is generated on the pinned lists):** `scripts/drill-bank.ts` installs a module resolve hook that points the AI's prior at `ai/test/fixtures/meta-lists.pinned.json` (the file CI reads) before importing anything, and refuses to write if the prior does not match the pinned file. The committed bank is therefore a function of the pinned lists, this engine and these scripts; a weekly meta PR cannot break it. Regenerating after `PINNED_META` is refreshed is a deliberate step (`npm run drills:bank -- lethal`).
- **Decision (generator):** seeds 1, 2, 3 ...; odd seeds a "strike" board (Life 1 or 2, two or three attackers more than Life, their hand the size of their Life), even seeds a "short" board (one attacker fewer than the hits needed). Boards come from the archetypes' own sourced lists (the public prior); the opponent's hand is `harderHand`. At most 6 items against one opponent Leader (the pair walks all twelve Leaders). Caps are 40,000 nodes in `public` and 200,000 in the `actual` dominance check, never a clock. The run that wrote the committed bank tried 204 seeds for 14 + 14.
- **Honest numbers:** lethal items cluster on the opponents whose public prior the solver can prove against (Rocks and Enel OP05-098 produce most; Kaido's and Boa's priors hit the node cap or a model gap, so they give none). The none items cover most opponents.
- **Decision (`harderHand`):** the `n` cards of the archetype's sourced lists with the highest `defenceValue` (printed Counter, or an Event's +power, plus 500 for an Event that also rests or draws), each at most as often as a list runs it, ties by card id. It is argmax by brute force on a toy list in the test. `INERT_LIFE` is OP01-081 (vanilla, no [Trigger], no script; it has a +1000 Counter, which the public model's worst-case card covers).
- **Decision (`scenarioOf`):** a pure addition to `engine/` (no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump). Expressible only at the active player's `main` decision with a single `turn/main` frame, no battle, resolving card, pending or delayed effect, no modifier of any kind, no Once Per Turn mark and no known hidden card; each is a closed `NotExpressible` reason. `over` swaps a seat's hand, Life (bare id = face down) and deck. A card's `played` turn becomes `playedThisTurn`; earlier plays are the same as no play for the rules.
- **Decision (`puzzleFromFlag`):** an `any-hand` flag at an expressible cut gives the opponent `harderHand`, `INERT_LIFE` face-down Life and `INERT_LIFE` deck cards (only the counts are public); the item is the same for any permutation of their hidden cards across hand, deck and Life. Hindsight flags and cuts the engine cannot describe (a counter step) take the last describable Main before the cut and a `prefix` of the record's actions remapped by position; `hand: 'real'`, label `hindsight`. Mulligan flags are not boards and return a reason. `BoardItem` gained an optional `seat` (absent = `p1`) because a played game's human may be `p2`.
- **Rejected:** admitting `bound` refutations as "none" (not any-hand); generating on the live meta file (CI would re-verify with other lists); a fixed `p1` seat for flag items (the human often sits second).

## 2026-11-26 — M9 WP5: the rules reference reads the two research files at runtime in a lazy chunk, and every demo is replayed on the engine

- **Context:** WP5 builds `/rules`, `/rules/<id>`, "Show me" and the tutorial citation link from `rules-digest.md` (137 items) and `rulings.md`.
- **Decision:** `data.ts` imports the three Markdown files as `?raw` and `loadRules()` imports it dynamically, so ~100 KB of text is a lazy chunk. Counts are asserted (137 digest items; **50 rulings**, not 52: RL-38 and RL-39 do not exist in the file). Digest item 73 is only an `[engine note]`, so it parses to empty text and is not listed (186 entries are shown). `[engine note]` clauses are cut to the end of their line. Route ids: `item/N`, `RL-NN`, `cr/x-y-z`; a CR number lists the entries citing it, falling back to what is inside it or to its parent rule. Search is a hand-written token index; the box is a real `combobox` (listbox, `aria-activedescendant`, Enter opens, Enter on a rule number jumps).
- **Decision:** 24 demos, all kept; each is data (scenario, `line` of `ActionPattern`s, `expect`, optional `before`) and `app/test/rules-demos.test.ts` replays every one with `reduce`, mirroring the board (the player's forced windows auto-applied except a damage decision; each step must match exactly one legal action; the scripted opponent must choose what its steps say), checks `expect`, checks the board filter `allowFor` allows exactly the replayed action on each of the player's steps (and narrows the engine's list on step 1), and mutation-tests a changed number, an impossible step and an ambiguous step. `rules-showme.test.ts` plays all 24 through the real `GameController`. Additive contract changes: `ActionPattern.option`/`.don`, `DemoExpect` kind `legal` (with `afterStep`), `DemoZone` `donDeck`, `RuleDemo.before`, `RuleEntry.question`/`.confidenceNote`. Demo links use the demo id (`/rules/<demo-id>/show?back=<entry id>`) because several demos share a ruling.
- **Rejected:** no automatic "read" credit and no Mark-as-read button (the progress store is WP6's; the done card links the lesson); the second player's turn-2 attack demo (the rules forbid it: RL-28, first battle is turn 3, so the demo shows that instead).

- **Context:** WP1, WP2, WP3 and WP5 build in parallel from the WP0 contracts; the spec gave `bankFor` one home (`ai/src/search/drills/index.ts`) that WP2 and WP3 would both have to edit, and every new route needs a page.
- **Decision:** `bankFor` dispatches to one file per drill (`bank-lethal.ts` for WP2; `bank-mulligan.ts`, `bank-counter.ts`, `bank-don.ts` for WP3), each an empty-bank stub, so `index.ts` never changes again.
