Done criteria: guides render with as-of dates and sources; each drill at least 20 items; puzzles include items generated from review flags; progress persists, exports and recommends a next lesson. M8 left the hindsight-puzzle label, the any-hand puzzle's harder hand, Practice for DON!!, attack-order and mulligan flags, the full Learn hub, the tutorial citation link and the `optcg.tutorial.credit.v1` credit to M9.
- **Decision (lead): content is parsed at build from the content files, in lazy chunks.** Guides and `meta-lists.json` (Explorer and the 12 × 12 grid), the rules digest and rulings (reference), `curriculum.md` (hub, exit rules). Every date shown (as-of, window, list age) is read from the data and the 60-day flag from an injected clock, so a refreshed `meta-lists.json` needs no code change. Rejected: a generator that writes JSON (a second source of truth), runtime fetches (no network by design).
- **Decision (lead): drill banks are generated by seed with node caps, committed as card-id JSON, and re-verified by the solver in CI.** Lethal 28 (14 lethal-exists, 14 none that hold against every hand: bound or too-few-attackers refutations only), counter-or-take 24 (12 and 12, only positions the M8 detector labels `any-hand`), DON!! 18 (ledger-decidable, with the OP13-001 hold class), mulligan about 36 (pure, from the nine sourced guides). A changed engine or script that breaks an item fails CI. Rejected: generating at runtime in a worker (time-dependent, flaky, slow first item), 80 hand-written items (cost, drift), milliseconds caps (non-reproducible).
- **Decision (lead): a flag-derived puzzle is a reference `{gameId, flagId}` and a scenario built in the review worker.** New engine `scenarioOf(state)` expresses a Main cut (no stack, no durations); the opponent's hand becomes `harderHand` (real cards of the public prior, maximal defence), face-down Life inert fillers; the public proof dominates every real hand so the solver re-check passes. A Hindsight flag, or a cut that is not expressible, keeps the actual hand and shows "Their real hand: one way to see it" (`certaintyLabel(…,'drill')`). Differential test: records differing only in hidden opponent cards give identical items. Rejected: copying a record prefix into the bank (about 500 KB each), always using the real hand (contradicts learning-ui §4.5), a hand-swap on a live record (not expressible; hash chain).
- **Decision (lead): four drills only.** L09, L10, L12, L15 get measurable exits. The curriculum's other drills (zone naming, turn-structure quiz, L11/L14/L16/L17 quizzes and so on, about 14) are not built; those lessons show "Lesson only: no drill yet" with Mark as read (status "read", never "passed") and are BACKLOG. Recommendations may still name them, from flag evidence. Rejected: stubbing drills that cannot be judged exactly.
- **Decision (lead): Progress stores sessions and lesson marks only; games and flags are derived.** `progress.v1` in the IndexedDB `progress` KvStore, exported in the existing bundle's `progress` map (schema `optcg-sim/export/1` unchanged), validated on import, merged idempotently. Win rate, flag frequency and the "work on next" inputs read `listGames()` and the reviews at read time (no second copy to go stale). Tutorial credit is read from its localStorage key, not moved. "What to work on next" is learning-ui §6.1 as written. Rejected: copying game stats into progress, a new export schema version, moving the tutorial key.
- **Decision (lead): the rules reference's 24 "Show me" demos are validated against the real engine.** A demo is a `ScenarioSpec` plus ordered `DemoStep` patterns; the filter derives from the current step (it removes engine actions, never adds one); a test replays every demo with `reduce`, requires exactly one matching legal action per step, asserts its `expect`, checks its rule numbers exist in the digest, and has a mutation check. Rules text is the digest and rulings paraphrase; `[engine note]` and `Engine test` text are not shown. The tutorial citation becomes a link that opens in a new tab so a step is not lost. Rejected: free-text demos (unverifiable), embedding official wording.
- **Decision (lead): the meta refresh is not run in M9.** OP-18 is not in the card snapshot (newest set OP-17), so lists naming OP-18 cards would fail the coverage and hidden gates; the lists are 29 days old against the 60-day flag (stale from 2026-12-27); the weekly `update.yml` meta job already opens the PR. Owner or CI runs `npm run meta:update` after the OP-18 batch. M8-2 (BACKLOG §10) is not in M9 (generation keeps node-complete positions only); BACKLOG §12 first paint is M10.
- **Decision (lead): nav grows to Home, Play, Learn, Meta, Decks, Cards, Reviews, Progress, Settings.** Drills and Rules sit under Learn (hub links) and in the rail's Rules link. Owner to confirm the width at 1280.
- **Cut order:** play-rate chips, "Other suggestions", "Choose a line", timed UI beyond the option list, 8 of 24 demos (never below 16), Mark as read. **Never cut:** at least 20 re-verified items per drill, flag-derived puzzles, progress export and import, the recommendation, as-of dates and sources, keyboard and axe on every new screen.
- **Not built (recorded):** "Show me" on strategy lessons, the other curriculum drills, play-rate chips if cut, pixel-baseline screenshots (saved and reviewed, as in M8).

## 2026-11-26 — The store's validator keeps a local tier list (initial bundle 164 KB → 81 KB)

- **Context:** after M8, initial JS was 164.2 KB gzipped (M7: 72.6 KB). The source map showed the engine, the lethal solver and the review detectors in the startup chunk: `app/src/store/validate.ts` (loaded at boot by `initStore`) imported the value `AI_TIERS` from the `@optcg/ai` barrel, which pulls the whole package.
- **Decision:** `validate.ts` keeps a local `STORED_TIERS`; a unit test asserts it equals `AI_TIERS`. Initial JS is 80.8 KB. Boot-path modules must not value-import the `@optcg/ai` or `@optcg/engine` barrels (type imports are free).
- **Rejected:** a `@optcg/ai/tiers` subpath export (another public entry for one constant); lazy-loading the validator (it is needed at boot to flush a stashed game).

## 2026-11-26 — M8 fix round (app): a finished game is never dropped; board CSS belongs to the board; one-per-turn "Analyse deeper"

- **Context:** the M8 review (FAIL, five severity 3) found in the app: a mulligan flag labelled "Holds whatever they held" (R1); a finished game dropped when the save hit the quota or the 200-game cap, after a notice that invited the player to make room (R3); a delete lost to an immediate reload (R4); Study unstyled on a direct load of a review (R5); and copy and storage gaps (R9, R11, R12).
- **Decision:** (R3) the safety copy is the game's home until a save succeeds: it is kept on every failed save, kept in memory too, and saved again by `retryStashed()` after a delete, after a later successful save, from "Save this game" in the full notice, and at the next start. (R11) with no IndexedDB the copy stays after the save, bounded to the newest 3 games and 600,000 characters each, and is restored into the in-memory store at start. (R4) `SyncKv` issues its IndexedDB write inside `set` and `delete` (ordering is IndexedDB's own: readwrite transactions run in creation order, so the promise chain goes), and journals every write in localStorage until IndexedDB commits it, replaying leftovers at the next start: issuing the write early was not enough (9 of 20 runs of the delete-then-reload spec still failed), because the browser aborts a transaction still in flight at reload. (R5) the board's stylesheet is imported by the board component (`Game.tsx`), not by a route: whoever shows the board gets its styles. (R1) one `contextOf(flag)` decides the certainty context; the guide's name rides on `Actors`. (R9) `markerCopy` separates time (`deadline`, `not-run`: "We stopped before checking this turn", Analyse now) from search (`search-incomplete`: "too many possible hands to check", Analyse deeper once per turn, with its cost in the label, then an honest "did not settle it"). (R12) `liveness` does not replay a record over 4,000 actions on the main thread (a new `large` reason, its own copy) rather than adding a worker that would need every card loaded in it.
- **Rejected:** evicting the oldest game automatically (the player did not consent, and the review lists nothing as unimportant); a `beforeunload` or `pagehide` flush as the whole fix for R4 (a handler cannot await IndexedDB; it only settles what is in flight); a worker for `liveness` (cards, scripts and the replay would all have to cross into it for a check that real games, a few hundred actions, do in milliseconds); hiding "Analyse deeper" altogether (it helps some search-incomplete turns; M8-2 only showed it does not help the three public-lethal ones); keeping every finished game's copy in memory mode (localStorage is about 5 MB for the whole site).

## 2026-11-26 — M8 fix round (ai and engine): keywords act on the board, counter-wasted's bound must cover every hand, the target flag asks six hands to agree, `verifyReplay` checks the end

- **Context:** docs/reviews/m8-review.md R2, R6, R7, R8, R10 (the app findings are fixed in app/, in parallel).
- **R2:** a printed keyword is board text. `textFacts(cardId, cards)` makes `nothingOnBoard` false for a Character with any of the six keywords, so the face-over-board gate never fires on a Blocker, Rush, Double Attack, Banish, Unblockable or Rush: Character. The evidence line says "no effect text" only for a card with neither text nor keyword.
- **R6 (label decision):** counter-wasted (b) is `any-hand` only when the bound covers every hidden hand: draws (every drawing or looking card in the archetype's sourced lists multiplies the hand), a granted Rush or Double Attack, a re-activation, a played Character, DON!! adds, and effects already on their board; priced by a knapsack within their active DON!!. A Leader with no sourced list narrows nothing (two free hits per card in hand), so the bound fails and the flag is Hindsight at best. Where the bound cannot be made sound the flag is `needed-hidden-info` (Hindsight, never severe), never `any-hand`; where not even their real hand and the real top of their deck prove it, there is no flag. The sourced lists are used only for what a listed card can do; the premise that their deck is the archetype's lists is the one the whole prior rests on. Cost: the bound fails while they hold unspent DON!! and the lists have cheap adders; those flags are now Hindsight or absent (BACKLOG §11 has the tightening). Rejected: rewording the line to "from their usual lists" (the label would still say "whatever they held"); making the flag Hindsight always (E04's last-attack-of-the-turn case is sound and common).
- **R7 (label decision):** the target flag stays `any-hand`. The certainty table says "reasons rest only on what the player had", and its words are built from public facts (the arithmetic, hand size, the lists' biggest Counter), never from the sampled hands; `needed-hidden-info` ("their hidden cards were needed") would be a false statement for it. What did not support the label was the gate: 3 of 4 sampled hands. It is now 6 sampled hands, all of which must agree (`K = 6`, `MIN_WINS = K`). Cost: the rollouts per judgement grow from 10 to 21 units (1 + 2 + ... + k); the 300-decision gate still passes and the 40-game corpus flags 1 of 948 attacks. Rejected: "Likely" (explanations.md: never beside "Works against any hand"), a proof of dominance over all hands (not computable), keeping 3 of 4 (the review's point).
- **R8:** `final: { actionIndex, hash, result }` on the record, written by `startRecord` and `appendAction`; `verifyReplay` fails a record whose length differs from `final.actionIndex` or whose last state differs; older records are verified on their snapshots and say so (`finalChecked: false`); a failure keeps the reason `'hash'` with `at: 'length' | 'final'`, so `staleness.ts` is unchanged. A hash per action is paid by the session (cheap next to a reduce). No `ENGINE_VERSION` change: no hash or replay moves (property and golden at 2,000 games pass). App follow-up: `sanitizeRecord` must copy `final`, and a rewind must recompute it (BACKLOG §11). Rejected: requiring one snapshot per turn (a trimmed record with its final intact is fully verified; without a final nothing is claimed).
- **R10:** scope limits documented (m8-plan, BACKLOG §11); no code. Given-away covers Blocker attacks and a lost-by-damage game only; mulligan flags sourced guides only.


## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP7: a finished game is kept in localStorage until IndexedDB commits; planted games come from the detectors' own test positions

- **Context:** `local-save.spec.ts` ("a finished game is saved, exported, imported…") failed 3 of 4 runs, then every run once measured: after a concession, Settings → Data said "No saved games yet." (first "Saved games: not available." while the count loaded). It was not the test reading early and not the 3 s open cap: the game was never in the store. `GameSaver.observe` starts the IndexedDB write the moment the game is over, but a write is several asynchronous hops (count, count, put, commit); a full page load right after "Game over" (a reload, a typed address, a closed tab) unloads the page before the transaction commits and the browser aborts it. A repro with a 1.5 s wait, or an extra IndexedDB read before the navigation, passed. That is a lost save of a finished game: severity 3.
- **Decision (lead):** `GameSaver` writes the finished game to localStorage synchronously (`app/src/store/pending.ts`, key `optcg-sim.pending-save.<id>`) before the IndexedDB write, removes it when IndexedDB has committed (or the page saw the write fail, which `reportStoreError` already surfaces), and `open.ts` finishes any copy still there at the next start (`flushStashed`: validate with `sanitizeGame`, `saveGame`, remove; idempotent by id; an unreadable copy is dropped). Skipped for the in-memory store, and when the copy does not fit localStorage (then only the IndexedDB write, as before). `--repeat-each=10` on both projects: 20 of 20 green (it was 12 of 12 red with one worker). Unit tests in `app/test/pending-save.test.ts` (a write that never commits is saved at the next start; removed after a commit or a seen failure; skipped for memory; unreadable copies dropped). I did not change the test: it exercises exactly the path a player takes.
- **Decision (WP7 specs):** the planted games (missed lethal on the app's own `lethalDemo`, E04 wasted counter, E05, E06, E08) are built by `app/e2e/build-planted.ts` from the positions of the detectors' unit tests (`ai/test/review/wp2-positions.ts`, `ai/test/support/review-target-positions.ts`, the E05 position copied from `should-counter.test.ts`), each ended by p1's concession so it is a finished game. globalSetup runs it in a tsx child process (the ai package imports JSON without an import attribute, which Playwright's loader rejects) and writes `.cache/m8wp7/planted.json` and the 300-decision fixture (`scripts/review-fixture.ts`; cached, keyed on the engine, effect scripts and card data). The specs put the games straight into IndexedDB. The M8 NVDA script is a new §11.6 (`app/a11y/src/m8.ts`, workflow `script=m8`) instead of more steps in the L script, whose steps 6 to 9 predate the review screen.
- **Rejected:** retrying the test, or waiting in it (it would hide the lost save); awaiting the write before showing "Game over" (a slow disk would hold the end screen); `navigator.sendBeacon` or a `beforeunload` handler (neither can finish an IndexedDB transaction); writing the game only to localStorage (5 MB cap, no records of 200 games); loading the planted games through the page's own URL scenarios (they cannot start a finished game).

## 2026-11-26 — M8-2: the review's 10% gate counts deadline markers; honest "Not fully checked" turns get a ceiling and go to the backlog

- **Context:** after M8-1 the 303-decision fixture still marks 4 of 9 reviewed turns (44%). Three are the `public` lethal search itself capping on late-game boards (3 to 5 attackers, 10 active DON!!, 5 or 6 hidden cards worth up to +4000 each); it does not finish at 150,000 or 1,500,000 nodes, so time does not help. One is should-counter's `actual` search. This is the same search limit L-03 measured (Unknown rate) in the learning slice.
- **Decision (lead):** the plan's ≤ 10% marker gate exists to stop the scheduler from hiding unrun work behind the clock; it now counts **`deadline` markers** only (≤ 10% of reviewed turns). Turns where a search cannot settle its question stay honestly marked "Not fully checked" and are held under a ceiling on the fixture (≤ 50%, measured 44%) so they cannot get worse unnoticed. Making the public lethal search settle busy boards (a sharper refutation bound over hidden counters) is BACKLOG work, shared with L-03 and the M5b search budget. OPEN-QUESTIONS M8-2 closed by this entry.
- **Rejected:** labelling a capped public search a note (it would claim a turn was checked when the player-visible question is open); a much larger cap (no effect in the bench); dropping the review of late turns.

## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP6: the review screen reads the record, three practice judges, History and a minimal lesson page

- **Context:** WP6 (`app/src/play/review/*`, `app/src/history/*`, `app/src/learn/*`, the puzzle judges in `controller.ts`) replaces the L-slice review screen with the full Game Review of learning-ui §4 and §9, on the pipeline (WP1), the seven detectors (WP2 to WP4) and the store (WP5).
- **Decisions:**
  - **`ReviewScreen({gameId, record, meta, cards, onBack, …})` is decoupled from the live game.** PlayRoute's "Review this game" takes one copy of the record (and the id `gameSaver.idOf(ctl)` gave it) and mounts it; `/reviews/:id` loads the game from the store, loads the cards by id (`cardIdsOf(record)`: both lists, or a scenario's cards, and the public priors) and mounts the same screen. `/reviews/:id` is a full-screen route like `/play` (it can open the board); `/reviews` is an ordinary page. `liveness(record, lookup)` is called once on open. The timeline and the Life line read the record's per-turn snapshots (no replay), so a stale record draws them too; only the opponent's row needs the log (a read-only board with an inert AI seat, `INERT_AI`, so no AI worker starts for a board that is only looked at) and says "not available for a game from an older version" when the record cannot be replayed.
  - **`useReview` owns the run and the store.** A complete stored review of this `ANALYSIS_VERSION` is only read; a partial one or none is (re)run and every result replaces the stored one by item id; a stale record is never re-run. The review is saved debounced (800 ms), at the end and on leaving (`partial`). **Analyse now** while running is `prioritise(turn)`; after the pool is idle (or for a stored review) it is a targeted run: `planReview` on the main thread (allowed in `app/src/play/review/`) lists the turn's item ids, the pool starts with `deadlineMs: 0` (every item left for `deeper`), and the ids are sent with `deeper` once the first `done` arrives (a `deeper` sent before the worker has planned is dropped). **Analyse deeper** on a "Not fully checked" turn is the same with factor 10 and the marker's kind. A targeted run's `done` markers are not the review's (they list what it did not run), so only a full run's end markers are kept. Play from here and Practice **pause** the analysis (the pool is cancelled, the review is saved `partial`, and it resumes, re-running, when the board is left) and mute the review's announcements.
  - **Hatching is honest about what is known:** while a full run is going, every one of your turns is hatched ("Not analysed yet") because items of a turn finish in phases across turns; once it is done only turns with a `deadline` or `not-run` marker are hatched ("Not analysed"). A `search-incomplete` marker is the dashed "Not fully checked" circle. Notes (E07, "Hindsight check not finished", M8-1) are shown only in the turn's detail under "Not flagged", never as a flag or a marker.
  - **Practice judges** (`restore.puzzle.judge`, additive): `win-this-turn` is the L-slice rule unchanged; `survive-turn` is solved when the game is not lost by the time the turn ends (a win on the way counts), failed by any result that is not the player's win; `no-counter-on-attack` reads the actions since the cut (`GameSession.actionsSince`, additive): a `counter` (or a counter ability) by the player fails it, `endCounter` without one solves it. Given away practises `survive-turn` with `turn = T` from the attack in T−1 (the cut is the Blocker's attack, `flag.restoreIndex`), as the lead specified; it is not "too large": the cut is the same record prefix every kind uses, and the AI plays its turn against its real hand as in the game. DON!! unused, target and mulligan: Practice is disabled with "Practice for this kind comes with the drills." (the reason is text beside the button, not only a tooltip). A stale record disables Play from here and Practice with the F16 copy. "Practised: solved / not yet" is written to the review store as it happens (a miss never undoes a solve), shown on the list row, the detail and History, and the button reads "Practice again".
  - **Study restore points** are the detectors' own `flag.restoreIndex` (counter kinds at the defender's counter decision, DON!! and missed lethal at the turn's first Main, target just before the attack, given away at the Blocker's attack, mulligan at the decision) and the turn's start (snapshot `actionIndex`) for a marker or an unflagged turn. "Step through the better line" (Show) is wired only for the missed-lethal `public` proof, as in the L-slice; the other kinds' lines are read, not stepped.
  - **History** lists from `listGames()` plus one `getReview` each. "Read-only: older version" on a row needs the game's cards, which History does not load, so the screen records the verdict when a game is opened (`meta` kv key `stale.<id>`, deleted when the game is found live again or deleted from History). "Analysis incomplete · Resume" is a stored review that is missing, not `complete`, or of an older analysis version; Resume opens the review, which resumes. Delete mode (`?mode=delete`, the quota notice's link; `useSearch` in the router re-renders on a query-only change) deletes only the picked games after an inline confirmation, then clears the "full" notice. Export per game uses `exportGame`.
  - **Lessons:** `/learn/:lessonId` reads `docs/content/curriculum.md` bundled by Vite as raw text (`?raw`, the way the tutorial's words are) and parsed once, not a generated JSON file: same effect, no generator to keep in step. A test checks that every lesson id the explanations can link to (`LESSON` and the two fixed ids) has a page. The worked example, "Show me" and drill are M9.
  - **Names, a11y:** flag names read "Turn 9, your turn: missed lethal, severe, holds whatever they held." (timeline cells); a flag list row is a listbox option named from its content ("Turn 9 your turn Missed lethal Severe Holds whatever they held"), because a different `aria-label` fails WCAG 2.5.3 (axe `label-content-name-mismatch`). The timeline is one `role="application"` region with a roving tab stop; Up and Down between the two rows is in accessibility §3.12 and §3.16. Review CSS classes are prefixed `rv-` (an unprefixed `.tl` collided with the board's top-left plates and broke the 720p post-game button; found by the e2e).
  - **Additive changes outside the WP6 files:** `ExplanationView` gained `lessonLinks` and `archetype` (the guide's name on the mulligan label); `ai/src/search/review/index.ts` also exports `guideName` and `isSourced` (for that label); `router.tsx` gained `useSearch`; `App.tsx` routes `/reviews/:id` full-screen and `/learn/:id`.
- **Spec items cut or reduced:** the opponent's "Why?" markers on the timeline (they need the AI's per-move explanations from the live game, which a saved record does not carry); a text-free thumbnail of the restored position in the detail (Study is one click away and is the full board); the resume slot stays WP5's cut. Test changes: `learning.spec.ts` (the flag is one timeline button and one listbox option, not two buttons; and a new Practised check at the end) and `reviews-stub.spec.ts` (the stubs became the real pages).
- **Rejected:** keeping the live controller behind the review (a saved game could not open the same way); re-running only the missing items on resume (the request has no item filter; the results merge by id, so a resume costs a full run but never loses what was stored); `aria-label` on listbox options (fails label-in-name); a lesson JSON generator.

## 2026-11-25 — M8: a turn whose public lethal check is refuted counts as checked even if the hindsight search runs out (M8-1)

- **Context:** the WP1 bench reviewed a 303-decision game in about 24 s but marked 6 of 9 turns "Not fully checked" (target ≤ 10%). In every case the `public` check (what the player could see) refuted lethal quickly; only the `actual` hindsight search over the opponent's real cards ran out of nodes on mid-game boards.
- **Decision (lead):** when the `public` check is refuted, the turn is checked for missed lethal. If the `actual` search then caps, the turn gets a quiet note "Hindsight check not finished: with their real cards there may have been a win you could not have seen" and no marker. A "Not fully checked" marker stays only when the `public` check itself caps (or another detector caps on a judgement it makes). The marker-rate gate (≤ 10%) counts markers, not hindsight notes. OPEN-QUESTIONS M8-1 closed.
- **Built (M8-1):** `checkLethalTurn` (`ai/src/search/lethal/review.ts`; `checkLethalItem` is its flag half) returns `{ flag, note }`: `public` refuted and `actual` out of nodes gives `note` = `hindsightNote` ("Hindsight check not finished"), no flag, no marker; `public` unknown stays a marker. `given-away` does the same where its hindsight (`actual`) search runs after the board check answered (their board alone, `threat`, finished): a note, `theirs` wording ("With their real cards they may have had a win whatever you did."); a capped board check stays a marker. `should-counter` is unchanged: all its searches are `actual` ones that find the flag itself, there is no player-visible answer beneath them, so a cap there stays a marker. Tests: `review-pipeline.test.ts` (planted position: public refutes in 36,875 nodes, actual in 56,873; cap 45,000 gives the note, 20,000 the marker, 150,000 nothing) and `review/given-away.test.ts` (caps 2,000 and 1,500).
- **Fixture bug found and fixed (`ai/test/support/review-record.ts`):** `playRecord` sent only the cards the engine had loaded at the start (`state.defs`), so the review could not sample the seat's undrawn deck or the opponent's sourced list ("Unknown card id") and every `target` item ended "Not fully checked" in about 1 ms. The app sends the whole set (`ctl.cards`); the fixture now does too. Not a change to the review.
- **Measured (seed 70012, 303 decisions, `p1`, one thread, bench `markerRate` = marked turns / reviewed turns):** before (759e8cba): 26.0 s, 7 of 9 turns marked (0.778: six missed lethal, should-counter, and `target` on turns 8 to 14 from the fixture bug). After: 26.1 s, 4 of 9 marked (0.444): missed lethal on turns 10, 12 and 14 and should-counter on turn 15. The target of 10% is **not met**; see OPEN-QUESTIONS M8-2. Hindsight notes: turns 4, 6 and 8.
- **Rejected:** dropping the hindsight search (loses real Hindsight flags where it finishes); marking every capped hindsight search (most turns would read as unchecked although the player-visible question is answered); a larger node cap (more time did not help in the bench).

## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP1 pipeline: item conventions, shards, caps, and what a 300-decision review costs

- **Context:** WP1 builds the planner, runner, scheduler, worker loop and pool that every M8 detector runs in (`ai/src/search/review/{plan,run,schedule,did}.ts`, `app/src/play/review/{pool,analyse,review-worker}.ts`).
- **Decisions:**
  - **Item conventions** (the table is in `plan.ts`; WP2 to WP4 are written against it): one item per mulligan decision, per `endTurn` (`don-unused`), per battle in which a reviewed seat played a Counter (`counter-wasted`, at the battle's first `counter`), per reviewed turn that passes the `attackersPossible` prefilter (`missed-lethal`, at the turn's first Main), per attack with two or more legal targets (`target`), and, only when a reviewed seat lost on the winner's own turn, two items at the winner's first Main of that turn (`should-counter`, `given-away`). `item.index` is the number of actions applied before the decision. Auto-skipped actions never get an item; a rewound branch is not in `record.actions` (a rewind truncates the line), so it is skipped by construction. A record with no result is refused (`ReviewRefused`, "The game is not over.").
  - **Missed lethal is ported, not rewritten.** `reviewMissedLethal` is now `planLethalItems` plus `checkLethalItem`; the per-turn walk (what the seat attacked with, whether it won) is read from the record inside `checkLethalItem`, so an item needs only `stateAt(index)`. One behaviour change, sound: when the `public` check refutes with `any-hand` certainty no `actual` search runs (an any-hand refutation holds against the hand they had), so it can no longer end as a spurious "not fully checked".
  - **Caps.** With a `nodes` option a search gets exactly that cap and no clock is read (tests, "Analyse deeper" of the L-slice screen at 1,500,000). Without it, `nodes` is 150,000 and `ms = min(kindCap, (deadline - now) / searchItemsLeft)`; kind caps: missed lethal 6 s, should-counter and given-away 4 s, target 1.5 s, the cheap kinds 0.5 s. The deadline is 55 s; an item that would start after it does not run and becomes a per-turn `deadline` marker. A `deeper` re-run uses `factor` times the cap and sits outside the deadline.
  - **The pool deals shards, not items.** Every worker plans the same record (deterministic) and runs the items at positions `index mod count` of the phase-sorted list; `prioritise` goes to all workers, `deeper` to all (a worker that owns none answers `done` at once). Results are keyed by item id. Size `min(3, hardwareConcurrency - 1)`, at least 1. `ReviewRequest.start` gained an optional `shard`; `ReviewFlag` and `ReviewMarker` gained an optional `lethal` proof (`public`, `actual`, `player`, `actionIndex`) so the L-slice screen and WP6's Study and Practice keep the line's handles. Both additions are additive.
  - **`startReview` keeps the L-slice screen's shape** (a promise of `LethalFlag[]`) on top of the pool, so `ReviewFlow.tsx` is unchanged until WP6; `startReviewStream` gives WP6 results, progress, `prioritise` and `deeper`. The guard test now also bans `reviewRecord`, `ReviewJob` and `planReview` outside `app/src/play/review/`.
  - **The fixture** (`scripts/review-fixture.ts`, `ai/test/support/review-record.ts`): seeded Pirate-vs-Pirate, `p1` human and `p2` ai, the first seed from 70,001 with at least 300 actions (a record's actions are its player-level decisions: AI moves carry no `intentId`, so none merge). Playing it takes about 20 s; it is generated, never committed.
- **Measured (one thread, seed 70012, 303 decisions, reviewing `p1`):** the review takes about 24 s: the cheap, lost-turn and value phases cost nothing; missed lethal costs everything (six turns, each at its cap). All six reach the cap in both modes: the `public` check is refuted by the hand bound at once on three of them, but the `actual` search of a real mid-game Pirate board does not finish in 150,000 nodes (about 8 s). The marker rate is therefore high (6 of 9 reviewed turns) and well above the plan's 10% target; see OPEN-QUESTIONS. Wall time is within the 60 s gate even single-threaded; the pool divides it.
- **Rejected:** an `actual`-mode bound from the real hand (the reduced defender's soundness needs the weakest card, not the strongest, so it refutes little); dealing items by a shared queue between workers (needs main-thread planning or message round trips for a gain the static deal already gives); a per-item node cap in the app (the clock protects the deadline, the node cap keeps the L-slice's 150,000).

## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP4: the attack-target flag, `TARGET_GAP` = 1 and three more conditions

- **Context:** WP4 (`ai/src/search/review/{target,explain-target}.ts`) flags an attack on the wrong target (E08 board over face, E09 face over board). The spec's gate was a `valueActions` gap over the attacker's legal targets, tuned on Warlord's own attacks: under 2% flagged, E08 and E09 flagged.
- **What the corpus showed (`ai/test/review/target-corpus.test.ts`, 40 seeded Warlord-vs-Warlord games on the pinned meta lists, 948 attacks, 581 with a choice of target):** the plain gap cannot do both. Warlord's own attacks differ from `valueActions`'s best by up to 9 units (and by about 1000 where the rollouts see a win), and E08's gap is 1.35 (the K.O. of Newgate, whatever the hand), so a threshold under the 2% bound (about 2) loses E08, and one that keeps E08 flags 5% to 7% of Warlord's attacks. E09, as the doc words it (no DON!! stated), had a gap of 0.08.
- **Decisions:**
  1. **`TARGET_GAP` = 1** (heuristic units, about one card each, as `GAP_THRESHOLD`), with three more conditions in `worthFlagging`: (a) the better target wins in at least 3 of the 4 guessed hands (per-guess values are recovered from the means of 1..k guesses, which share a seeded prefix, so `score.ts` is untouched); (b) neither value is 100 or more (a value near 1000 means the rollouts won the game: a lethal question, answered exactly by missed-lethal); (c) the difference has a reason in the cards' public text, the same facts the explanation is built from: board over face needs a Character that lifts their Leader's power (an ongoing power effect on their Leader, as Newgate's), face over board needs a Character whose text does nothing on the board and a Leader hit that takes two cards or more to stop. Together: 1 of 948 (0.11%; 0.17% of those with a choice) of Warlord's attacks flagged (bound 2%). Dropping (c) gives 5% to 7%; dropping (a) or (b) alone adds a few attacks each.
  2. **The planted E09 holds two active DON!!** that nothing spends (the doc says nothing of your DON!!): with none the heuristic's gap is 0.08, with two it is 2.28. E08's gap does not depend on the rest of the position (checked over your Life, active DON!! and their hand).
  3. **Battle numbers come from the engine, at the first window the opponent owes:** the attack is declared, your own prompts ([When Attacking] to use or decline, its cost, its target) are settled by trying every answer (at most 300 nodes) and keeping the one that leaves the target weakest, and the attacker's and target's power are read where the opponent's first decision opens. Nothing of theirs has run, so nothing hidden is read; E08's "return 1 rested DON!! to give it −2000" comes out of this (Newgate 8000 to 6000, they need 3000, two cards at +2000).
  4. **Words from feature differences:** the target's ongoing text from its script labels (`textFacts`), the arithmetic, their Life and hand, and the biggest Counter in their sourced lists (`biggestCounter`). claim, outcome, nextTime, conceptId, lessonId and certainty equal E08 and E09 in the doc; the evidence lines differ in wording (a generic template, not Newgate's and Hawkins's own sentences) and the figure keys are generic (`targetPower`, `stopNeeded`, `maxPerCard`, `cardsToSave`, `effectBoost`, `attacksAtLeader`, `chosenPower`, `handSize`, `biggestCounter`), so the doc's `newgateBoost` and `newgateAfterMinus` are not matched. A search that runs out of budget is a "Not fully checked" marker, never a clean attack. Budgets: 150,000 nodes is 120 rollouts per guess, `ms` is shared by the 1 + 2 + 3 + 4 guesses the calls play.
  5. **Within-turn attack order is not built** (spec): each declaration is judged alone. The later same-turn attacks appear in "What you did" and as the other steps of the line.
- **Rejected:** raising `TARGET_GAP` to 2 (loses E08); changing `valueActions` or the heuristic for one flag (shared with the Pirate and Warlord); judging the target with Warlord's own search (the flag would be Warlord's agreement with itself); a "characters only" corroboration by cost or power (Elbaph cards with +12 cost made the heuristic call a K.O. worth 3 to 6 units).

## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP2: the exact flags (DON!! unused, counter wasted, mulligan) and what their items point at

- **Context:** WP2 builds the three detectors that need no search (`ai/src/search/review/{don,counter-wasted,mulligan}.ts`, `exact.ts`, `ai/src/search/mulligan.ts`). WP1 plans their items; the readings below are what the detectors assume, so WP1's planner can match them.
- **Item indexes (assumed, tested directly with planted records):** `don-unused`: `index` is the human `endTurn` action. `counter-wasted`: the first action of the human's counter decision (a detector given a later index inside the decision backs up to it). `mulligan`: the human `mulligan` action. A detector returns `null` for an index that is none of these, for an AI seat and for an index in `record.auto`.
- **DON!! unused** reads the ledger on the state where the turn passes (the first state after the `endTurn` whose turn number differs), so [End of Your Turn] effects have resolved and nothing of theirs has run (M7-R1); E07's Usopp restand therefore reads 5, as the doc says. It flags from 1 unused DON!! (moderate from 3), only if the turn gave them a use: an attack declared or legal, or a playable card costing no more than the unused DON!!. `activate` abilities are not counted as a use (their cost is not readable from the legal list), which only makes it flag less. Game turn 1 never flags (1 DON!!, no attack). `explainUnused` gained an optional last parameter `threshold` (default `UNUSED_FLAG_AT`, so the live coach is unchanged) and says "that 1" for one DON!!. The history row is the attack on their Leader that failed by the smallest margin the unused DON!! could cover; without one the row comes from `explainUnused('ending')` on the state before `endTurn`, else a playable card.
- **Counter wasted.** (a) "lost anyway": the hit lands after the committed counters (ending the counter step with `reduce`, a Life Trigger declined), never when the hit ends the game; exact, `any-hand`, moderate, conceptId `counters-and-blockers`, L05 (E19). (b) "not needed": the counters stopped the hit, Life was at least `LIFE_SAFE` (3), and a public upper bound on their remaining Life-taking hits does not reach the Life left after taking it (E04, L10). The bound counts the attackers `legalActions` offers against the Leader after the hit (a Double Attack one counts 2) and, per card in their hand (only the number is read), one added attacker if their active DON!! pay for the cheapest card in their archetype's sourced lists that is a Rush Character or sets a rested Character active. If only the real hand proves it, the flag is Hindsight (`needed-hidden-info`, severity `hindsight`). A counter whose card does more than add power (an Event whose script has a second effect or a condition) is never judged. Both cases carry the `no-counter-on-attack` practice judge; for (a) that judge accepts only "take the hit", not the "also fine" alternative the line shows.
- **Mulligan.** `mulliganVerdict` is the Pirate rule moved to `ai/src/search/mulligan.ts` (Pirate calls it). Only a guide that cites a handbook entry (`SH-` in the rule's source) judges: the default playbook and "no sourced rule" guides give no flag. The nextTime's guide name is the archetype id's suffix ("Rocks"). The E11 fourth row (leader-specific prose) is not generated.
- **Rejected:** `preview` for the lost-anyway test (it stopped before a Life Trigger, so the hit read as not landed; `reduce` with the Trigger declined is exact); judging `activate` abilities as a DON!! use; flagging a hit that ends the game as a wasted counter (trying was right); one more shared helper in `app/` (all of it stays in `ai/`).

## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP5: local save in IndexedDB, `verifyReplay`, the validator, and what was cut

- **Context:** M8 WP5 (`app/src/store/*`, `engine/src/record.ts`, `app/src/settings/*`, the tutorial timing store). Spec: DECISIONS "M8 plan", `m8-plan.md`.
- **Decisions:**
  - **`verifyReplay(record, cards)`** (engine, additive; `verifyReplayWith` in `record.ts`, exported from `game.ts`): replays from the start without the `ENGINE_VERSION` string check, requires the game's own cards and scripts versions to match, and compares `summaryHash` with every `TurnSnapshot.hash` at its `actionIndex`. Result `{ok: true, engineDiffers}` or `{ok: false, reason: 'cards' | 'scripts' | 'hash' | 'error' | 'no-snapshots'}`. It changes no hash and no replay (tested; property and golden gates at `ENGINE_GAMES=2000` pass). `liveness()` in the app is one call to it (the engine restricts full-state calls to `session/`, so `replay` is not used there); liveness is always computed from the record, never stored, so an imported file cannot claim it.
  - **A `summaries` store** (database `optcg-sim` v1, beside the spec's `games`, `reviews`, `current`, `timing`, `progress`, `meta`): `{id, meta}` with an `endedAt` index, written in the same transaction as the game. History lists from it, so listing never deserialises records. Measured: a 303-action game is 559 KiB as JSON (21 snapshots are about 90% of it), so reading 200 records to draw a list would be about 100 MB.
  - **Writes are all or nothing:** every write runs in one transaction that is aborted on any failure (including a synchronous throw from `put`), and a browser `QuotaExceededError` (or code 22) becomes `StoreQuotaError`. The 200-game cap is the same class (`'max-games'`). Nothing is ever deleted without the player: the notice "Saved games are full. Delete old reviews?" links to `/reviews?mode=delete` (History's delete mode is WP6's page; the query name is the contract).
  - **Synchronous small stores:** `TimingStore` and `readLethalProgress` are synchronous today, so `initStore()` (awaited in `main.tsx` before the first render, 3 s cap) reads `timing` and `progress` into memory (`SyncKv`) and writes behind in order. A failed write is reported, never thrown. One record per tutorial run, last 20 kept.
  - **Fallback:** no IndexedDB, an open that throws, or one that does not answer in 3 s gives the in-memory store, the one notice "Saving is off in this browser; Export keeps a copy." (not shown on the board or in the tutorial; dismissible), and the tutorial log and lethal progress stay in localStorage as before.
  - **Migration** copies `optcg.tutorial.timing.v1` and `optcg.progress.lethal.v1` (entries validated; runs by run id, progress merged by game and turn keeping the furthest step), writes a meta mark, and deletes the keys only after every write completed. A failure leaves the keys and the store opens anyway; it is idempotent. The key names are duplicated in `store/migrate.ts` (an import cycle otherwise) and a test pins them to `tutorial/timing.ts`.
  - **Import is untrusted:** a hand-written validator builds new objects field by field (unknown fields dropped; `__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype` keys dropped; nesting, node and list-size limits; ids restricted to `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`; 50 MB cap checked before reading), so one damaged game is left out with a reason and the rest import. It checks shape and size, not play: a forged record imports as data and `liveness` refuses it. Everything renders as React text (a source scan test forbids `innerHTML`, `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, `eval`). Stored reviews pass the same whitelist (`sanitizeReview`), which is how "flags trimmed of heavy facts" is met: the types carry no heavy field today, so extra facts a detector might attach are dropped rather than stored.
  - **Persistent storage** is requested only when "Keep my saved games" is pressed; Settings says when the browser cannot be asked.
  - **`GameSaver`'s default `onError`** is now `reportStoreError` (WP0 seam, additive): a failed save flips the one "full" notice and never touches the game.
  - **Dev dependency** `fake-indexeddb` (tests only; the wrapper itself has none).
- **Cut:** **the resume slot's UI and checkpointing** (Home and `/play` "Resume your game vs <Leader> (turn N)?"). The store half is built and tested (`getCurrent`, `setCurrent`, `clearCurrent` on both stores). What remains: a debounced checkpoint in the PlayRoute save effect (write when `actionCount` changes, clear at game over), the `SetupChoice` stored beside the record (an additive field on `CurrentGame`, since `startGame` needs the decks and tier to rebuild the config), `startGame` passing `config.restore`, and the banner. Cut because it touches `PlayRoute`, `Home` and the controller that WP6 and WP7 are editing, and the live-AI continuation from a restored record is untested.
- **Found, not fixed:** (1) 200 long games (about 560 KiB each) would export to about 110 MB, over the 50 MB import cap. "Export everything" says so when its file is over the cap and points to single-game export from Reviews. Dropping the snapshot views of the AI's seat, or a lighter snapshot, would cut it by half; to M9 if it matters. (2) `MemoryStore.importAll` counts a game as added before it skips it for the cap (WP0 code; the IndexedDB store counts correctly). (3) Import does not evaluate staleness per game (that needs each game's cards loaded); History does it when a game is opened, with `liveness`.
- **Rejected:** reading records to list games; a localStorage fallback for saved games (a record is too large); migrating decks, coach settings or tutorial progress (they stay in localStorage as specified); auto-deleting the oldest games at the cap; requesting persistence on first save.

## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP3: the two search flags (should have countered, lethal given away)

- **Context:** `ai/src/search/review/{should-counter,given-away}.ts` replace the WP0 stubs; both run exact searches from the record and plug into the registry as `(item, ctx) => TurnResult | null`.
- **Decisions:** (1) **Items.** `should-counter`: `item.turn` is the turn the player lost and `item.index` is at or before the winner's first Main of it; any other turn, a seat that is not human, a win by anything but damage at 0 Life, or a conceded game returns null. `given-away`: the flag lives on your turn T−1 (`flag.turn`, `restoreIndex` and `decisionIndex` = the Blocker's attack); the item may name T−1 or T and finds the same game, so the planner (WP1) may create it from either. (2) **Should-counter** runs `lethal` (`actual`) at each attacker Main `Mi` of the lost turn; the game's final state counts as proven. The flagged decision is the defender's first Counter or Block in battle `i` (else its `endCounter`, else a Block of nobody; `record.auto` actions never count) where `Mi` is refuted and `Mi+1` proven; the stronger line is the defender's moves along `search()`'s refutation (`bestAttempt.trace`) replayed with `reduce`, one `Move` per battle. `any-hand` only when the attacker's hand at `Mi` is empty and the refutation holds with `deckKnown: false`; otherwise Hindsight. A cap hit before a flag is a marker. (3) **Given away** is the lead's definition exactly: base `threat()` gives `holds === false`; per Blocker attack, the branch skips the battle (the attack until you are back at your Main), re-applies your later T−1 actions while legal (an illegal one of yours is skipped; an opponent action that no longer fits drops the candidate), ends the turn, settles to their Main, and re-runs `threat()`; refuted or `holds === true` flags, `any-hand`. When the base is not a board-only loss but their real hand won (`lethal` actual proven at their Main), a branch refuted in `actual` mode is a Hindsight flag, never severe. No candidate helps: a turn note "Their board won whatever you did." and no flag. (4) **Practice** for both is `survive-turn` with `turn` = the lost turn T; for given-away the puzzle opens at the attack in T−1, so WP6's judge must run from there through the end of T. (5) **Lesson.** E21 is concept `lethal-math` with lesson L15 (the lead's choice); E05 is `counter-or-take`, L10. (6) **Shared helper** `defence-line.ts` (`renderDefence`: the defender's choices as one `Move` per battle, used for "What you did" and the line). (7) The detectors return null on a context with no record, so the WP0 registry stub test still passes.
- **Rejected:** a flag from the first lost battle by position alone (the exact `Mi`/`Mi+1` test is what makes it a mistake and not a loss); calling `any-hand` when their hand is merely empty-looking in `public` information (it is read from the full state in review, and the differential shows the flag unchanged by their deck, Life and, for given-away, hand); showing the E05 doc's "so these numbers couldn't change" (Kaido's Leader effect can return DON!! mid-turn, so the copy says their hand adds nothing instead).

## 2026-11-25 — M8 WP0 contracts: item ids, flat store, one save per finished vs-AI game

- **Context:** WP0 fixes the seams WP1–WP7 build on (`ai/src/search/review/`, `app/src/store/`, `app/src/play/save-game.ts`).
- **Decisions:** (1) `ReviewRecord` is declared structurally in `ai/` (`GameRecord` plus `seats`, `auto`, `rewinds`) so `ai/` never imports `app/`; the app's `SessionRecord` satisfies it. (2) Detectors share one signature `(item, ctx) => TurnResult | null`; `ctx.stateAt(index)` is the runner's replay cache (WP1), so a detector never replays on its own. `TurnResult.itemId` is the item that produced it. (3) The L-slice `LethalFlag` stays as is; `lethalToReviewFlag` / `lethalToTurnResult` adapt it (a `not-fully-checked` flag becomes a `ReviewMarker`, never a flag). (4) `ReviewStore` uses flat game and review methods (`saveGame`, `getReview`, …) with `timing`, `progress` and `meta` as small key-value stores; `importAll` takes an already validated bundle (WP5's validator sits in front). The in-memory store enforces `MAX_GAMES` (200) with a `StoreQuotaError` and never deletes silently. (5) The save seam is a `GameSaver` keyed by controller identity: one `saveGame` per finished game against the AI, none for hotseat or unfinished games; a failed save is reported to a callback and never blocks the game. Game ids are `g<endedAt base36>-<seed base36>`.
- **Rejected:** importing `SessionRecord` into `ai/` (layering); a per-turn save (a game is saved once, at its end; the resume slot is WP5's).

## 2026-11-25 — M8 plan: seven flag kinds, a 55 s scheduled pipeline, IndexedDB with `verifyReplay`

- **Context:** M8 (PLAN row 19, 30 h) widens the review to every flag category PROMPT.md lists and adds local save. A planning pass produced `docs/design/m8-plan.md` (eight work packages, about 40 h, with a cut order).
- **Decisions (lead):**
  - **Lethal given away** means: on your turn you made a move that let their board alone win on their next turn, and the same turn without that move would not have. M8 checks Blocker attacks as the candidate moves; certainty is `any-hand` only when both threat checks are board-only.
  - **`verifyReplay`** (engine): a saved record whose ENGINE_VERSION string differs is still live if replaying it reproduces every `TurnSnapshot.hash`; otherwise it is read-only with the F16 copy. Without it every engine bump would turn every saved game read-only.
  - **Lesson links** open a minimal `/learn/:lessonId` page built from curriculum.md (title, concept, drill, exit criterion); the full Learn hub stays with M9.
  - **Persistent storage** (`navigator.storage.persist`) is offered in Settings → Data, never requested automatically.
  - Only sourced guides judge a mulligan; Hindsight flags are never severe; a search cap yields a "Not fully checked" marker, never a clean turn.
- **Rejected:** judging mulligans against the default playbook (a guessed rule); flagging within-turn attack order separately (missed lethal covers the case that matters); requesting persistent storage on first save.

Phase and nothing of theirs has run. The old read came after a whole turn of theirs and counted the next turn's DON!!.
