## 2026-11-25 — M7 WP7: the S2 kit, three scenarios, the M7 screen-reader script and the doc fixes

- **Context:** M7's last work package before the independent review: everything the owner's session 2 needs (usability-plan §5.3, §7), the screen-reader script (accessibility.md §11.5) and the doc conflicts the plan listed. Code: `app/src/play/setup/usability-scenarios.ts`, `app/a11y/src/m7.ts`, the `a11y-nvda` workflow. Docs: `docs/design/usability/S2-kit.md`, `learning-ui.md` §10, `accessibility.md` §3.3 and §11.5, `flows.md` F1/F2/F20, `usability-plan.md` §7.
- **Scenarios (each proven by `app/test/usability-s2.test.ts`, played by `app/e2e/usability-s2.spec.ts`):**
  - `ub-hint-1`: Kaido, turn 7, 4 active DON!!, Yamato and Basil Hawkins in hand, Rocks with a bare board. The hint (Warlord, the player's own meta list) returns "play Yamato, give 1 DON!! to the Leader, attack their Leader, end with 5 DON!! active", with the numbers it rests on; the test checks the line, that its first step replays through the engine, that asking twice gives one answer, that the wall-clock budget gives a line starting with Yamato, and that no card from their hand is named. Chosen over the E15 position (Luffy): with the app's own meta list the hint there was "give 5 DON!! to the Leader, attack, end with 8 DON!! active" from 6 DON!! (an unexplained count a beginner would stumble over, not checked further), while this position's line is one a beginner can follow card by card.
  - `ub-why-1`: Rocks against the Pirate AI (Kaido, turn 6, 6 DON!!). The AI plays Yamato (its "Why?" cites its guide, "a DON!!-adding Character in its first three turns") and ends its turn with Basil Hawkins in hand and 3 DON!! up; the game then stops at the player's Main Phase. It is built to stop at a Main, not at a defence: **at an owed decision the decision panel is tall and the coach stays a strip, so the Coach's Why button does nothing visible there** (found while building the e2e; `W` and a log line's "Why?" still work, and the log is a three-line ticker). Not changed here; logged below. Tests: the AI's first move is Yamato, it has a Why with the guide's line, the last move's Why is "It ended its turn", every AI move with a choice has one.
  - `ub-blunder-1`: Kaido, turn 9, Kaido (12000) ready, Rocks at 0 Life with two +2000 Events. The lethal is proven against any hand; `missed-lethal` fires for `endTurn` with the claim "You have lethal this turn, and this move gives it up." and a legal better move; playing Yamato (which keeps the lethal) is not flagged; with Settings → Blunder warning on, the controller holds End turn and shows the note. Not a copy of `ub-lethal-1`'s board (S1's 1.4 task): this one needs a single hit, so the point is the warning, not the DON!! split.
- **S2-kit.md:** 75 minutes; prep; task cards 2.1 to 2.5; teach-back sheet with keys and the 2/1/0 rubric (Q1 to Q8 as the tutorial battery, "What does your Leader do?" for `t7-leader`, Q11 for the hint); SEQ; observation template; debrief; how to export the timing log and keep it local (only numbers go into `S2-*.md`; a private window forgets the log on close, so export first); VoiceOver stays the owner's listening pass; the pass criterion (tutorial under 20 minutes from the log, teach-back answered at a mean of 1.5, coach never interrupts unprompted). Reads the `blunder` race honestly: a press inside the first second of a Main can commit silently (M7-W5-1), so the card tells the facilitator to wait and re-press.
- **Screen-reader script:** `app/a11y/src/m7.ts` with dry, NVDA and VoiceOver specs, in four parts (T tutorial t1 to t3, leave and resume; H hint; S Settings; B blunder note). The workflow's `script` input gains `m7` and `all`. The dry run passes (about 2 minutes); the NVDA run is dispatched by hand (never run here): `gh workflow run a11y-nvda.yml --ref <branch> -f browsers=both -f script=m7`. `app/test/a11y-m7.test.ts` holds the wiring. "Focus on Continue" in the brief is **Next** in the built tutorial.
- **Doc fixes:** `learning-ui.md` §10 (the step IDs and one line each); `accessibility.md` §3.3 (**M7-V3 settled: `G` then `L`, `S`, `D`, `N`, `K`, `T`, `F` all go to the Leader row, as built**; `o` is not a go-to letter) and §11.5 rewritten to the built script; `flows.md` F1 (no live-region line on Home, no "Resume your game", no first-move coach line: not built), F2 (the spoken line, the completion screen with its exports, the first-game note's words) and F20 (rows for DON!! math, threat alerts, the tutorial timing log and the welcome question; the detector has no "Off"; no Tempo switch); `usability-plan.md` §7 (step IDs in the budget table, the log is in `localStorage` for now, where the export lives). The two fixes WP4 made (accessibility.md §3.12's `H` row and ARCHITECTURE §5's coach worker) were checked and stand.
- **Found, not fixed (for the review):** (1) the Coach's Why button is inert during a tall decision (a defence, a choice): either open it inside the decision body as the hint does, or hide the button there. Severity 2. (2) NVDA's browse mode may keep the letter keys the tutorial's step text names (`G`, `L`, `H`, `P`) when focus is on the step text (a group, not a control); the M7 script records it and carries on with an assist, as L-SR12 did for the coach. To be judged from the first NVDA run.
- **Rejected:** a seeded wall-clock AI in `ub-why-1` (the kit uses `aibudget=iterations` so the AI repeats); testing the Why on a defence (the coach does not show there); a scripted VoiceOver run of §11.5 by the agent (the owner's Mac, the owner's choice).

## 2026-11-25 — M7 WP5: the blunder gate, minimal Settings and the interruption-contract test

- **Context:** learning-ui §2.2 (the contract) and §2.8 (the warning), flows F20; WP2's `scoreDecision` / `warningsOf`, WP0's `commitGate` / `heldAction` / `act(..., { skipGate })`, `Decision.tsx` `noteSlot` / `commitLabel`. Code: `app/src/play/coach/{blunder.ts,BlunderNote.tsx}`, `app/src/settings/SettingsRoute.tsx`, `controller.ts`.
- **Decision:**
  - **`BlunderGuard`** (one per controller) owns the gate. It installs `controller.commitGate` only while the warning is on and the coach is offered (not hotseat, review, puzzle or tutorial), so every default game has no gate at all and the WP0 inert-seams test still holds. With it on, it asks the coach worker for a `score` (priority 3, 1.5 s budget) as an own-turn Main or effect-choice decision starts (the kinds the scorer can flag; other decisions are never scored), and again when the pointer or focus reaches the decision's commit controls (`onCommitIntent` on `DecisionPanel`) if nothing has been asked for that decision. The table is keyed to `${actionCount}:${kind}` and dropped when the decision changes.
  - **The 300 ms rule is real, not a flag:** at the press, a ready table decides at once. A table still pending holds the press for at most 300 ms (`BLUNDER_WAIT_MS`): an answer inside it warns or commits; a late answer commits the held press silently and the decision is spent, so nothing is ever shown for it. No answer (the worker's `not-implemented`, an error) commits at once. A decision is warned about once (`spent`): "Commit anyway" and any later press go through with no check. The gate gets the action's `intentId` too (additive: `CommitGate(action, intentId?)`, `controller.heldIntent`), because the late path commits later and a DON!! give needs its intent.
  - **What is shown:** the note in the decision panel's `noteSlot` (`BlunderNote`): the explanation's claim and outcome, "Instead: <the better action>" when it is still legal, "Use the better choice" (plays the better action, past the gate), "Commit anyway" and "Don't warn me about this". When the held action is End turn the primary button's own label becomes "Commit anyway" (`commitLabel`) and the note is its `aria-describedby`; for any other held action the note carries its own "Commit anyway" button, so a label never lies about what the primary does. The note is spoken once, politely ("Warning: ... Choose the better move, or press Commit anyway."). Focus never moves; no dialog.
  - **Mute:** "Don't warn me about this" adds the kind to `profile.muted` and leaves the action uncommitted (the next press goes through); Settings lists the muted kinds with "Warn me again". The gate filters `warningsOf` by the muted list at the press, so muting applies to the table already in hand.
  - **Announcement source tags (additive):** `controller.announce(text, source)` and `alert(text, source)` take `'game' | 'coach'` (default `'game'`); `controller.onAnnounce` reports every polite and assertive line with its source. The coach and the guard announce as `'coach'`. The contract test reads it.
  - **Settings** (`/settings`, a header link, a lazy route): Coach (verbosity, lethal detector, blunder warning, DON!! math, threat alerts, the muted list, reset), Tutorial timing (the stored runs, Export JSON and CSV through WP6's `toJson`/`toCsv`/`download`, Delete with an inline "Delete for good / Keep it" step), and "Show the welcome question" (`clearWelcome()`). Native controls only; every change is spoken politely and stored at once; a game reads the profile when it starts. Opponent speed, reduced motion and the rest of F20's Game/Keyboard/Data rows are not built (M8).
  - **The contract test** (`app/test/coach-contract.test.ts`, `app/e2e/coach-contract.spec.ts`): a whole game with every coach setting at its default has zero coach focus requests (`focusSignal`, `hintFocus`, a `focusin` inside the Coach region), zero expansions (`profile.expanded`, the region's `data-mode`), zero assertive coach lines and zero `hint`, `score`, `threat` and `lethal` requests. Variants: tier 1 always on; blunder on. The watcher is tested against a deliberately leaky coach, and the real code was made to leak once (see the report) to see both tests fail.
- **Cut:** a score re-ask when the profile's mute list changes mid-decision (the gate filters, so nothing needs it); a "Don't warn" that also commits (a mute is not a commit); F20's Game, Cards and Data rows.
- **Rejected:** a synchronous-only gate that treats "not ready" as "late" (the spec says a press waits up to 300 ms); relabelling the primary button for every held action (a mislabel for choices whose primary is not the held action).

## 2026-11-25 — M7 WP4: the coach panel

- **Context:** learning-ui §2.1 to §2.9, flows F9, accessibility §3.12; WP1 (hint), WP2 (`donLedger`), WP3 (threat) on main. Code: `app/src/play/coach/{Coach,HintSection,MathTempo}.tsx`, `hint.ts`, `tempo.ts`, `coach.ts`.
- **Decision:**
  - **Strip:** Hint, Why, Lethal, Math, Tempo and the chevron, each one word; the key letters are no longer drawn on the buttons (a 320 px rail has about 50 px per button) and the badge rides the button's top-right corner so no label grows. Badges: "ready" (Hint, "old" when stale), the existing Lethal ones plus "▲ Threat" when alerts are on, and Tempo's "paid+given/total" (a silent meter, so always shown). The profile `tab` default is now `hint`; a stored tab is kept.
  - **The coach worker** gets the player's own `game` (own list, both Leaders, tier Warlord) in `init`, via `GameConfig.myDeck` (set from the chosen deck or the scenario's list) and `coachPort(init, game?)`. The AI seat's worker never sees it (test). Hint and threat requests are `searchView(me)` only.
  - **Stopping a hint:** Esc cancels the request and the next request to that worker restarts it (`CoachPort.restart?`, `AiClient.restart` = the crash path) if the stopped hint's budget has not elapsed. The only higher-priority request that can wait behind a hint is none (the coach worker has priorities 2 to 4), so the rule is "any request behind a stopped hint".
  - **Do step 1 / Stage this:** main-phase: `controller.stage` lines up the next action of the hint's first step from the legal list (picks of one set: whichever the list offers, R14); the decision panel's note slot shows "Lined up by the coach: ... Do it / Clear", focus goes to Do it, and nothing is applied until the player presses it. Defence: the Blocker and Counters go into the defence planner. Where the decision panel owns the rail (a defence, a choice) the hint renders inside the decision body.
  - **Ticks:** the player's own actions are recorded (`Coach.noteAction`, before the engine applies) and read against the line by kind, printed card id and target; a step ticks when done, anything else greys the hint ("Board changed: ask again"). A new turn clears it. A hint asked on their move waits and starts when the decision is yours.
  - **Threat (additive protocol change):** the `threat` request gains `priority?: 2 | 4` so the always-on alert runs at 4 and a press at 2 (`priorityOf`). Runs at their Main Phase start and after each of their moves only when "Tell me when their board threatens lethal" is on; `L` asks at 2. Results are keyed to the position; the copy always says it does not count their hand. The alert speaks only for a proven threat.
  - **Tempo:** from `donLedger`; frozen at your last turn's split on theirs; E06/E07 lines from `explainUnused`/`explainHeld` on your turn.
  - **Hotseat:** no coach panel, hint or threat; the defender's panel shows "<attacker>, look away." while the hand is shown (F13b.3).
  - **Tutorial:** coach features stay off (`supported` is false in the tutorial).
- **Cut:** "Make taller" on a long hint (the hint scrolls in the open coach); the lesson text has no link target until the curriculum routes exist (M8+).
- **Rejected:** a second hint button state for "stale vs finished" badge text; re-running the hint on a board change (flows F9.3: never automatic).

## 2026-11-25 — M7 WP2: blunder scoring and the DON!! tempo ledger

- **Context:** learning-ui §2.8 and §2.9, explanations E06, E07, E17. Code: `ai/src/coach/{blunder,tempo,explain-blunder}.ts`, `ai/src/search/score.ts` (the only place with full-state engine calls, by the ESLint boundary), worker `score` branch.
- **Decision:**
  - `scoreDecision(view, ctx)` values every legal action (k = 4 guessed states from the seat's own list and the archetype prior, seed from the view's public fingerprint, rolled out to the next turn like the planners) and flags three kinds. `missed-lethal`: a public lethal is proven and the action is `endTurn`, or after the action (in one guess, follow-ups settled) no lethal is proven any more; a search cap ("unknown") never flags. `unused-don`: `endTurn` with at least 3 unused DON!! (`donLedger`), **and** an attack and a DON!! give are still legal (a warning on DON!! nothing can use now is noise; the review keeps the plain ledger rule). `gap`: only in `choose`, `optional` and `trigger` decisions on your own turn; best value minus the action's value at least `GAP_THRESHOLD` = 2 (about two cards).
  - Value of an own-turn effect choice adds the DON!! the ledger holds for an opponent-turn effect, at the Counter rate (0.25 per 1000 power, so 0.5 per DON!! for OP13-001): without it the rollout cannot see a Leader's defence, and E17 (setting 4 DON!! active past OP13-001's limit of 5) would not be a gap. Setting 4 vs 2 differs by exactly 2.5.
  - `donLedger(view, cards)`: total, active, paid (rested), given, held {n, reasons}, unused. Held: the cost of each [Counter] Event in hand (script timing `counter`), and an `onOpponentAttack` effect that rests DON!! from the cost area when its given-DON!! condition holds and the active count is within its stated limit (so with 6 active, OP13-001 holds nothing: RL-46); capped at active. Read from public scripts, like `eventCounterOf`. M8 imports this function.
  - `ScoreTable` gains an optional `warnings` (additive to the WP0 contract): by action key, each `{kind, explanation, better}`. `index.ts` exports `scoreDecision`, `answerScore`, `warningsOf`, `donLedger` and the explainers. Without the seat's list (no `game` in the worker) the table is `incomplete` and only the plain rules run.
  - `explanations.md` gains E06b, the live form of E06 (the tempo meter's text is E06's claim and first row; the history rows are the review's). Snapshot tests compare E17, E07 and E06b with the doc, field for field.
- **Threshold evidence:** 30 seeded Warlord-vs-Warlord games on the pinned meta lists (`BLUNDER_GAMES=10 BLUNDER_FROM=0,10,20`): 5,727 scored decisions, 16 flagged (0.28%): 15 gap, 1 unused-don, 0 missed-lethal. Gap quantiles over effect choices: p90 0, p99 about 1.2 to 2.5 per run. Under 1% overall and by kind; T = 2 keeps E17 (2.5) flagged. Larger gaps (up to 992) are won-or-lost positions where Warlord's deeper search and this table's shallow rollouts disagree; they are the residual false positives.
- **Alternatives rejected:** flagging `endTurn` with unused DON!! regardless of attackers (noise after the last attack); a value gap on Main-phase actions (rollout noise, and learning-ui §2.8.6 wants clear mistakes only); using the settled state after an effect choice for the ledger (the quick policies play the whole next turn first).
- **Caveats:** the worker's first scoring round always runs in full (as the planners), so a Main with many options can exceed `budgetMs`; the late-warning rule (300 ms, WP5) already drops such answers. Under heavy CPU contention the corpus saw 1 to 5 s on a few positions.

## 2026-11-25 — M7 WP6: the guided tutorial (eight scripted steps on the real engine)

- **Context:** WP6 builds `app/src/tutorial/` and its content (PROMPT.md "Guided tutorial", flows F2, usability-plan §7). Cards were checked first: every ST-21 and ST-22 card plays fully (none is in `coverage-report.json`'s backlog; `app/test/tutorial.test.ts` asserts `notAutomated === 0` for both decks and that the scenario cards belong to them).
- **Taught cards (chosen so each lesson is clear):** t2 and t5 use Jinbe (ST21-005: cost 2, 4000, Counter +1000, no effect), so the first Character and the first Counter are the same easy card; t3 gives DON!! to the Leader; t4 is Leader against Leader (5000 against 5000: the tie goes to the attacker, which is the lesson); t5 is their Leader 5000 into yours 5000, answered with +1000; t6's top Life card is Gum-Gum Dawn Whip (ST21-016), whose [Trigger] K.O.s Decalvan Brothers (ST22-008, 4000), so Activate and Add to hand both do something visible; t7 is ST21-001's own `[DON!! x1] [Activate: Main]` giving 2 rested DON!! to Stussy; t8 is the Leader plus Tony Tony.Chopper against a lone Leader at 1 Life whose Life card (Edward.Newgate, ST22-003) has no Counter and no Trigger.
- **No rules logic in the app:** a step's `allowed` removes engine-legal actions and never adds; where it needs "a counter was played" it checks that the counter card left the hand (so `endCounter` is allowed once Jinbe is gone), not a power comparison. `done` reads the player's own view. The scripted opponent (`scripted.ts`) chooses only from `view.actions` of its own seat. Words that state a number read it from the view (`{leaderPower}`, `{oppLife}`); `leaderBase` is the printed power from card data.
- **Seams (all additive):** `TutorialStep` gained `visit`, `lit`, `failed`, `script`, `alwaysAsk`, and `ScenarioRef` takes a spec without `cards`; `TutorialFilter.onStray(handle | null)` for a click on something the step does not teach (a card that is not lit, End turn); `DecisionPanel` gained `tutorial` (no Rewind, no game-over footer) and `tutorialSlot` (the step card above the body, next to WP0's `noteSlot`, which stays below it for WP5); `Game` gained `tutorialSlot` and, when `config.tutorial` is set, hides the coach, gives the panel the tall geometry, drops the New/"played this turn" chips (a card is not sick because the step narrows the moves), and sends `P` to the step text; the controller refuses rewinds in the tutorial; `Board` gained `data-zid` on the player's zones (step 1 counts focus and click by zone). No WP0 contract changed.
- **Timing:** `timing.ts` accounts every event against an injected clock: a step's wall time is exactly active plus hidden; time outside the tutorial (Leave to Continue) is not counted at all (the run clock stops at `exited()`); an idle span over 120 s is added to `idleMs` and left in `activeMs`; hidden time never counts as idle; `rewinds` counts Back and t8's restart on the step that was left; a resume restarts the step and counts one exit on it. Storage is behind `TimingStore` (localStorage `optcg.tutorial.timing.v1`, last 20 runs; IndexedDB is M8). Export is a Blob download (JSON, CSV one row per step); ESLint now bans `navigator.sendBeacon` everywhere in `app/src` and `fetch` outside `art/` and `sw.ts`.
- **Progress and Home:** `optcg.tutorial.progress.v1` holds `{runId, step}` and is written on entering a step (and one step on when a step is done), so "Continue the tutorial (step N of 8)" is always the step that restarts. Completion writes lesson credit L01 to L07 (`optcg.tutorial.credit.v1`, for M9) and ends the welcome question (`optcg.welcome.v1`). The question is asked until answered; "Just look around" does not answer it (flows F1 step 5). Focus is not forced onto the first choice on Home (it would put the skip link second); Settings → "Show the welcome question" is WP5's, using `clearWelcome()`.
- **First-game preset:** `/play?preset=first` selects the tutorial decks (new "Tutorial decks" group in the Setup pickers), Rookie, and names itself on screen; the game sets Learner verbosity and the tier-1 detector on the loaded profile in memory only. Caveat: the coach saves the whole profile on its next setting change, so such a change during that game keeps the two values (low severity; the profile `firstGame` flag is `true` once the preset is taken and `false` once Home is chosen after completion).
- **Restricted picks:** t5 allows the Counter on the Leader only; t7 allows only the option "2" (choosing 0 or 1 would spend the once-per-turn ability on less than the lesson and leave the step unfinishable).
- **Words:** `docs/content/tutorial.md` (front matter: sources are CR numbers and the docs read, asOf 2026-11-25, confidence high), imported with Vite `?raw` and parsed at load; `app/test/tutorial.test.ts` is the content check (front matter, every key, every slot filled by the runner, intros of at most two sentences, only fixed rule facts typed as numbers, every card id exists).
- **Found, not mine:** accessibility.md §3.2 says `G` then `L` goes to the Leader, but the board maps `l`, `s`, `d`, `n`, `k`, `t` and `f` all to the first item of the player's Leader row, so keyboard copy says "press Right until your Leader is focused" (tested). WP7 or the accessibility owner should decide which is right.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a separate tutorial board (the lesson must be the real board); filtering with `whyNot` text for "Not yet" (an action can be legal in a real game: the line is the step's); one shared completion in Setup (the completion screen is where the time and the export are).

## 2026-11-25 — M7 WP3: the opponent's-turn threat check (a foreign Main stays searchable; ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.18)

- **Engine:** `skeletonOf` no longer marks the skeleton opaque for a foreign decision when that decision is `main` and the resolution stack is exactly the turn frame waiting in its `main` step (`atTurnMain`). Such a decision is built from no card: its shape is kind, player and a fixed prompt, and the skeleton keeps it in the **view's** public shape (`GENERIC_PROMPT`), not the owner's own prompt. Every other foreign decision (a damage prompt, a block, a counter, a choose, an order, a mulligan) stays opaque (M5a-02). "Empty stack" in the plan means the turn frame only, since the engine's turn is itself a frame; a Main decision with anything above it is opaque, which can only err toward not searching. `projectForSearch` is not recorded, so goldens are unchanged and `ENGINE_VERSION` stays 0.5.18 (the 2,000-game property run replays unchanged).
- **Tests (engine):** `engine/test/support/foreign-decision.ts` (`checkForeignDecision`) asserts, for every foreign decision reached, that the skeleton is searchable exactly at a foreign Main on the turn frame and that its decision equals the view's. `skeleton-diff.test.ts` and the leak property in `leaks.test.ts` call it, with floors on how many foreign Mains they reach. Verified to fail on leaky versions: copying the foreign Main verbatim (its prompt is the owner's own text) fails every skeleton-diff test and both leak runs; copying every foreign decision verbatim and never marking opaque fails the same ones, and would also leak a damage prompt's Life card name.
- **Threat search (`ai/src/search/lethal/threat.ts`):** two searches on the engine, both from your `SearchView`. Their hidden hand, deck and Life are blank cards (nothing guessed, and what the view knows of their cards is ignored); your face-down Life is blank too, with a [Trigger] chance stated outside the search from your own list when the request carries `ownDeck` (the unseen part of the list against the unseen cards; no chance is claimed when the list does not match what the view shows). Search 1 asks what the board threatens against a defender that takes every hit (`Cap.defence: 'none'`): that is `status` (`proven` = the board threatens lethal, `refuted`, `unknown` on the cap). When it threatens, search 2 uses your real hand and Blockers as the defence: `holds` says they stop it. "You must stop N of their A attacks" is A − (Life + 1) + 1 and is omitted when an attacker has [Double Attack]. Reading of the WP0 doc comment "whatever you hold": `proven` is the board threat, not "unstoppable", because "you must stop 2 of their 3 attacks" only makes sense when stopping is possible; `holds` carries the other reading. `Cap.attackerHand: 'ignored'` is the search-level move filter (it matters for real hands, as the soundness comparison uses).
- **Certainty:** `proven` is `any-hand` only when your list says no face-down Life card can be a [Trigger]; otherwise `needed-hidden-info`. `refuted` is always `needed-hidden-info` (their hand could add attackers). The copy says "This does not count the cards in their hand."
- **Contract changes (additive):** `ThreatResult` gains optional `holds`, `mustStop`, `triggerChance`; the `threat` request gains optional `ownDeck` and `nodes`; `CoachPort.threat` opts gain optional `ownDeck`; `placeholder` in `public.ts` is exported. The worker's `threat` branch calls `answerThreat`; `answerCoach` in `dispatch.ts` still holds the WP0 stub so the contract test stands.
- **Rejected:** a threat that reads the real state (actual mode) in a live game (cheating by construction); filling their hand with the prior's worst-case cards (it answers "could they", not "does the board"); a skeleton carve-out for any foreign decision with no hidden reference (the review M5a-02 failure was exactly those).

## 2026-11-25 — M7 WP1: the hint is a line from Warlord's own turn search, with a fresh memory

- **Context:** WP0 fixed `HintResult` (a line, the view actions of its first step, an E15 explanation, at most two alternatives, a certainty) and left the worker answering "not implemented". The hint must answer from the player's own view only, never touch the AI seat's session, and give one answer to one position.
- **Decision:**
  - **Search.** `hint()` (`ai/src/search/hint-line.ts`) builds a fresh `WarlordSetup` from the request alone (the player's playbook, the public archetype prior for their opponent's Leader, `k` 8) and runs Warlord's `planTurn` with a new `WarlordMemory`; `planTurn` and Pirate's `planTurn` gained an optional `out` parameter (`rootRanking`, additive; the existing tests are untouched). The **line is the most-visited path** of the tree from the root to the end of the turn, in the view's handles; its **first step** is the first macro (a play with its picks, or gives and one attack). A proven lethal (the public solver) is returned as the line. Defence decisions search whole defences (block, then counters) with Warlord's flat search and insert the picks an Event raises; choice decisions return the picks, one at a time on the guesses; mulligan and who-goes-first use Pirate's rule. Engine-touching code is under `ai/src/search/` because the lint boundary allows full states only there; `ai/src/coach/hint.ts` is the worker side (`answerHint`).
  - **Same answer twice.** The seed is an FNV hash of the view's fingerprint and its legal actions, so an iteration budget repeats exactly; on a wall-clock budget `answerHint` also remembers its last 12 answers by seed and budget.
  - **Own list.** The coach worker is given `GameInfo`; the hint uses `game.ownDeck` only when `game.leader` is the Leader the request's view shows for the asking seat, otherwise the public list for that Leader stands in for the player's deck. WP4 should send `game` (the player's deck, `tier: 'warlord'`) in the coach port's `init`, and the cards of the player's own list, or the own-deck guesses fall back to that public list.
  - **Words.** `explain-hint.ts` builds the copy from facts read off one replay of the line on a guess, but every name comes from the view's handles (a face-down or unseen card is never named) and every number is the player's own or public: the powers an attack meets, "they need 6000 - 1000 + 1000 = 6000", the DON!! left active after the end-of-turn effects, their Life and active Blockers. The outcome states its condition ("unless they spend 6000 of counters"), never "Likely". `certainty` is `search-incomplete` when the search gave fewer than 20 rollouts per first move it considered (a wall-clock search that ends before the tree is meaningful), else `any-hand`: the outcome names its condition, so it holds against any hand. A line that would name a card the player has not drawn yet stops there and ends "Then ask again".
  - **Alternatives.** The next two different first moves of the root, each with one sentence from the largest heuristic `Breakdown` term the best line wins on, worded only when the counts move the way the sentence says (never "5 Characters, not 4" for a worse line); otherwise "it ranked lower in the games the search played out".
  - **Picks are a set.** The engine takes the picks of one prompt in its own candidate order, which is by instance id and which the view hides. A run of `pick` actions in `viewActions` is therefore an unordered set: a consumer stages it by taking, at each step, whichever of them the current legal list offers (ai/test/hint.test.ts does). Logged in OPEN-QUESTIONS.
  - **E15.** The builder is general, so it does not reproduce E15's card-specific evidence (Kaido's -2000, Nami's +10000 of defence). The test ties E15's claim, its line's kinds and cards, its figures (`leaderPower`, `stopOnYamato`, `stopOnQueen`, 5 active DON!!), its concept and lesson, its condition and its certainty to the doc, and recomputes each figure through the engine.
- **Left as is:** `answerCoach` in `dispatch.ts` still stubs `hint` (WP2 and WP3 edit that file; the worker no longer calls it for hints).
- **Rejected:** a Pirate-based hint (a one-step plan, not a line through the tree); reusing the AI's `AiSession` (it holds the AI seat's plan and battle memory); naming cards from the guesses ("it is probably holding..."): never.

## 2026-11-25 — M7 plan: eight work packages, starter decks for the tutorial, the coach keeps its own worker

- **Context:** M7 (PLAN row 18, 40 h) builds the guided tutorial and the rest of the coach. A planning pass over PROMPT.md, the design docs and the L-slice code produced `docs/design/m7-plan.md`: WP0 contracts first, then WP1 hint, WP2 blunder scoring and tempo ledger, WP3 opponent's-turn threat check, WP6 tutorial in parallel, then WP4 coach panel, WP5 blunder gate, Settings and the interruption-contract test, WP7 S2 kit and review. Honest estimate about 52 h; the cut order is in the plan.
- **Decision (lead):** the tutorial uses real starter decks, **ST-21** (Monkey.D.Luffy: a `[DON!! x1] [Activate: Main]` Leader, so step t7 teaches a true Activate: Main) for the player and **ST-22** for the opponent; both are fully playable since M6b. Each step is a fixed scenario naming exact cards; the complete product decklists are an owner check on the official site (OPEN-QUESTIONS). The coach keeps its own worker holding only the player's data (DECISIONS 2026-11-05), so ARCHITECTURE §5 is amended; `H` moves focus to the hint result (L-11), so accessibility.md §3.12's `H` row is corrected. The tutorial timing log lives in `localStorage` behind a storage adapter (IndexedDB moved to M8).
- **Rejected:** reduced Rocks and Kaido lists for the tutorial (their Leaders have no Activate: Main, so t7 would teach an optional [When Attacking] cost instead); one shared worker for the AI and the coach (the coach would see the AI seat's data).

## 2026-11-25 — M6b-2 wave 8 fix round: W8-R1 lazy batch id, W8-R4 exact turn test (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.18)

- **Decision:** `fireDelayedMain` takes `d.s.nextMod++` only when an entry fires, so a Main Phase with nothing delayed consumes no modifier id: the `mod-<id>` of EB02-030's replacement (and the hash of games recorded at 0.5.18 before wave 8) is what it was (`mod-5` in the review's position). Its turn test is now `x.turn <= d.s.turn` (it runs once, as the Main Phase begins, so an entry of the same turn was made before it): an entry made in the opponent's Refresh, Draw or DON!! Phase runs at that Main Phase instead of a turn later. No scripted card makes one, so no play changes. `TIMING_WORDS` names the new timing in the order prompt.
- **Version:** unchanged, since the lazy allocation restores the pre-wave ids; goldens and the 2,000-game property run replay with the same hashes (see docs/reviews/m6b-wave8.md, Fix round).

## 2026-11-25 — M6b-2 wave 8: D2, D8, D9 (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.18)

- **Context:** DECISIONS 2026-11-23 "M6b-1 decisions" approved D2 (activate an Event from the hand or trash by an effect), D8 (a delayed effect at the start of the opponent's next Main Phase) and D9 (an effect acting on the event's subject) for wave 8 after their rulings; D1, D5 and D7 stay stats-only. Write-up `docs/research/m6b-wave8.md`; card readings `docs/research/wave8-scripting.md`.
- **Rulings (official Q&A in `.cache/track-f/qa/`, cited in the write-up and OPEN-QUESTIONS):** M6b-Q27 and Q28 settled: an Event activated by an effect from the hand is trashed, its printed cost is not paid, its written activation cost is, only its [Main] can be activated (OP12-041, OP15-046 entries), and from the hand it counts as an Event activation for watchers (OP12-041: Page One) while from the trash it does not (EB03-031). M6b-Q91 (new, closed): PRB02-005's conditions are read when played (PRB02 entry). M6b-Q11 (OP16-079) kept as read: the official entry asks about negation, which the engine already allows. New open readings M6b-Q92 (an Event whose written cost cannot be paid is not a candidate) and Q93 (the choice from a hand is always asked). The official material settled all three items; none needed more than the audit's recommendation.
- **Decision:**
  - **D2** extends `Action.activate`: `of: 'self'` as before, or `{ ability: 'main', of: Selector }` with one zone (`hand` or `trash`), your own cards, a pick of 1 and a filter that requires Events (validator `activateEvent`). From the hand it reuses the `play` frame with `free: true`; the Event branch of `runPlay` now skips the printed cost when `free`, then reveals, trashes, emits `EventActivated`, records the activation and fires "when you activate an Event" as for a played Event. From the trash it emits `EffectActivated` and runs the Event's [Main] frame with the card left in the trash. Candidates are Events with a [Main] whose written activation cost is payable now (`payable` with the Event excluded as a payment), as `eventPayable` does for a play.
  - **D8** adds `delayed: { at: 'startOfOpponentsNextMainPhase' }`: `Delayed.at` (absent for the end of turn, so nothing existing changes), a `fireDelayedMain` hook where `flow.ts` enters the Main Phase (after that phase's own `startOfMainPhase` effects, in its own batch), and the end-of-turn pass skips entries with `at`.
  - **D9** accepts `Selector.lki: 'subject'` (already in the types, refused by the validator) in an auto whose timings are all `cardPlayed`: `Ev.subject`, and `eval.ts` `poolOf` restricts the pool to that card while it is in the selector's zones and passes its filter. `fire` gives the pending effect the subject only when the ability reads it (`readsSubject`), so no other pending state, hash or record changes.
  - **Hidden information and the AI:** the Event chosen from a hand is a prompt over a secret pool (asked with an empty result too; leak cases in the D2 test file); `hidden.ts` and `hidden-gate.ts` classify it; `winsByEffect` counts an `activate` of another card's Event as unclassified.
  - **Cards scripted afterwards:** OP12-041, OP15-014, OP15-046, EB03-031, PRB02-005, OP16-079 (40 author tests); `cards:effects-index` and `cards:measure` rerun.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a new action `activateEvent` (`activate` already means "activate an ability" and its `of` field was the planned extension point); paying the printed cost when activating from the hand (the official Q&A says it is not paid); recording an Event activation for the trash version (the official Q&A says it differs from activating the card); a delayed effect that re-reads its conditions at the Main Phase (the official Q&A says it does not); a hidden `subject` binding in `ctx.b` (an implicit name; `lki: 'subject'` was already in the type); passing `ev.subject` to every cardPlayed watcher (it would put a new field in the pending state of every game with such a card, and hashes would move); auto-answering the one-class DON!! rest prompt now, so that PRB02-005 does not show it (it changes the tests and flows of OP11-024, OP11-035 and OP12-018; left to BACKLOG).
- **ENGINE_VERSION is not bumped and the goldens are not re-recorded:** every new path is reached only by the new actions, shapes and the six newly scripted cards (none is in a golden or property deck; a record holding one carries another per-game scripts hash); the selector `lki` was refused before; `Delayed.at` is set only by D8; `free` Events were unreachable (the `play` action takes Characters and Stages); the cardPlayed subject reaches no existing pending effect. The golden games and the 2,000-game property run replay with the same hashes.
- **Gates:** `npx vitest run engine cards ai` 31,275 passed, 26 expected fail, 10 skipped, 164 todo (2,985 files); `ENGINE_GAMES=2000` property and golden 32 passed (10 files); typecheck, eslint, prettier clean. New tests: 20 planted (m6b-w8), 4 bound, 4 hidden, 40 card tests.

## 2026-11-25 — Leftovers batch fix round: LF-R1 and LF-R2 (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.17 to 0.5.18)

- **Context:** the review of the 51 leftovers cards (`docs/reviews/m6b-leftovers.md`) failed on two severity 3, each against an official Q&A entry the scripts and tests had not cited.
- **LF-R1, a created replacement covers a fixed set:** official EB-02 Q&A for EB02-030: a Character played after the Counter is not covered. `addReplacement` (D6) now snapshots the cards its selector matches when the effect resolves (`Modifier.covers`, read by `collectReplacements`), so only those are offered the replacement, each one every battle K.O. of the turn (M6b-Q54 stands for that set). The snapshot is part of the modifier and so of the hash (ids become board positions, as for any modifier). Printed replacements keep their live pool. EB02-030's ruling text is corrected; `textHash` is unchanged (it hashes the card text). EB02-030 is the only script that uses `addReplacement`. Rejected: a per-Character Once Per Turn (the text has none).
- **LF-R2, delayed "at the end of this turn" effects come after every [End of Your Turn] effect:** official ST-23 to ST-28 Q&A for ST24-005. `endTriggers` (`flow.ts`) now queues each player's delayed effects in a batch after that player's own [End of Your Turn] / [End of Your Opponent's Turn] batch (turn player first, then the other, as before), so the controller no longer gets an `order` choice that puts the DON!! set first, and an effect that spends or reads active DON!! sees them before the set. The nine other scripts that use the path (EB02-015, OP08-074, OP11-092, OP11-107, OP13-024, OP13-038, OP13-066, OP14-031, OP15-025) and ST24-005 ran green unchanged; two engine tests in `engine/test/hooks/m4a10-latent.test.ts` (M4a-10b) asserted the old order (an `order` prompt between the delayed and the [End of Your Turn] effect) and were rewritten (a test error: the Q&A gives the order). Rejected: ordering the delayed effects after the autos inside one batch (the batch is one `order` prompt, so it would stay a choice).
- **ENGINE_VERSION:** bumped once, 0.5.18: both change play for cards already pushed. The three goldens were re-recorded (`UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`); their hashes are unchanged (only the version string differs), since none holds these cards. Records of other versions open read-only as before.
- **Also in this round (tests and docs only):** LF-R3 three both-suite survivors killed (OP12-024 base vs current cost, ST22-015 one Life card; OP12-028's `exactly: 1` mutant is equivalent, see BACKLOG §9), and `OWN-EFFECT.test.ts` separates "your opponent's effect" from "any effect" for the eight protections; LF-R8 `g.decider` guards on the absence assertions; LF-R4, LF-R6 and LF-R7 recorded as B3-V6 to B3-V8 and under M6b-Q25.

## 2026-11-25 — M6b-2 gaps G69 to G71: a hand-trash history condition, a given-DON!! cost, an attacker condition (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.17)

- **Context:** the leftovers audit (`docs/research/m6b-audit/leftovers.md`) found three cards no wave could express: ST33-004 Borsalino, ST28-004 Kouzuki Momonosuke, OP11-088 Shu. Write-up `docs/research/m6b-wave8a.md`; readings `docs/research/leftovers-scripting-d.md`; open questions M6b-Q87 to M6b-Q89 (already opened by the audit, unchanged).
- **Decision:**
  - **G69** is a `Condition`, `{ handTrashedThisTurn: P }`, not a new timing: the card reads a state ("during the turn in which"), and G22's `trashedFromHand` is an event for a watcher. It sits beside `koedThisTurn` and `eventsActivatedThisTurn` in the per-turn history (`History.handTrashed`, optional, recorded only in a game with a card that reads it, so every other game's state and hash are unchanged). It is recorded in `run.ts` `handTrashed()`, the single place every effect or cost trash from a hand passes through, effect and cost alike (M6b-Q31, RL-16).
  - **G70** is a cost, `{ returnGivenDon: N }`. It reuses the DON!! class prompt of DON!! -N (candidates are the cards holding given DON!!, with counts) so any split of the N is a legal answer, including several from one card; the DON!! go rested to the cost area and the `DonReturned` event says `costRested` (as a card leaving the field does). No `donReturned` watcher fires, since nothing goes to the DON!! deck.
  - **G71** is a `Condition`, `{ attackerIs: Filter }`, valid only in an auto ability whose timings are all `onOpponentAttack`, once as the ability's own condition (it narrows the trigger to a Character, so a Leader attack neither offers nor spends the Once Per Turn) and once as an effect `if` (the colon-less "If that Character has <Slash>", which spends it). It matches `s.battle.attacker` at its current attributes, so a granted attribute (G59) counts.
  - **Cards scripted afterwards:** ST33-004, ST28-004, OP11-088 (with ~22 author tests in `engine/test/cards/`). OP11-088 is `optional: true` because the text says "can be activated" (W57-R5).
  - **Checker:** `optional-cost.ts` accepts "when your opponent's Character attacks" as the printed window of an [On Your Opponent's Attack] (OP11-088); ST28-004 has no `costOptional` (the checker refuses it on an [Activate: Main]; the activation itself is the choice).
  - **Test support:** the `modifier` test op forwards `attribute`.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a timing `handTrashedThisTurn` or reusing `trashedFromHand` with a "this turn" window (a timing fires once, at the trash, and the discount must hold for the rest of the turn, including for a card drawn afterwards); a cost `moveDon` to "the cost area" (G65's action moves DON!! between cards, a cost is paid and not chosen by selector, and the destination here is not a card); extending `returnDon` with a `from: 'given'` field (it returns to the DON!! deck and fires `donReturned`, a different rule from this cost; one more mode would make its validator and `reservedActive` harder to read); an attacker `Selector` for G71 (a filter on the one attacking card is all the text asks, and a selector would suggest a pick); allowing `attackerIs` in `whenAttacking` or a permanent (the attacker is the card itself there, and a permanent has no battle to read).
- **ENGINE_VERSION is not bumped and the goldens are not re-recorded:** the new condition, cost and history field are read by no scripted card but the three new ones, none of which is in a golden deck; `tracksHistory` stays false for every other game; the golden games and the 2,000-game property run replay with the same hashes.
- **Gates:** `npx vitest run engine cards ai` 30,032 passed, 26 expected fail, 10 skipped, 164 todo (2,841 files); `ENGINE_GAMES=2000` property and golden 32 passed (10 files); typecheck, eslint, prettier clean. New tests: 17 planted (m6b-w8a), 3 bound, 6 + 7 + 8 card tests. Fail-on-revert evidence in `docs/research/m6b-wave8a.md`.


## 2026-11-25 — Waves 5 to 7 fix round, scripts part: an auto with a colon cost needs no "you may", "can be activated" autos are optional (W57-R1, W57-R5; R4 `viaOf`)

- **Context:** the review (docs/reviews/m6b-waves5-7.md) found two rules readings contradicted by the official Q&A: R1, an [On Play] whose text before the colon is a cost may be declined with or without "you may" (general-rules Q&A "play a Character card with an [On Play] effect without activating", rules-digest 112; the OP09 Q&A for Kuzan treats the placement as a cost); R5, "This effect can be activated when ..." makes the auto optional (rules-digest 112, the OP12-081 Koala Q&A: "you may decline and use it later").
- **R1 decision:** `optionalCostProblems` no longer requires "you may" before the colon of an auto with a cost (the cost is optional by construction, CR 8-3-1-4). It still refuses a script cost with no cost clause in the text, and now also refuses a colon clause with no script cost (every colon clause, "you may" or not; "choose one:" excluded), so no scripted auto can spend a declinable cost as a mandatory first step. OP09-101 is scripted with the placement as the cost. The 2026-10-29 reading (a cost without "may" is mandatory when payable) is withdrawn for autos; it stays for an Event's `costOptional`, which is the player's choice to play the card. Scan of all scripted cards: OP09-101 was the only one.
- **R5 decision:** `optional: true` on OP12-081, OP11-102, OP09-001, OP10-042, OP11-040, OP11-041 (the [Your Turn] draw), OP11-043, OP13-100, OP16-041, OP16-048 (the [Once Per Turn] Blocker); the checker requires it for any auto whose text says "can be activated" and that has no cost and no `may` step. PRB02-009 is a `may` already. A declined use does not spend the [Once Per Turn] (the engine spends it at the start of resolution after the answer). The use is asked before the effect's own `if` is read, so a use whose `if` then fails (hand size over the bound) spends the once-per-turn, as before.
- **Engine detail:** the ask stage of `runEffect` drops a queued optional auto whose [Once Per Turn] is already spent (a K.O. that is a removal and a K.O. queues two uses; accepting the first no longer asks the second).
- **R4:** `viaOf` returns `effect` for a frame of a `trigger` ability: a Character's own [Trigger] "play this card" is not "a Character's effect" (the OP12-081 Koala Q&A).
- **Alternatives rejected:** keeping "you may" required and adding a per-card exception for Kuzan (the checker would stay wrong for the unscripted cards with a bare colon cost, about 25 in OP-01 to OP-08); asking the optional use only when its `if` holds (the effect body's conditions are not readable at the ask).
- **ENGINE_VERSION:** no further bump (0.5.17 covers this fix round); the goldens hold none of these cards and were not re-recorded.

## 2026-11-25 — Waves 5 to 7 fix round, engine part: W57-R2, R3, R6, R8, R11 (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.16 to 0.5.17)

- **Context:** the waves 5 to 7 review (docs/reviews/m6b-waves5-7.md) failed on five severity 3. This part fixes the engine ones that are not the Kuzan/Koala/Camie script and checker changes (another agent has R1, R4, R5, R9, R10, R12).
- **W57-R2 (3), total power:** `selectCards` no longer floors a candidate's power at 0, and `checkPick` tests a pick for feasibility: legal while some completion (the most negative of the later candidates, up to the picks left) stays within the bound, so a -2000 and a 6000 Character fit 4000 whichever is listed first (official Q&A, OP09-018). `done` is refused, and not listed, while the picks so far are over the bound. The same rule serves `totalCost`, where a negative cost is never met. M6b-Q71 is settled by the Q&A. *Rejected:* checking the total only at the end (picks that can never finish would be offered and the AI would meet dead ends); non-canonical pick order (ADR 0003 keeps picks in candidate order).
- **W57-R3 (3), last-known information:** `lastKnown` (ops) builds the snapshot `moveCard` already took (power, base power, cost, types, DON!!, rested, cause), `runKo` and the `removed` hook pass it as `Ctx.lki`, and `matches` reads `power`, `basePower` and `cost` from it for that card. `koed` and `removed`/`leftField` filters (the only filters on a card that has left the field) now read the card as it was on the field, not its printed values in the trash. M4b-14 and M4b-2 are closed; M6b-Q17 superseded. *Rejected:* making the trash card carry its old modifiers (it would leak into every other read of the trash).
- **W57-R6 (2), K.O. source that left by a self-trash cost:** the `trashSelf` cost records where the card went and its last-known power and cost on the effect frame (`left`); a K.O. the same effect makes names that card as its source, matched by id in the trash with `lki`, so Smoker (OP11-005) is protected the same whether the source trashed itself as a cost or from its [On K.O.]. Other self-leaving costs are not covered (no scripted card has one; BACKLOG 8). *Rejected:* re-pointing `f.card` at the trash id (it would change what "this card" means for every later step).
- **W57-R8 (2), `cardsVersionOf`:** a game's card-data hash now includes `baseEffect` and `printed` of every card in it when a script of any card in that game reads them (`hasBaseEffect`, `hasTiming`); every other game hashes exactly as before. The done criterion is "an old saved record stays live after an unrelated card is scripted": that holds, because a game without such a script (every existing record) keeps its hash, and scripting a card or correcting the text of a card in another game changes nothing. A game with one of the seven text-reading cards (EB02-022, EB03-001/003/007/009/039, PRB01-001) now goes read-only if the text of any card in it is corrected, which is the point (the correction changes which cards Usopp may play). No golden or existing record holds such a card, so none moves. *Rejected:* hashing the text for every game (every record would change once, against the criterion); a one-time card-data version bump (it would invalidate all records for a field only seven cards read).
- **W57-R11 (1):** not fixed, listed in BACKLOG 8: DON!! are counts in the engine, so holding a particular DON!! needs identity for DON!!.
- **ENGINE_VERSION:** bumped once, 0.5.17. R2 changes the legal picks (and the listed actions) of a total-power choice with a negative-power Character, R3 changes when `koed` and `removed` watchers fire, R6 when a protection applies. The three goldens were re-recorded (UPDATE_GOLDEN=1); their hashes are unchanged, only the version differs, since none holds these cards. Records of other versions open read-only as before.

## 2026-11-25 — Leak property: a deck top revealed in place repeats its handle by design (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.16)

- **Context:** the standard-decks leak property failed at seed 1045752502 (PROPERTY_SEED 298648808): p1 "relinked" `$cbefi8i` by handle in `CardMoved.newInstance`. Replay: p1's OP11-073 reveals the top card of p2's deck (G44, `reveal ... player: 'opponent'`: `CardRevealed` from `deck`, fresh key, card stays on top, `markKnown` for both players); several actions later p2's OP14-081 [On Play] mills 3, the first being that card, which leaves the deck to the trash (face up, so the key is not re-rolled) with the handle it was revealed under. `State.known` still held it for both players just before the move.
- **Finding:** not a hidden-information leak. The card never left the deck or was shuffled (a move into a hidden zone, a shuffle and `ops.moveCard` out of the deck's order all re-roll the key), and the engine records the viewer as knowing which card sits at that deck position (`known` map in the view). Seeing the same handle when it is milled tells p1 only what p1 was already shown. The engine behaves as ADR 0005 states; the test tracker only knew the Life "revealed in place" exemption (M4b-09) and treated the deck top as a hidden stretch.
- **Decision:** `LinkTracker` generalises the in-place exemption (`revealedInPlace`) to a `CardRevealed` from `deck` as well as `life`: that handle only, for the very next sighting (any sighting of the card now spends it, so a later return under an old handle still fails). Planted tests in `link-tracker.test.ts`: the deck repeat passes, a shuffled-then-returned old handle fails, a never-revealed card gets no exemption; seed 1045752502 is pinned as a fast-check example in the standard leak property. Reverting the tracker change fails the pinned seed and the new tracker test.
- **origin/main (dd89651):** same engine and scripts path (G44 and both scripts are in it) but the property run `PROPERTY_SEED=298648808 ENGINE_GAMES=2000` passes there (the games diverge, the pattern is simply not sampled); the replayed seed passes too. No deployed behaviour is affected, since nothing in `engine/src` or the scripts changed.
- **Alternatives rejected:** re-rolling the key when a known deck card is milled (the handle would then differ from the reveal the viewer saw, breaking the UI's reveal-to-trash continuity for no information gain); exempting every `known` card regardless of handle (weaker: it would also let an old handle through after a re-key).
- **ENGINE_VERSION:** no bump. Only test support and tests changed; no recorded game or hash changes, goldens untouched.

## 2026-11-25 — G68: turn the top or bottom Life card face-up or face-down (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.16)

- **Context:** ST36-005 Eustass "Captain" Kid pays two costs by turning "1 card from the top or bottom of your Life cards" face-down or face-up, which the G8 cost (the top N) cannot say.
- **Decision:** extend `turnLifeFaceUp` and `turnLifeFaceDown` with `{ n: 1, from: 'top' | 'bottom' | 'topOrBottom' }` instead of a new cost. The card at the end must be in the opposite face state (M6b-Q59); "top or bottom" lets the player choose either end that can be paid, asked only when both can (a public choice; only the public face state is read). The Life card turned down is hidden under a fresh handle, the one turned up becomes public. ST36-005 is scripted with it and `redirectAttack` (D3). Reading recorded as M6b-Q86.
- **Alternatives rejected:** a separate `turnLifeFace` cost (two shapes for one rule); reading "top or bottom" as "the top, else the bottom" or as a free choice of end even when its card is already in the target state (the second would make the cost payable as a no-op, the M6b-Q59 competing reading); an `n` above 1 (no card needs it).
- **ENGINE_VERSION:** no bump. The number form is unchanged; the object form is new, used by no script until ST36-005, and no recorded game has that card, so every golden and property game replays with the same hashes.

## 2026-11-25 — M6b-2 wave 7: G59 to G67 and D3 (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.16)

- **Context:** wave 7 of the M6b-1 audit (`docs/research/m6b-wave7.md`): a timed attribute grant, the player restriction on adding Life to the hand, a Character placed into a Life area, a power cost on your Leader, "cannot be played by effects" for a card in the hand, looking at Life cards and placing them back, moving a given DON!!, a permanent negate, a stat of a selector as a `Num`, and (D3, decided 2026-11-23) the narrow `redirectAttack`. 20 cards become expressible; none is scripted.
- **Decision:**
  - **G59** extends `grant` with `attribute` beside `keyword`; the modifier is a new kind (`grantAttribute`), read through `Derived.attributes` by the `attribute` filter. A grant may give both, which is what OP15-093 says.
  - **G60** is a restriction on the player, `cannotAddLifeToHandByOwnEffects`, stored on their Leader like `cannotDraw`. It stops the controller's own Life card going to their own hand by their effect, the cost form included (M6b-Q31, RL-16), and nothing else (M6b-Q77).
  - **G61** extends `move` instead of adding an action: from `characters` to `life` with a position (`top`, `bottom`, `topOrBottom`) and a face state, as an effect, as a cost (the `move` cost, which now may name the opponent's Character when it goes to Life) and as a replacement payload. A payload names the replaced card with `{ ref: 'replaced' }`, which `applyReplacement` binds only when the payload reads it. The move is a removal frame with a new destination `life`, so protection and replacements see it; a cost counts as an effect for "cannot be removed by your opponent's effects". A face-down card in Life is known to both players (they saw it leave the field).
  - **G62** is a cost `power { target, amount, duration }` narrowed to your own Leader (one card, a fixed amount); the payment is the modifier, and the selector must match (an active Leader).
  - **G63** is `cannotBePlayedByEffects`, a card restriction that only a hand-zone permanent on its own card may carry; the effect `play` drops such a card from its candidates. The layers already computed hand-zone permanents.
  - **G64** is one action, `lookAtLife`, in two shapes (look at one card of either Life and place it at the top or bottom; look at all of yours and order them), reusing the `look` list, `markKnown` and the `order` decision. The cards never leave Life (two small ops, `placeLife` and `orderLife`, so no `lifeRemoved` fires) and keep their face state (rules-digest 108; M6b-Q81).
  - **G65** is an action, `moveDon { n, from, to }`, over your Leader and Characters; nothing about the DON!! changes but the card that carries them.
  - **G66** is a continuous `negate { target }` applied at the first stage of the layers: `derive.ts` reads every permanent negation once per memo and adds a `negate` modifier to the cards it names, so everything that reads a card's negation (`negated`, `isNegated`, the permanents, the replacements, the autos) sees it. A source never negates itself; filters read printed values only (the validator uses the deck-filter keys), which also keeps the reading from recursing.
  - **G67** adds two `Num`s: `{ statOf: Selector, stat }` for the one card a zone names (the Leader or this card, no pick) and `{ totalCost: Selector }` for the sum of current costs.
  - **D3** is an action, `redirectAttack { to }`, valid only in an auto ability on `onOpponentAttack`, over your Leader and Characters (active or rested). It rewrites `battle.target` and its key, emits `AttackRedirected` and fires nothing again: the declaration's timings are read once, so the Block Step that follows is the one for the new target. The Q&A read, and the reading chosen where they are silent, are in M6b-Q85.
  - The lethal bound: every new op and form is unclassified (searched) except that the ops that change no Life count and decide nothing are `LIFE_SAFE_OPS`; a move into Life counts as changing Life.
  - **ENGINE_VERSION is not bumped and the goldens are not re-recorded:** no scripted card uses a new shape, and the changes to paths scripted cards run are invisible to them (see the wave write-up); the golden games and a 2,000-game property run replay with identical hashes.
- **Rejected:**
  - A new action for "place a Character into Life": `move` already says "from, to, which end, face state"; the validator keeps the combinations that exist.
  - A new action `lookAtLife` per shape, or reusing `lookAtTop` with a Life zone: `lookAtTop` takes cards from a deck and moves them; here nothing leaves the area and the cards keep their face state.
  - Moving a looked-at Life card with `moveCard` (as `lookAtTop` does for a deck): it would fire `lifeRemoved` and re-roll the card's handle for a card that never left.
  - A permanent negate as a stage after the others: a negated source must produce nothing, so the negation has to be read first.
  - Reading `byOpponentEffect` as effect-only: a cost of the opponent's that moves your Character would then slip past "cannot be removed by your opponent's effects" (RL-16).
  - A `Num` `statOf` over any selector with a pick: a `Num` cannot ask; the Leader and this card are the one-card zones.
  - Having [On Your Opponent's Attack] fire again for the redirect's new target (the audit's proposal, M6b-Q10): the timing is the attack's, not the target's.
  - Building ST36-005 (a cost that turns the top or bottom Life card): not in the audit's gaps, listed in the write-up.
  - Settling M6b-Q76 to Q85 now.

## 2026-11-25 — M6b-2 wave 6: G49 to G58 (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.16)

- **Context:** wave 6 of the M6b-1 audit (`docs/research/m6b-wave6.md`): filters on printed text, "played rested", effects the opponent performs, a place-this-card cost, play of a revealed top card, `sameNameAs`, `Pick.totalPower`, the `triggerActivated` timing, "add this card to your hand" in a [Trigger] and the variable-count hand trash. 22 cards become expressible; none is scripted.
- **Decision:**
  - **G49** is two filters read from the verified card text, not from the script (`cards/src/printed.ts`, `Def.baseEffect`, `Def.printed`): `hasBaseEffect` (keywords and reminder text are not an effect, M6b-Q29; a [Trigger] is, M6b-Q70) and `hasTiming` over the eight printed timings. A bracket followed by "effect" is a reference, not a printed timing. The two `Def` fields are left out of `cardsVersionOf`, so no recorded game's card-data version moves.
  - **G50** is a continuous `playRested: Filter` read by the play when it places the card (`playsRested`), not a layer.
  - **G51** extends two actions instead of adding new ones: `play` and `addDon` take `player: 'opponent'`. The opponent's choice and the "may" are the existing selector `chooser` and `chooseOne { chooser: 'opponent' }`. The validator ties `play`'s `player` to their hand, owner and chooser.
  - **G52** is the existing field cost (G1) with `from: 'self'` accepted: the card leaves through a `remove` frame with the cause `cost`. "In any order" with another card (OP10-026, OP10-027) is two costs in the order written (M6b-Q73).
  - **G53** is the existing `play` reading `deckTop` or `lifeTop` with a `{ ref }` filter naming a `reveal`; the validator accepts the top of a deck or Life only in that form. A pool that is only a bound top card is not secret (`hiddenPool`), so no extra question is asked. A Life card played fires `lifeRemoved` (M6b-Q40).
  - **G54** is a filter `sameNameAs: Name`; cost selectors already bind, so no compile change was needed.
  - **G55** adds `totalPower: { lte: Num }` beside `totalCost` on an `upTo` pick, through the same `Decision.limits` (`what: 'power'`) and `checkPick` (M6b-Q71).
  - **G56** is a timing `{ event: 'triggerActivated', by: P | 'any' }` fired in `answerTrigger`, after the revealed [Trigger] is announced and before it resolves, so the watchers queue behind it (M6b-Q72). `any` is for OP13-106, which names no player.
  - **G57** is `move` of `self` to your hand, allowed only in a [Trigger]; the card in the `resolving` slot goes to the hand and `runTrigger` already skips the trash.
  - **G58** needed no change: the text has no colon, so it is an effect, and `trashFromHand` with `pick: { any: true }`, `bind` and `refCount` (OP15-002) already reads it. The audit's gap came from reading the trash as a cost. A test pins the shape.
  - The lethal bound: `play` and `addDon` by the opponent and the permanent `playRested` are unclassified (searched); a cost that moves this card counts in `bouncesOf`.
  - **ENGINE_VERSION is not bumped and the goldens are not re-recorded:** no scripted card uses a new shape, and the changes to paths scripted cards run are invisible to them (see the wave write-up); the golden games and a 2,000-game property run replay with identical hashes.
- **Rejected:**
  - Reading `hasBaseEffect` from `Def.abilities`: an unscripted card has none, so the filter would call every unscripted card "no base effect" (audit section 5.2).
  - A new action for "your opponent plays" and one for "your opponent adds DON!!": a `player` field on the two actions reuses their frames, and the opponent's choice is already expressible.
  - A new `place` cost for this card: the field `move` cost already carries the removal frame, the cause and the checks; only `self` was missing.
  - A new action "play the revealed card": the `play` action and a `{ ref }` filter say it, and the validator keeps the top of a deck or Life playable only in that form.
  - Asking the order of the two Kin'emon cards placed "in any order": it adds a decision for no observable difference (same name, hidden deck).
  - Changing the card-data version for the new `Def` fields: it would have invalidated every golden for a derived value.
  - A `Pick` of its own for G58 or a cost with a variable count: the card is not a cost.
  - Settling M6b-Q70 to Q75 now.

## 2026-11-25 — M6b-2 wave 5: G39 to G48 (ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.16)

- **Context:** wave 5 of the M6b-1 audit (`docs/research/m6b-wave5.md`): `delayed` steps that remember bound cards, `donReturned` qualifiers, Life face state, `returnDon` with a variable count, `damageTaken`, "choose a cost" with a reveal of the opponent's deck top, a move from the top or bottom of Life to the hand, K.O. protection by source, `cannotAttackTarget`, and `skipRefresh` for DON!!. 25 cards become expressible; none is scripted.
- **Decision:**
  - **G39** is a runtime fix with no new DSL: `Delayed` and `PendingEffect` carry the frame's `b` and `pushEffect` restores it. A reference to a card that has since left the field finds nothing (M6b-Q38).
  - **G40** extends the `donReturned` timing (`min`, `cause` with the three values `removed` uses) and replaces `fireEvent` by `fireDonReturned`: one event per returning instruction with the whole count, both fields' watchers, `owner` read. A cost counts as part of the effect (RL-16, M6b-Q31), so EB03-033's "by your effect" includes DON!! −N (M6b-Q65).
  - **G41** extends the Action `turnLifeFaceDown` (`'all'`, the cost form keeps its number) and adds the `Num` `lifeFaceUp`. A count of face-up cards is a `cmp`, so no new Condition.
  - **G42** is a cost form `{ returnDon: { atLeast: n } }`: a count decision (fewest first), then the existing class choice; not allowed in a replacement payload, which cannot ask.
  - **G43** counts the Life cards a damage frame takes and fires when it ends with any: after the [Trigger] resolved, once per damage (M6b-Q66).
  - **G44** is three small parts instead of one fused action: `chooseNumber` (a `mode` choice stored in the frame's numeric bindings and announced with the existing `ModeChosen`), `reveal` with `player: 'opponent'` (shows the top card to both and marks it known), and a `Num` `chosen`; the comparison is the existing `ref`/`matches` condition with `baseCost: { eq: { chosen } }`. No new decision kind and no new event type (M6b-Q67).
  - **G45** is an ordinary `move` from `lifeTopOrBottom` to your hand, with `exactly: 1`; the end is a blind choice shared with the `lifeToHand` cost.
  - **G46** adds `by?: Filter` to the scoped `cannotBeKOd` restriction. The K.O. source travels on the `ko` frame (`src`), not in the `Cause`, because the cause is copied into events and a raw instance id must not reach a view. A filtered protection is kept apart from the bit mask (`Derived.koBy`); a source that is gone matches nothing (M6b-Q68). Filters that read anything relative to the two cards are refused.
  - **G47** adds the restriction `cannotAttackTarget: Filter` (timed and permanent) as a modifier read by `checkAttack` through `Derived.cannotAttackTarget`.
  - **G48** reuses `skipRefresh` with a DON!! selector (`donCostArea`, `{ state: 'rested' }`, a count): a `skipRefreshDon` modifier on the Leader holds `amount` rested DON!! through the Refresh Phase. "Up to n" holds as many as there are (E-D4, DON!! are interchangeable). M6b-Q69.
  - The lethal bound needed two numbers (`lifeFaceUp`, `chosen`); every new action and restriction form is unclassified there, so the solver searches.
  - **ENGINE_VERSION is not bumped and the goldens are not re-recorded:** no scripted card uses a new shape, `donReturned` already had only `owner: 'you'` watchers, and no existing delayed body reads a binding; the golden games and a 2,000-game property run replay with identical hashes.
- **Rejected:**
  - A fused `revealOpponentTopAndCompare` action: it fits six cards and nothing else; the three parts are each reusable (`chooseNumber` for any "choose a number").
  - A new decision kind and event type for "choose a cost": the option choice (as every DON!! count) and `ModeChosen` already carry them to the UI, the log and the AI.
  - Putting the K.O. source in `Cause`: events and last-known snapshots carry the cause, and an instance id there would reach a projected view.
  - Folding the `by` filter into the `koShield` bit mask: a bit cannot hold a filter.
  - Excluding costs from "by your effect" (the audit's reading of EB03-033): it contradicts the Q31 decision and RL-16 for every other cause; one flag changes it if a ruling says so.
  - A `Condition` `lifeFaceUp`: a `Num` serves the two cards and composes with `cmp`.
  - Choosing which rested DON!! are held back: they are interchangeable, so it would ask a question with one answer.
  - Settling M6b-Q65 to Q69 now.

## 2026-11-25 — An `attack` timing that names both attacker and target needs both (OP12-081; no ENGINE_VERSION change)

- **Context:** BACKLOG §5: `attackMatch` (engine/src/flow.ts) checked only the selector of the side that watches the event, so "When this Leader attacks your opponent's Leader" (OP12-081 Koala) also fired on an attack on a Character.
- **Decision:** when a timing gives both `attacker` and `target`, both must match the battle. OP12-081 is scripted (its draw is an effect-level `if`, the colon-less rule). Planted test from both the attacking and the attacked side; both it and OP12-081's "attacks a Character" test fail on the old code. No existing script names both sides in one timing (OP17-040 uses two timings), so no recorded game changes and ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.16.
- **Rejected:** a new timing kind for Leader-on-Leader attacks (the selector pair already says it).

## 2026-11-25 — Block 3 batch B: a quoted type in the card data is an exact type; a failed [Trigger] goes to the trash; batches run in parallel

- **Context:** the batch B blind tests raised two disagreements (EB02-044 Sengoku `black "Navy" type`, OP09-095 Laffitte `"Blackbeard Pirates" type card`) by reading a quoted type as a substring match (rules-digest 27, CR 2-4-3-1). Separately, OP10-100's notes said an activated [Trigger] whose colon-less "If" fails goes to the hand, while the OP-09 scripts and the engine send it to the trash.
- **Decision (lead, confirmed by review B3C-R3 and B3B-R6):** in optcgapi text, `"X" type` is the official `{X} type` (exact): data-sources rows 43 and 54 show the rendering, the substring form is always printed `type including "X"` (87 times against 295), and OP09-044/046 print both forms in one sentence. Scripts keep exact types; the two tests now assert a substring-only card is not offered. An activated [Trigger] goes to the trash after resolving whether or not its "If" holds; only a declined Trigger goes to the hand. OP10-100's notes were corrected; no engine change.
- **Process:** batch B's scripting ran while batch A was under review, and both batches went through one gate and one push after both fix rounds. Rejected: pushing batch A alone with batch B's unreviewed scripts on the same local `main`.
- **Rejected:** treating the quoted form as substring (would widen 295 filters against the official text); an engine change for the Trigger destination (it was already right).

## 2026-11-25 — Block 3 batch A fix round: the AI leaves out an activation that changes nothing; a hand reveal is announced; OP10-098 asks the cost-4 pick first (B3B-R1 to R3; no ENGINE_VERSION change)

- **Context:** the batch A review (`docs/reviews/m6b-block3-A.md`) failed on B3B-R1 (severity 3): OP10-030 Smoker and EB04-016 have an unlimited [Activate: Main] with no cost and no Once Per Turn ("set up to 1 DON!! as active, then you cannot set DON!! as active using Character effects"). Once the restriction is in place every repeat does nothing, but it stays legal; the Pirate ranks `activate` above `endTurn` (`structural` 2.5 against -1 in `ai/src/search/mcts.ts`), the state does not change, the value ties, and the AI activated it until the 2,000-action cap (seed 5044 of the review's batch decks reproduces it; no other layer caps repeats).
- **Decision (B3B-R1):** `isNoOpActivation` (`ai/src/search/sim.ts`) is the filter, applied in both candidate generators (`mainCandidates` for Pirate, `treeCandidates` for Warlord and Emperor, so all three search tiers). An `activate` is left out of the candidates when **every answer** to the prompts it raises (a bounded search: 40 leaves, 4 prompts deep, 8 answers wide; beyond that it is kept) ends in a state with the same **semantic hash as before, reading modifiers as a set**. The set reading is a new optional argument, `summaryHash(state, dedupeMods = false)` in `engine/src/hash.ts` (default unchanged, so goldens and records are untouched): the plain hash differs after every Smoker repeat because the identical restriction is added again, so the plain hash cannot tell a no-op. A rule-neutral test, not a card list: any ability that spends a cost, moves a card, changes a counter or power, adds a modifier that is new, or is answered differently by a prompt is kept; only a repeat that provably changes nothing is dropped. `endTurn` stays in the list, so ending the turn is always available. A guard test (`ai/test/noop-activation.test.ts`) runs the planted position through all three search tiers and the seed-5044 game (Pirate; Warlord too) and fails with the filter off.
- **Also checked:** the Warlord and Emperor move lists get the same filter, since their trees share `treeCandidates`; Rookie picks uniformly at random among the legal actions, so it is not in the loop (not tested here).
- **B3B-R3:** a Selector gets `reveal?: true` (DSL, validator: only `{ from: 'hand', owner: 'you' }`; runtime: `select()` emits `CardRevealed` with reason `effect` for each chosen card once the choice is made, nothing while it waits or when declined). OP10-119 and OP10-058 set it; both `it.fails` are plain tests. The review also suggested marking the cards known until the effect ends; not done: the cost `reveal` does not either, and the `CardRevealed` event is what the opponent's view and the log use.
- **ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.16.** The only change that reaches a recorded game is the new `CardRevealed` event for a hand reveal, which exists only for the two scripts that set the flag; no golden game contains OP10-119 or OP10-058 (the golden suite passes with no re-recording), and no rule or random draw changes. The `summaryHash` argument defaults to the old behaviour. Rejected: bumping the version and re-recording all goldens for a change none of them can see.
- **B3B-R2:** OP10-098 asks the base-cost-4 pick first, then the base-cost-6 pick. Reasoning checked in play: with the 6 pick first, choosing a cost-4 card as the "6 or less" K.O. left the second pick with no candidate (one K.O. instead of two); with the 4 pick first, whichever card is chosen the 6-or-less pick can still take any other card, so every pair the text allows (one at 6 or less, one at 4 or less, two different cards) stays reachable and no click loses a K.O. Both author and independent tests follow.
- **Rejected for B3B-R1:** breaking ties toward `endTurn` in the search (a ranking tweak; any new unlimited repeat that does change state, or a tier that ranks differently, would loop again); a cap on repeats of one ability per turn (a number with no basis in the rules, and a legitimate repeatable ability exists); an engine-level repeat cap (the loop is a decision-making fault of the AI, a human may repeat as often as they like); filtering by card id or by the script shape (misses the next such card).

## 2026-11-24 — A colon-less "If" is an effect condition, everywhere (OP-11/OP-12 review, B3A-R1)

- **Context:** OP11-041 Nami's "[Once Per Turn] When ... , if you have 7 or less cards in your hand, draw 1" spends its Once Per Turn when the hand check fails, while OP11-036, 075 and 076 ("[On Play] If ..." with no colon) were scripted with ability-level `conditions`, which do not.
- **Decision (lead):** an "If" with no colon after it is an effect condition (an `if` step): the ability triggers or is activated, its Once Per Turn is spent, and nothing happens when the check fails. Only an "If" before a cost colon is an activation condition. OP11-036/075/076 now use effect-level `if`, matching EB04-012, EB03-013 and Nami; tests pin it.
- **Rejected:** keeping both encodings (inconsistent Once Per Turn behaviour between cards with the same wording).
- **Also recorded:** M6b-Q59 — "turn N Life cards face-down" needs a face-up top card and "face-up" a face-down one (the engine's reading; no official ruling for the face-down mirror); cards listed there.
