- **Context:** the owner asked for a checkpoint. This entry lists every decision still open, so a resumed session or the owner can find them in one place. Decisions already taken are in their own entries below.
- **Taken by the lead in this stretch and recorded elsewhere:** M6b-Q31 cost half (a cost counts as "trashed from your hand by an effect"); "power the same as your opponent's Leader" reads current power (EB04-052, matching OP16-036); "set up to N DON!! as active" asks a count; "set the power to 0" lowers by current power (`targetPower`); a "Then" after a failed "If" does not resolve (M4b-5, applied to OP13-102/108); the AI lethal gate counts wins by effect. One more, recorded here: **ST22-011** was verified with the Limitless text, which has the cost clause "You may reveal 2 cards with a type including "Whitebeard Pirates" from your hand:" that optcgapi drops; treated as a dropped clause (a clear upstream defect), not a reading. Rejected: leaving it unverified as an open question (the clause is plainly part of the card and the rest of the text matches).
- **Open, waiting on the owner (official card list):** EB05-031 cost; B4-V1 to B4-V7; B3-V1 to B3-V4 (OPEN-QUESTIONS). Each card stays unscripted until read.
- **Open, waiting on the lead (design items, wave 8):** D1 rules-changing Leaders (OP13-003, OP13-079, OP15-022); D2 activating an Event from hand or trash (OP15-014, OP15-046, EB03-031); D5 whole-side [On Play] negation; D7 a watcher registered by an Activate; D8 a delayed effect at the opponent's next Main Phase; D9 an effect acting on the event's subject (OP16-079). D3 (`redirectAttack`, OP16-080, ST36-005) is in wave 7. None is decided; each card stays unscripted until it is.

See docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md.

Two lead calls added: the optional-cost checker also recognises an untagged "This effect can be activated at the start of your turn" unit (OP11-040), as it already did for the untagged opponent-attack and K.O. forms (rejected: an allowlist entry per card); and the meta-card verification test gets a 30 s timeout (rejected: excluding it under load). Readings flagged for the OP-11/OP-12 review (not decisions yet): OP11-034 Hatchan's full `cannotBeRested`, OP11-041 Nami's Once Per Turn spent with 8+ cards in hand, OP11-043 Ichiji's GERMA condition as an activation condition, OP12-016 Rayleigh's bound "card given these DON!!", OP12-017 one card in total, OP12-040 Kuzan `includeCosts` (follows M6b-Q31), OP12-096 (M6b-Q8), OP12-118 (M6b-Q25).

## 2026-11-24 — Wave 4 fix round: the lethal solver's attacker-count gate lets an effect win through (AI only, no ENGINE_VERSION change)

- **Context:** the wave 4 review (`docs/reviews/m6b-wave4.md`, W4-R1, severity 3) showed that `attackersPossible` refuted a seat before `scanAbility` or the search ran, so a win by `damage`, `winGame`, an opponent Life move or any other unclassified effect was reported "refuted, any hand" (a proof claim that was false). "Unclassified means search" held only inside `scanAbility`.
- **Decision:** `attackersPossible` returns true when the attack count passes **or** `effectReach` finds a card the seat can reach that `winsByEffect` flags. `winsByEffect(cardId)` (`ai/src/search/lethal/bound.ts`, memoised, reads typed scripts only) is true when any [Activate], auto or replacement ability (not a [Trigger], not a permanent) holds a `damage`, `winGame`, `trashLifeUntil`, a `move` from `lifeTop` / `lifeTopOrBottom` / `life` (or an unknown source form), an opponent `draw`, a `mill` not aimed only at the controller, or **any op not on a short list of ops known not to touch Life, deck-out or the winner** (so a future op is searched until classified). Cost, timing and Once Per Turn are ignored: it can only err toward searching. `effectReach` looks at the hand, Leader, Characters and Stage, the trash when a reachable card plays or moves from there, and the deck when it is known (`deckKnown`) and a card can bring cards from it: the same reach as `attackReach`. The `refuted` contract is unchanged: it is still only given when the gate or the search proves it. The search and `refutedByBound` already treat these shapes as unclassified (`attackerPotential` null), as the review confirmed.
- **Cost:** a seat whose reachable cards hold such an effect now gets a search where it got an instant refutation; `ai/test/budget.test.ts` still holds (run alone), and the lethal suites are green.
- **Rejected:** counting effect damage in the reach bound (a number per effect, and every future op would need one); gating on the attack count only for seats with no scripts (would not cover a Leader's own effect).
- **Probe result worth knowing:** the review named OP16-116, EB03-053 and EB03-057 as shapes in the gate; OP16-116 and EB03-053 also hold a `play`, which `reachOf` already counts as an extra attack, so the old gate passed them in the positions tried here. EB03-057 is the real-card case the new check covers by itself (pinned by `winsByEffect`).

## 2026-11-24 — Block 4 tail fix round: "set the power to 0", "same as the Leader" and the test kits (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.16)

- **Context:** the block 4 tail review (`docs/reviews/m6b-block4-tail.md`) failed on B4T-R1: EB04-010 scripted "Set the power of up to 1 of your opponent's Characters to 0" as `setBasePower 0`, which leaves power the target already has (an earlier buff).
- **Decision:**
  - B4T-R1: a new Num `{ targetPower: true }` is the current power of the card a `power` or `cost` action applies to (read once per target, floored at 0). "Set the power to 0" is `power` with `amount: { mul: [{ targetPower: true }, -1] }` (CR 4-12-1, rules-digest 35: it lowers by the current power; a power already at 0 or below is left alone, CR 4-12-2). Validator: only inside a power or cost amount, like `donGivenToTarget`. Lethal bound: 20,000. No new action. A `setBasePower` grep judged every script against its text: only EB04-010 says "the power ... to 0"; ST34-004 says "base power becomes 0" and stays a base-power set, as do the other "base power becomes N" scripts. Given DON!! on an opposing target add no power on the active player's turn, so only buffs matter for EB04-010 (the review's given-DON!! probe was in a position where they add nothing).
  - B4T-R2 (lead decision, closes M6b-Q29 and OP-16-Q2): "base power becomes the same as your opponent's Leader" reads the Leader's current power, as OP16-036 / OP16-055 / OP16-104 already did. EB04-052 `refStat` changed from `basePower` to `power`; its tests flip. EB01-061, OP04-069 and OP06-009 (same wording, unscripted) follow the same reading when scripted.
  - B4T-R3: the kit helpers `choose`, `payWith` (kit.ts), `pickInOrder`/`restCost`, `pickByCardIds`/`payHand`/`pickInst` (op17/kit-op17b.ts), `pickMixed`/`drive` (m4b/kit-b.ts) and `pickTargets` (st29/kit-st29a.ts) send `done` only if the pending decision is the one they were answering (`stillSameDecision`: same kind, prompt, purpose, source, and remaining min and max reduced by the picks made). Tests that relied on the swallowing now answer the next empty pick explicitly (OP12-015, OP14-116, OP17-085, OP17-092). The EB04-015 Jinbe `it.fails` is a plain test with raw picks; DISAGREEMENTS-a B-1 is closed. `payCost` (op16) and `mayPayHand` (st-b5) only send `done` to a `cost` choose and were left.
  - B4T-R4: EB04-007 has no colon before "If your opponent has a Character with 8000 power or more", so it is part of the effect: an `if` step, not an activation condition. The Activate is always offered and spends Once Per Turn.
- **Rejected:** a `setPower` action (a Num reuses `power`, its duration, layers and expiry); clamping to 0 inside the action (the Num floors, so a negative power is left alone); keeping the base-power reading for EB04-052 (one wording, two readings).
- Goldens re-recorded for the version string only.

## 2026-11-24 — M6b-2 wave 4: G30 to G38, D6, G14 (b) and two backlog items (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.15)

- **Context:** wave 4 of the M6b-1 audit (`docs/research/m6b-wave4.md`): Life to the trash, looking at the opponent's deck, "can also attack active Characters", base-power swap, `donGiven`, `winGame`, `damage`, a replacement for being rested, `koed` for the opponent, D6 (an Event creates a replacement), one replacement over two events, and two engine gaps found while scripting (`[DON!! xN] [On K.O.]`, `play` from `self`).
- **Decision:**
  - A replacement an effect creates is a `replacement` modifier on the controller's Leader that holds the ability as `mod-<id>`. `abilityOf` resolves that id and `collectReplacements` reads `state.mods`, so the offer, the answer, the payload frame and the events are the printed-replacement code.
  - A rest by an effect goes through a `rest` frame only for a card that has a replacement for it; every other rest is immediate, so no game without such a card changes.
  - `replaces` may be a list: one ability, one `considered` key, one Once Per Turn. M6b-Q6 stays open; both answers are expressible (a list, or two abilities).
  - `[DON!! xN] [On K.O.]`: `ev.don` is set only when the K.O.'d card has an `onKO` auto with a `donGiven` condition, so other games' pending effects and hashes are unchanged.
  - `from: 'self'` plays are checked at run time: `resolving` for a [Trigger], the trash for anything else.
  - Looking at the opponent's deck is `lookAtTop` with `player: 'opponent'`, no take and `rest: 'stay'`; the validator refuses every other combination.
  - `damage` is the battle damage frame (a Trigger decision per Life card); `winGame` ends the game at once (M6b-Q50); `swapBasePower` takes two selectors so a Leader and a Character can be swapped (OP14-009).
  - The lethal bound needed one line (`donGivenToTarget` is at most 10); every new action is unclassified there, so the solver searches.
- **Rejected:**
  - A `Keyword` for "can also attack active Characters": the schema's keyword list is printed keywords and card data; a modifier kind keeps it out of the card data.
  - A single `swapBasePower` selector with `exactly: 2`: it cannot say "your Leader and 1 Character" (OP14-009).
  - Storing the created replacement in a new state field: the modifier already has the duration, the expiry order and the hash.
  - Routing every effect rest through a frame: it adds a stack step to every game.
  - Keeping `ev.don` on every K.O.: it changes every pending effect's hash.
  - Settling M6b-Q6, Q50 to Q55 now.
- Goldens re-recorded for the version string only.

## 2026-11-24 — OP-13 fix round: `cannotPlay` scoped to the hand; "Then" inside failed "If" (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.14)

- **OP13-R3 (sev 2).** `restrict { cannotPlay: Filter, from?: 'hand' }`: with `from: 'hand'` the bar applies only while the card is in the hand (`playBlocked` reads the card's zone; the modifier carries `from`). The validator accepts only `hand` and rejects any other `from`. OP13-028 Shanks ("you cannot play cards from your hand during this turn") uses it, so a same-turn play from the trash or deck by an effect (OP13-082 Five Elders, probed by the review) is no longer barred. Every other `cannotPlay` script (OP13-023, 118, OP14-024, EB05-021, OP17-085, 092) has no zone in its text and keeps the any-means bar. Settles M6b-Q33. Planted `engine/test/cards/m4b/actions.test.ts` ("cannot play cards from your hand"): the hand barred and the trash play allowed with the scope, both barred without it, validator rejects `from: 'trash'`; with the zone check removed the first test fails.
- **OP13-R1/R2 (sev 3), script-only.** OP13-102 Edison: the rest step is inside the `then` of the life comparison. OP13-108 Jewelry Bonney: the opponent's Life add is inside the `then` of the {Egghead} `if` (M4b-5: a "Then" after a failed "If" does not resolve). Author tests flipped, independent `it.fails` D-1/D-2 are plain tests; all four fail on the old scripts. Already-merged behaviour change: OP13-102 and OP13-108 only (Edison no longer rests a Character when the "If" fails; Bonney's opponent keeps their Life without {Egghead}).
- **OP13-R6.** The OP13-050/052 `it.fails` (B-1) are plain tests asserting the empty pick is asked.
- **Independent tests** of OP13-024, 034, 035, 038 answer the OP14-R2 count prompt (the 18 failures on local main); each card has a fewer/None test.
- **Not changed:** OP13-066 stays on the open M6b-Q34 reading (OP13-R4). OP13-R5 (a fixed-target `giveDon each` cannot give 0 to a target) is in BACKLOG section 6 with the reasoning: a None option per target costs a prompt per target (OP14-105 has up to six). OP13-081 Koala can already decline (test added).
- **Rejected:** a `from` zone list on `cannotPlay` (only the hand is named in any card text); making the zone check part of `Filter` (it would also change `matches` for selectors, where `from` already means the pool).
- Goldens re-recorded for the version string only.

## 2026-11-24 — A card being played is public mid-play, and its hand slot is not (OP15-R7; no ENGINE_VERSION change)

Context: `projectForSearch`'s skeleton treated a card on a `play` frame as known (`seenBy`, CR 2-7-2) while the view hid it (still zone `hand`), so at a "Character area is full" prompt equal opponent views gave different skeletons (skeleton-diff "life mode", seed 222, step 74; nightly 10,000 games would hit it). Decision: the view reveals it. `visibleTo` (hence `canSee`, `seenBy`, `actionFromView`) is true for a card on a `play` frame past its `pay` step (`beingPlayed`), since `pay` emits `CardRevealed` for it; a queued batch play not yet at `pay` stays hidden. `seenBy` lost its separate `play` rule and `leaks.test.ts` `hiddenFrom` its own `playing` set: both now go through `canSee`, so the leak property no longer permutes that card (it is public) and still permutes every other hidden card. Revealing the identity must not reveal the slot (ADR 0005, "the position of a card in the opponent's hand"): `viewOrder` lists the other player's hand with the played card first and numbers the placeholders `p1.hand.0..` without a gap, in the view and in the skeleton alike (the skeleton's `hidden` keys and hand list use it, so the determinizer's `fill` keys match the view and it never resamples the played card: it is in `seen`). The UI shows the opponent's hand as card backs and a count only, so nothing changes there. Planted `engine/test/play-reveal.test.ts`: positions differing only in the played card give different views and skeletons; differing only in an unrelated hand card give identical ones; the played card's original slot (first, middle, last) gives identical ones. With the `visibleTo` reveal reverted 2 of 3 fail; with the slot normalisation reverted 1 of 3 fails. The 10,000-game run then found a sibling of the same shape (seed 948, viewer p1): a Life card the viewer knows (`s.known`) counted as seen in the skeleton (`canSee`) but the view's `known` map listed only deck positions, so equal views gave different skeletons; `known` now covers deck, Life and hand placeholders (a positional handle each), planted in the same test file (fails with it reverted). `leaks.test.ts` link tracker now maps view slots through `viewOrder`. Engine play is unchanged: no version bump, goldens untouched. Rejected: dropping the card from the skeleton's known set (the AI would then resample a card the rules show, and the view would still hide a declared card); revealing it at its real index (leaks the hand slot); exempting it in the leak property while leaving the view hidden (the mismatch stays).

## 2026-11-24 — OP-14 fix round: opponent's-End-Phase duration, "set up to N" count, cost trash counts, [Trigger] conditions (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.13)

- **R1 (OP14-R1, sev 3).** "Until the end of your opponent's next End Phase" (`untilEndOfOpponentsNextTurn`) created on the opponent's own turn now ends at that turn's End Phase (RL-11: the effect's owner is the non-turn player then). `duration()` records `after: turn - 1` when the effect's controller is not the active player, `after: turn` otherwise; `flow.ts` is unchanged. Created on your own turn it still lasts through the opponent's next turn. OP14-082 Oinkchuck's manual `if opponentsTurn` split is gone (plain duration). Every other script with this duration was checked (OP14-029, 033, 098, 119, 120, OP15-019, 074, 097, EB05-009, 059): all create it on the owner's own turn, so none changes. Only OP14-111 Perona's [On K.O.] on the opponent's turn changes (it was wrong).
- **R2 (OP14-R2, sev 2).** `setActive` of DON!! with an `upTo` pick asks a count (a `mode` choice, most first, "None" last) like `addDon`, whenever at least 1 rested DON!! could be set; an exact set and a case with no rested DON!! ask nothing. Reverses the E-Q11 note "set active is unchanged": there are counter-examples where fewer is better (OP07-023 Caribou, OP13-001 Luffy, the whole "give rested DON!!" family). Scripts are unchanged (they already say `upTo`); the author and independent tests answer the prompt, one new test per card declines or sets fewer. OP14-024 A-1 is resolved for the independent author.
- **R3 (OP14-R3, sev 2; lead decision)** A cost is part of the effect (rules-digest 112, CR 8-1-1), so "a card is trashed from your hand by an effect" includes a card trashed as a cost: OP14-045, 049, 056 now have `includeCosts: true`. This settles the cost half of M6b-Q31 (OP12-040 and ST15-001 are unscripted); the open-question note for Q46 (one event per instruction or per effect) is unchanged.
- **R4 (OP14-R4, sev 1).** No engine change was needed: `giveDon` with n 1 and an exact target already asks the target as a min-0 pick (`done` is legal), so "give up to 1 rested DON!!" (OP14-005, 114) can be declined; the review missed the `done` action. Tests pin it. Consistent with E-Q11: a count prompt is asked only when the count has more than one value, and the min-0 target pick covers the one-DON!! case.
- **Also:** a [Trigger] ability's `conditions` are now evaluated when it resolves, as for `auto` (BACKLOG, OP-13 group 4); the validator already rejected unknown modifier durations and an empty `filter: {}` (tests added).
- **Rejected:** a per-script manual split for every use of the duration (the engine was wrong, not the scripts); a count prompt for `giveDon` n 1 (a second way to decline the same thing); keeping `setActive` automatic.

## 2026-11-24 — Empty "up to" hand picks: no engine skip; OP14-052 and OP14-116 were a test-helper artifact (no ENGINE_VERSION change)

Context: independent tests for OP14-052 Hannyabal (after a trash-3 cost) and OP14-116 Salamander (after a +2000 pick) showed no play pick when no hand card qualified, while OP14-043/047/058 asked it, a suspected severity 4 leak. Finding: the engine asks the pick in every frame. `select` (`effects/run.ts`) skips a hidden-zone pick only when the whole zone is empty and there is no filter and no `restrict`, a public fact (zone size); a `play` always carries `restrict`, and 052/116 carry a filter, so a hand with no match is asked at max 0 with 0 candidates, with or without a cost frame (probe: after the third cost pick the decision is `choose`, purpose effect, max 0). The tests' `payHand`/`pickInst` helpers (`choose` in `kit.ts`) send `done` after the last pick when the choices are empty, so they answered the following empty pick themselves. Decision: no engine change, no version bump, goldens untouched; the two tests now pick raw and are plain `it`; DISAGREEMENTS-b/-d mark the entries resolved. Planted `engine/test/cards/empty-pick-hidden.test.ts` (no cost, and an optional hand cost paid first; two positions differing only in a hidden hand card: asked at max 0 vs 1, and p2's projected view and events equal); with the skip widened to any empty pick it fails 4 of 4 and 13 independent OP-14 tests besides. Already-pushed scripts on main are not affected. Rejected: changing the shared helpers (other independent tests rely on the swallow and would fail), and a new leak-property check (the two-position tests cover the shape; the property already compares permuted hidden cards over random play).

## 2026-11-24 — Static hidden-gate check over every script (no ENGINE_VERSION change)

Context: the OP15 fix round found OP15-002 Lucy asking its "trash any number of Event or Stage" pick only when the hand held one, so the pause told the opponent what the hand held (ADR 0005). The 2,000-game leak property only samples such cases. Decision: `cards/src/hidden-gate.ts` (run over the whole registry by `cards/test/hidden-gate.test.ts`, with planted gates so it can fail) reuses the typed walk in `hidden.ts` and reports a hidden-zone condition that decides whether a prompt is asked: (1) an `auto` with a hidden ability-level condition and a prompt, a cost or `optional`; (2) a `trigger` with one (the owner is always asked about a revealed [Trigger]); (3) an optional `replacement` with one; (4) an `if` whose hidden condition makes `then` and `else` differ in prompts; (5) a `forEach` with a hidden count around a prompt. Not reported: a hidden condition with no prompt in the gated branches (the outcome is public, as the card text says); `activate`, `permanent` and Event [Main]/[Counter] offers (shown to their owner only); an `if` whose branches ask the same prompts. A prompt is a selector `pick` (not `blind`), `may`, `chooseOne`, `lookAtTop`, `draw`/`addDon upTo`, or a DON!! choice. Allowlist `HIDDEN_GATE_ALLOWED` (card/ability, one-line reason) exists and is empty; a stale entry fails the test, so it can only shrink. The sweep of all scripts found two more: OP16-041 Buggy and ST32-003 Dracule Mihawk gated a play-from-hand pick on `exists` in hand; both now gate on a hand of at least 1 card and ask the pick at max 0 (their author tests pin it; the old assertions "nothing asked with no eligible card" were the leak). No independent test changed. Script-only change, goldens unchanged. Rejected: flagging every hidden-reading ability (a public outcome such as "if you have X in hand, draw" is what the card says and cannot be hidden); a runtime assertion in the engine (cannot know what the other player could infer); allowing "the prompt is always followed by a public outcome" implicitly (the prompt comes first and an up-to pick need not produce one), so such a case needs an allowlist line. Limits: a hidden read only inside a Selector filter's number is not walked.

## 2026-11-24 — Hand-zone cost permanents move to a last layer stage (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.12; OP15-R1)

- **Context:** review OP15-R1. A hand-zone cost permanent sat in stage 1 (cost), and a stage reads only the stages before it, so "If your Leader has 0 power or less, give this card in your hand -2 cost" (OP15-013, OP15-102) read the Leader's power without its power-stage permanents (Sabo printed 5000 with a permanent -1000 and timed -4000: 0 power, read as 1000).
- **Decision:** a new stage 6 holds the cost effects whose source is in hand (`zone: 'hand'`). Its conditions read stages 1 to 5, the fully derived power, so the read is right; no field stage reads a stage above its own, so there is no cycle. `LAST_STAGE` is 6. A field-zone cost permanent stays in stage 1. Goldens re-recorded: only the version string changed.
- **Consequence:** a permanent in stages 1 to 5 no longer sees the discounted cost of a card in hand (no script reads a hand card's cost there). Hand-cost effects read each other without the other's stage 6 discount, so two of them cannot loop.
- **Rejected:** re-reading power at the last stage for hand abilities only (a second evaluation path, still cyclic if the hand cost fed a power condition).
- Also in this fix round: OP15-002 Lucy's pick was gated on the hand holding an Event or Stage (a hidden fact visible through the prompt); the 2000-game leak property caught it. The gate is now the public hand size, so the pick is asked at max 0.
- Also considered and not done (OP15-R7): revealing a card on a `play` frame in the view (`canSee`). It removed the skeleton/view mismatch but fails the leak property "views are invariant under hidden permutations" (the played card is a hidden hand card in the property's model), so it is in BACKLOG instead.

## 2026-11-24 — M6b-2 wave 3: G20 to G29 (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.11)

- **Context:** wave 3 of the M6b-1 audit (`docs/research/m6b-wave3.md`): the opponent's DON!! given to the opponent's cards, `cannotSetDonActive`, hand-trash and rested timings, `Num` division, `cardPlayed` origin and `via`, per-turn history, `returnDon` for the opponent, the K.O. cause on [On K.O.], and `removed`. It unblocks 44 cards, closing block 5 except OP16-079 (D9) and OP16-080 (D3).
- **Decision:**
  - Per-turn history (`State.hist`, keyed by turn) is recorded only in a game where some script reads it, so every other game's hashes, goldens and replays are unchanged.
  - `by` qualifiers match effects only. Costs count only with `by` omitted (G24) or `includeCosts: true` (G22). G29's `cause` counts costs as effects, as G11 did (RL-16).
  - A hand-trash event fires once per instruction. `removed` watchers form their own batch, queued before the K.O.'d card's [On K.O.].
  - `removed` fires in `moveCard`, so a rule trash (full Character area) matches when `cause` is absent.
  - The K.O. cause travels as an event payload (`ev`) on the pending effect and is checked again at resolution.
  - `giveDon` with `state: 'any'` asks which DON!! kinds only when both exist and not all of them go.
  - The AI lethal bound leaves `cannotSetDonActive` unclassified, so the solver searches.
- **Rejected:**
  - Always recording history: it changes every golden hash.
  - A history field cleared at turn change: keying by turn needs no clearing step.
  - Settling M6b-Q30, Q31 or Q41 now: both readings stay expressible.
  - One batch for removal watchers and [On K.O.]: it needs a `runKo` restructure.
  - Hooking only the K.O. and remove frames: it misses the cost and rule paths.
  - A new `Frame` per K.O.
  - Always asking the DON!! kind.
  - Classifying `cannotSetDonActive` as neutral without a per-shape proof.
- New open questions M6b-Q41 to Q47 are in OPEN-QUESTIONS.

## 2026-11-24 — `giveDon` with `each` asks the targets and a count per target (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.10)

- **Context:** the ST block 5 review (STB5-R1, severity 3) showed that the wave 2 rule for "give up to M rested DON!! to each of up to N targets" (the DON!! on hand cap the targets at `floor(DON / M)`, every target gets exactly M) made legal plays unreachable: ST30-014 with 1 rested DON!! could give nothing, and 3 rested could not give 2 + 1.
- **Decision:** the target pick allows up to `min(N, DON available)` targets (a target needs at least 1); then, in pick order, each target is asked a count from `min(M, DON left - targets still to serve)` down to 1, most first (the same `mode` choose that `giveDon` already uses, so UI, projection and AI are unchanged). A range of one value is not asked, so OP14-105 (M = 1) plays as before. Zero is not offered per target because choosing fewer targets already means that (and the reservation keeps every picked target served).
- **Rejected:** keeping the cap (a rules error), and a 0 option per target (a second way to say "fewer targets").
- Games recorded before are unchanged in play (the three goldens differ only in the version stamp).

## 2026-11-24 — Random Standard decks in the property games; a replacement-offer leak fixed (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.9)

- **Random Standard decks** (`randomStandardDeck`, `standardDecksFor` in `engine/test/support/decks.ts`): a Standard Leader and 50 Standard-legal cards in its colours, scripted cards weighted 6:1, restricted limits, banned pairs and deck clauses respected, validated with `deckIssues(..., 'standard')`. `standard-games.test.ts` (invariants, replay, listed and unlisted actions), both leak properties and the skeleton diff now run on them too (60 games per push, 10,000 nightly), with seed and decks logged on failure. The default meta-deck games are unchanged (goldens depend on them). This is the M6b done criterion "10,000 nightly random-Standard-deck games"; the first 10,000-game run was invariant-clean.
- **It found a hidden-information leak (severity 4 class):** a replacement whose cost is paid from a hidden zone (OP16-018: trash a 6000+ Character from hand instead of being K.O.'d) was offered only when payable, so the pause told the opponent what the hand held. Now an optional replacement with a secret cost is offered whenever its public conditions hold, with accept unavailable when it cannot be paid (`engine/src/flow.ts`; the `costIsSecret` rule already used for auto abilities and looks). The same class was found by inspection in a [Trigger] that activates the card's own [Counter] or [Main] and fixed the same way. Planted test `engine/test/hooks/leakfix-replacement-offer.test.ts` fails with the fix reverted. Non-optional replacements are unchanged.

## 2026-11-24 — The owner orders cards placed at a deck bottom from hand or trash; a hand-to-deck leak fixed (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.8)

Context: the OP-16 review (OP16-R3, disagreement B-1) found that "place N cards from your hand at the bottom of your deck in any order" gave the owner no order choice: `run.ts` asked only for cards leaving the field. Decision: several cards of one owner placed at a deck bottom always get the owner's `order` decision, whatever the source zone (field, hand, trash) and whether it is an effect or a cost (OP17-095's replacement payload included); no script flag, the same rule the field case used, so future "in any order" cards need nothing per card. This supersedes the 2026-11-16 note that OP17-095's payload has no order decision. **A hidden-information leak was fixed with it (severity 4 class):** a hand card moved into a deck was marked `revealed`, which named it in the opponent's projected events (OP16-047 and OP17-053 make the opponent bottom two hand cards). Hand cards going into a deck are no longer revealed; trash cards stay public. Planted tests in `engine/test/cards/m6b-order/` include a projection check that fails with `revealed` put back. The nightly leak property did not catch it because it plays meta decks only, which run neither card: the random-Standard-deck nightly games (an M6b done criterion) come next. Also from the OP-16 review (OP16-R7): author tests for the OP16-081 cost boundary and OP16-104 power vs base power, each seen to kill its mutation. Goldens re-recorded for the version string only.

## 2026-11-24 — E-Q11 revisited: "add up to N DON!!" asks a count (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.7)

Context: E-Q11 (2026-10-29, kept at M4b) let `addDon` add the maximum without asking, on the reasoning that more DON!! is never worse and no card needed fewer. The OP-17 review (OP17-R2) disproved it: 38 Standard cards compare your DON!! count with the opponent's ("equal to or less than", "at least N less"), so a forced add can switch your own cards off (OP17-068 or OP17-078 with OP06-061/063/065/069 or EB04-038; two EB05-033 in one turn). Decision: an `addDon` part with `upTo: true` (already on all 20 scripts that use it) asks a count, most first, with "None" last, reusing `giveDon`'s decision (purpose `mode`), so UI, projection, replay and AI needed no change; exact adds and an empty DON!! deck ask nothing; a two-part add asks per part. The count is public. `setActive` stays automatic (no counter-example found). The four `it.fails` that documented E-Q11 now pass; 9 planted tests in `engine/test/cards/m6b-eq11/` (7 fail with the change reverted; 2 guard unchanged behaviour). Pirate and Warlord rollouts take the first option (the most); MCTS can explore fewer; Rookie picks at random, as for every decision. Also: playbook `cite` text quotes types (`"Rocks Pirates"`), so the hidden-name check in explanations cannot match a type that is also a card name. Rejected: keeping E-Q11 (wrong under the rules and now observable); a new DSL field (the scripts already said `upTo`).

## 2026-11-24 — M6b-2 wave 2: ten more engine gaps (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.6)

Wave 2 of `docs/research/m6b-runtime-audit.md` §4 (D4 and G11 to G19), built by an agent in a worktree and merged after a full suite run (5,733 unit, 234 e2e); detail and revert results in `docs/research/m6b-wave2.md`; planted tests in `engine/test/cards/m6b-w2/`, each seen to fail with its implementation broken. Goldens re-recorded for the bump; hashes identical.

- **Damage replacement (D4, EB05-052 and Gloriosa's shape)** covers the whole damage, offered before any Life card moves (a Double Attack's two cards are one damage), and is offered at 0 Life too, else the card is useless when it matters. A damage frame at 0 Life is pushed only when the defender has a possible replacement, so every other game runs exactly as before. Rejected: per Life card; only while Life remains; always pushing the frame (golden churn). M6b-Q8 stays open.
- **K.O. and leave-field causes:** `effect` includes costs (RL-16), `opponentEffect` excludes your own costs (RL-14), `any` covers only removals that go through the engine's frames (rule moves such as making room are never replaced).
- **Deck-building clauses** (`anyNumberInDeck`, `deckRestriction`) are checked in the single validator `deckIssues`, read from the loaded script or, before scripts load (the deck builder), a static `DECK_RULES` table; a test loads every script and checks the table agrees. Rejected: loading every script in the builder (bundle budget).
- **`oneOf` cost:** the choice comes first, each alternative is its own list of payments (so later DON!! needs are reserved correctly); refused when nested, in a replacement, or more than once per list.
- **giveDon each:** "up to N targets, up to M each" gives the full M to each chosen target.
- Also: `nameIncludes`, distinct-name counts, lookAtTop take to play (through the normal play frames), K.O. on a Stage. Deferred: one Once Per Turn covering two events (OP13-046), the AI deck generator reading deck clauses. Open questions M6b-Q5, Q8, Q9, Q36 left open.

## 2026-11-24 — The Standard pool verified against Limitless (keyword-only cards play fully)

The EB-05 review (EB05-R6) and the independent testers found that unverified cards which print a keyword (for example [Blocker] on ST08-006) have empty `keywords` in the source data, so the engine ignored the keyword: printed keywords reach the engine only through a verification override (E-D7). `cards:verify-meta -- --set <SET>` now takes a whole product (Standard-legal cards only); it was run over all 52 Standard products with unverified cards (about 2,060 Limitless pages, cached in `.cache/m1/limitless/`, one request a second; robots.txt allows). **1,950 cards matched field for field** and got verified overrides with their printed keywords (the M1 method); 110 disagree in some field and stay unverified until their batch is scripted (the runbook's step d.1 settles them). Verified cards: 183 → 2,013. Standard coverage 19.5% → 20.6% (keyword-only cards now count as playing fully). Alternatives rejected: parsing keywords from optcgapi's text (ADR 0006: text is never parsed into behaviour); waiting for each batch (the keyword-only cards would stay wrong in play for months).

## 2026-11-24 — Blocks cited for EB-05 and nine starter decks (runbook step a)

EB-05 (the first scripting batch) and the starter decks with no cited block had `unknown` Standard legality. Each product's block was read from one Limitless card page (the source `standard.json` already accepts for ST-30): EB-05, ST-33, ST-35 and ST-36 block 5; ST-22, ST-23, ST-24, ST-25 and ST-28 block 3; ST-08 block 1 (not Standard). 113 cards became Standard-legal and ST-08's 15 rotated; the Standard pool is now 2,124 cards. As for the other products, every printing is assumed to carry the product block. Promos keep `unknown` until each is read (their block is per card). Tests that used EB05-001 as the example of an uncited card now use the promo Leader P-011; `play-frequency.json` was re-enriched (it records each card's Standard status).

## 2026-11-24 — M6b-2 wave 1: ten engine gaps (ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.5)

Context: wave 1 of `docs/research/m6b-runtime-audit.md` §4 (G1 to G10), built by an agent in a worktree and merged after its report and a full suite run; detail and per-gap revert results in `docs/research/m6b-wave1.md`. Each gap has planted tests on vanilla test cards (`engine/test/cards/m6b-w1/`, 44 tests), and each was seen to fail with its implementation broken. Goldens re-recorded for the version bump: hashes and event sequences unchanged.

- **Field costs (G1)** go through the existing removal and K.O. frames with cause `cost` (last-known information, given DON!! returned, [On K.O.] fires), not a plain move; the validator accepts them only for your own Characters and Stage. **A K.O. cost on a protected Character is offered and fails**: earlier costs stay paid and the effect does not resolve (RL-16, "legal but failed payment"); the check is whether the card left the field, so a replaced K.O. fails the same way. Rejected: hiding protected Characters from the choice (cannot express the replaced K.O.).
- **A cost counts as an effect for protection** ("cannot be K.O.'d by effects" stops a K.O. cost; rules-digest 112, RL-16); "by your opponent's effects" stops only the card owner's opponent's (RL-14, OP04-079).
- **`mill` clamps to the deck, and an empty deck loses at once** (RL-26, digest note 274.2). The audit's sentence that milling must never lose by itself contradicted the rulings and was not followed.
- **`cannotBeRested`**: unscoped blocks every rest, own effects, attacks and blocks included (digest 57); the opponent-effect form blocks only a rest by an effect of the owner's opponent.
- **"During this battle" with no battle in progress** creates nothing and asks for no target (any action with a battle duration).
- **One `scope` field on modifiers and one `restrictionMod` mapping** for timed and permanent restrictions, not one modifier kind per scope.
- **Two more shapes refused by the validator** that used to pass and misexecute (a player restriction on a card target; a permanent `cannotDrawByOwnEffects`); no script used them.
- **The lethal bound** counts `cannotActivateBlocker` as a Blocker lost (it was treated as neutral, which would have been unsound); scoped restrictions, `mill`, `activate` and the new costs stay unclassified, so the solver searches instead of trusting the bound.
- **Open questions left open:** M6b-Q12, Q30, Q31 (the mechanisms support either reading; settled when their cards are scripted).

## 2026-11-23 — Tests run on a pinned copy of the meta file

Context: the first real `update.yml` run (2026-11-24 UTC) produced a meta PR whose CI failed 23 unit tests and 4 e2e tests: pinning only the test decks (M6b-0) was not enough, because the AI's opponent model (`ai/src/knowledge.ts`), lethal certainty, the review flags and the AI's explanations all read `docs/research/meta-lists.json`. A weekly PR that can never pass CI would never be merged. Decision: Vitest and the e2e build (`PINNED_META=1` in `playwright.config.ts`) alias every import of `meta-lists.json` to `ai/test/fixtures/meta-lists.pinned.json` (the file as of 2026-10-28; it replaces `strength-decks.json`). Tests about the live file itself read it from disk and still run on the PR: every list is Standard-legal (errors only; an uncited block such as EB-05's is a warning, as in the deck builder) and round-trips through every export format. Checked by running the failing tests with the PR's meta file in place: all pass. Alternatives rejected: letting the meta PR fail until a person updates expectations (the PR is weekly and machine-made); injecting the meta into `priorFor` (every caller would change, for a test concern). Update the pinned copy only on purpose, with a DECISIONS entry.

## 2026-11-23 — M6b-1 decisions: the runtime audit and the M6b-2 wave plan

Context: three auditors checked 1,175 cards (the 1,120 in-scope backlog cards plus EB-05) against the DSL and the runtime (`docs/research/m6b-audit/block{5,4,3}.md`); a fourth merged their 122 gaps into 67 implementation gaps and 9 design items with a wave plan (`docs/research/m6b-runtime-audit.md`). 883 cards need nothing new, 292 need a gap, 15 need a design decision.

- **Wave order as proposed.** Wave 1 (own-Character costs, `cannotBeRested`, the `attribute` filter, the `mill` action, activating the card's own [On K.O.], scoped K.O. protection, the giveDon, turnLifeFaceDown and returnDon-active costs, `cannotActivateBlocker`) makes 84% of the audited cards and 88% of the pool weight expressible, including 54 of EB-05's 55 cards, so it goes first, before the first batch (EB-05, then OP-18 once imported).
- **Design items: the auditor's recommendation, each.** D4 (replacement on taking damage, EB05-052) in wave 2, so EB-05 closes fully; D6 (a replacement created by an Event, EB02-030, weight 161) in wave 4 as an `addReplacement` modifier; D3 (`redirectAttack`, OP16-080 and OP14-060) in wave 7, narrow (to your Leader or a legal Character), after checking the official Q&A; D2 (activating an Event from hand or trash), D8 (a delayed effect at the opponent's next Main Phase) and D9 (an effect on the event's subject) in wave 8 after their rulings. D1 (three rules-changing Leaders), D5 (whole-side [On Play] negation, OP09-081) and D7 (an Activate-registered battle watcher) stay stats-only for now (weights 9, 14, 3) and go to `docs/BACKLOG.md`. Rejected: building every design item in wave 1 (rulings first; the weight is small).
- **Readings that conflict with rulings on file are settled by the rulings,** not the auditors: a cost is an effect for "cannot be K.O.'d by effects" (rules-digest 112, RL-16; M6b-Q31), and "only Characters without a Counter" is false with no Characters (E-Q4; OP16-022). The 22 open questions (M6b-Q1 to Q40 in OPEN-QUESTIONS) are settled when their card's batch is scripted, from the official Q&A, or the card waits.
- **The one engine defect** (a `trash` cost from the field validates but runs as a plain move, with no cause and no removal frame) has no user today (no script uses it); wave 1's own-Character costs fix it, and the validator rejects the shape until then.
- **Card-data problems** found by the audit (lost signs, lost attribute names, a lost timing header, scrape typos, empty keywords) are fixed through each card's verification override when its batch is scripted (runbook step d.1).

## 2026-11-23 — M6b-6 app updates and M6b-7 AI for any Leader

- **App shell (M6b-6).** The service worker precaches every built file (pages, chunks, the per-set card data and effect-script chunks, styles, fonts; not source maps), about 140 files, into `optcg-shell-<build>`, where the build id is a hash of the files' contents (`app/vite.config.ts`). Pages are network-first with the cached `index.html` as the offline fallback; hashed files are cache-first, matched with `ignoreVary` (the preview server's `Vary: Origin` made the module entry miss offline). Old shells are deleted when a new worker activates. Offline is tested (`app/e2e/update.spec.ts`).
- **Updates wait for the player.** The first install takes control at once; a later build installs and waits. The page shell shows "A new version of the app is ready … Reload to update"; the game route renders outside that shell, so the prompt can never appear mid-game, and the app never reloads by itself. Accepting sends `skip-waiting` and reloads on `controllerchange`. An open tab checks for a deploy hourly as well as on navigation. The e2e test "deploys" by rewriting the served `dist/sw.js` with another build id (Playwright routing does not see the browser's fetch of a worker script), at one resolution only.
- **`version.json`** at the site root: build id, build time, cards through (newest booster), card count and fetch time, the meta's as-of date, engine version. The About page (M10) and bug reports read it.
- **IndexedDB records move to M8** ("Review: local save"). Nothing saves records yet, so a store now would have no reader; M8 builds it with its UI. Records already carry the per-game scripts version (M6b-0), so a saved game opens live unless its own cards' scripts changed.
- **AI for any Leader (M6b-7; built by an agent, merged after review of its report and a full suite run).** `playbookFor(leader, ownDeck, cards)` never returns null: a Leader without a playbook gets `defaultPlaybook`, built from the AI's own deck (mulligan pieces: 1–3 cost Characters and Counter cards, S9; go second, S17; neutral weights; no biases, so "Why?" never cites a guide that does not exist; `isDefault` changes the mulligan copy from "Its guide keeps" to "It keeps"). `priorFor` never returns null: a Leader without sourced lists gets a synthetic prior from `cards/data/play-frequency.json`, which now carries colours, category, Standard status and copy limit per card and every Standard Leader's colours (`enrichFrequency`, tested against the committed file). The pool is every Standard-legal card of the Leader's colours that was played; three 50-card guesses at typical copy counts serve determinization. **The lethal detector stays conservative:** with a synthetic prior it keeps the 4000 Counter floor and the "no sourced list" gap, so it never claims "any hand" against a guessed deck, and it reads the whole pool for the defence bound. Trigger notes for a guessed deck say "a typical deck for this Leader runs N Triggers; their list may differ" and never rule a Trigger out. Rejected: a static `default.json` (cannot name the AI's own early plays); passing the Leader's colours into `priorFor` (the app calls `priorCardIds` before card data loads); treating the synthetic prior as sourced (it would allow "any hand" against a guess).

## 2026-11-23 — M6b-5: the update pipeline (`update.yml`) and the new-set runbook

- **`.github/workflows/update.yml`**, weekly (Mondays 06:17 UTC) and on dispatch, two jobs. `cards`: `cards:update -- --refresh --allow-script-changes`, `cards:measure`, a PR body from `scripts/cards-pr-body.ts` (new, removed and changed cards; scripted cards whose text changed; products with no cited block; Standard coverage before and after; a rotation warning within 60 days of `regulation.to`). `meta`: `meta:update -- --refresh --write` with its report as the body, and the standings cache kept between runs. Each job commits to a fixed branch (`update/cards`, `update/meta`) and opens or updates its PR (`scripts/update-pr.sh`).
- **Errata on a scripted card is written, not refused,** in the scheduled run (`--allow-script-changes`), and listed in the PR. The new test `cards/test/script-text.test.ts` compares every script's `textHash` with its card's current text, so that PR's CI fails until each card is re-verified. Before this, nothing checked the hash against the data: a text change to a scripted card would have passed CI.
- **CI on the update branches:** PRs opened with `GITHUB_TOKEN` do not trigger `pull_request` workflows, so the job starts CI with `workflow_dispatch` (now allowed in `ci.yml`), which runs every card test (no base to diff against).
- **The repository does not allow Actions to create pull requests** (`can_approve_pull_request_reviews: false`). Changing a repository security setting is the owner's call, so the job does not need it: it always pushes the branch, and when PR creation is refused it writes the compare link and the body to the run summary. Owner item in STATUS Blockers.
- **The M1 meta-card tests read the pinned lists** (`cards/test/data.test.ts`): a meta PR brings cards that are not verified yet, and its report lists them for the next batch. They should not make the meta PR unmergeable.
- **`docs/runbooks/new-set.md`** writes out steps a to h (cited block, audit, engine wave with planted tests, parallel scripting with overrides and author tests, independent tests, adversarial review, one fix round, the "Scripts: <SET>" PR).

Alternatives rejected: `peter-evans/create-pull-request` (a third-party action with write access; `gh` does the same); a personal access token so PRs trigger CI (a long-lived secret; `workflow_dispatch` is enough); committing straight to main (ADR 0014: nothing reaches main unreviewed).

## 2026-11-23 — M6b-0 re-baseline: about 830–880 h without block 2

Context: PLAN amendment A1 asks for a re-baseline at the end of M6b-0, once `cards:measure` has measured the backlog. Measured: 1,660 unscripted Standard cards (block 5 180, block 4 422, block 3 510, X and block 1 exceptions 8, block 2 held 540). Decision: M6b-3 is estimated from the cards in scope while block 2 is held (about 1,300 to 1,400 with EB-05, OP-18 and uncited-legality cards) at the plan's own rate of about 2.6 cards an hour: about 500–540 h; M6b-4 75–80 h; M6b about 830–880 h, plus about 240 h if block 2 stays legal after the 2027-03-31 rotation. PLAN §1 carries the arithmetic. Alternatives rejected: keeping 1,000–1,100 h (it counted block 2, which the plan itself holds); a new rate before any non-meta batch has been scripted (the first batch measures it, and the existing trigger, fewer than 3 cards an hour, forces a re-plan).

## 2026-11-23 — M6b-0: effect scripts load per set; the tagger is a tool (`cards:measure`)

- **Per-set script chunks.** `cards/effects/<SET>/index.ts` and `cards/effects/registry.ts` are generated (`npm run cards:effects-index`; a test fails when they are stale, and the generator formats as Prettier does). `SCRIPTED_IDS` comes from the registry without loading any script, so the card browser and the deck builder load none. `SCRIPTS` is one shared table that `loadScripts(ids)` fills set by set. The app's `loadCards` (every game start, scenario and restore goes through it), the AI worker (on `init` and `cards`; queued work waits for the load) and the review worker load what their game needs. **The engine refuses to build a scripted card whose script is not loaded** (`toDef`, `donDeckSizeFor`), so a missed load fails loudly instead of playing the card as vanilla (planted test `engine/test/rules/scripts-loaded.test.ts`). Tests, benches and tools load everything (`test-setup/scripts.ts`, a Vitest setup file); the test helper `asVanilla` now also removes the card from `SCRIPTED_IDS`. `SCRIPTS_VERSION` (a hash of the whole table at import time) is removed; records use `scriptsVersionOf`. The AI and review workers each shrank by about 120 KB raw; script code now ships only in the per-set chunks. Rejected: an async engine API (every reducer caller would change); one chunk per card (thousands of requests per game); the static table (each worker would carry every script, about ten times today's at the full pool).
- **The tagger is ported** (`cards/scripts/measure*.ts`, `npm run cards:measure` and `cards:report`; E-D10). Zero mismatches against the Python tagger on all 2,860 texts; clause tag rate 97.75%. `cards/data/coverage-report.json` (deterministic; a test regenerates and compares it) holds per card the status, tags and play weight, plus the backlog and the gap list. Playing fully means scripted, vanilla, or verified keyword-only: the same `automation()` the app shows. Standard means legal or restricted; `unknown` legality is listed, not counted. Backlog order: block 5, 4, 3, X, then 2 (marked held, ADR 0014); within a block the newest set first, Leaders first, then play weight (`play-frequency.json` copies; decks for Leaders), then id. Gap primitives map to DSL names only where the mapping is clear; others are "unknown", never guessed, and the implemented flag is coarse (`move` exists, not every zone pair). The hand corrections in `dsl_map.py` for the 182 meta cards were not ported: those cards are all scripted.
- **Per-push card tests** (`npm run test:ci`, CI's `checks` job): `npm test` minus the per-card test files (named by card number under `engine/test/cards/` and `engine/test/independent/cards/`) of cards the push did not change, against the PR base or the push's previous commit. A change to the engine, `cards/src`, card data (not the coverage or frequency reports), the script loader, shared test kits or the toolchain runs every card's tests; so does a missing base. The regenerated script registry does not widen the run, or every scripting push would run the full suite. The nightly runs `npm test` in full. Rejected: running every card test per push (at the full pool, tens of thousands of tests per push); a path filter in the workflow (cannot express "the tests of the cards whose scripts changed").
- **Baseline:** Standard 351 of 2,011 cards play fully (17.5%): block 5 31.3%, block 4 17.6%, block 3 17.1%, block 2 (held) 11.2%. 246 cards have unknown legality. The backlog holds 1,660 unscripted cards, 663 of them with play weight. The largest gaps not yet in the DSL: replacement abilities (58 cards), other costs (52), automatic event timings (52), mill (47).

## 2026-11-23 — M6b-0: Limitless terms checked; `meta:update`; pinned test decks; per-game scripts version

- **ADR 0014's open check: passed.** Limitless developer docs (https://docs.limitlesstcg.com/developer.html, read 2026-11-23): "you do NOT need an API key to access the API" except the `/games/{id}/decks` categorisation rules; keys are for higher limits and "public-facing projects". No attribution requirement, and nothing in the docs or the play.limitlesstcg.com Terms of Service (2021-02-28) restricts automated reads, redistribution or non-commercial use. The live API sends `ratelimit-policy: "50-in-5min"`. robots.txt: onepiece.limitlesstcg.com allows everything; play.limitlesstcg.com, docs.limitlesstcg.com and optcgapi.com have none (404), so nothing is disallowed. Scheduled access is allowed; no owner decision needed. **The request gap is 7 s, not ADR 0014's 3 s**: 3 s would exceed 50 requests per 5 minutes, and the script also slows down when the header says fewer than 5 remain, and honours `429` with `Retry-After`.
- **`npm run meta:update`** (`scripts/meta-update.ts`, pure logic in `cards/scripts/meta.ts`, tests `cards/test/meta-update.test.ts`). It counts as the file's `countingMethod` says: Standard events of 24+ players with decklists in the last 90 days. An event using a card of a **set** the snapshot lacks entirely (OP-18 before upstream has it) is skipped, as "not yet released in English"; a card of a known set that the snapshot lacks (promos P-108, P-116, P-135, P-153 on 2026-11-23) is a source gap, so only that decklist is skipped and the card is reported. A decklist that breaks the current Standard list is skipped. First live run: 34 events, 2,024 decklists; the top 12 Leaders equal the current file's 12 archetypes. The candidate `meta-lists.json` is not applied by hand: it lands through the first reviewed update PR (M6b-5). Per Leader: top-8 count, meta share over counted decklists, and the three best-placed lists (one per event first). **The candidate keeps every hand field** (tiers, sources, notes) and **never removes an archetype**: one that leaves the top 12 is reported, since a guide and a playbook depend on it. New top-12 Leaders are added with empty hand fields. It also writes `cards/data/play-frequency.json` (per card: decks, copies, share; per Leader), which orders M6b-3 batches, and a report that is the meta PR body. Responses are cached in `.cache/meta/`; settled events (over 7 days old) are never re-fetched. Without `--write` the candidate goes to `.cache/meta/out/`. Rejected: replacing the hand-kept file wholesale (loses tiers and sources); fetching `/details` per event (one extra request per event; the standings show whether decklists exist).
- **Test decks pinned** to `ai/test/fixtures/meta-lists.pinned.json` (a copy of the 36 lists as of 2026-10-28). The engine goldens and property tests, the AI gates and the app test support read it, so a meta PR cannot move a gate or a golden. The M6 tests that check the meta file itself (legality and round trip of every list) still read `meta-lists.json` and no longer assume 36 lists. The AI's archetype prior (`ai/src/knowledge.ts`) still follows the live file, so a meta refresh can still shift gate numbers slightly through the opponent model; that is the AI learning the new meta, and the nightly shows it.
- **Per-game scripts version** (`scriptsVersionOf`, `engine/src/record.ts`; closes M4b-17 as far as it can be). A record's `versions.scripts` is now the hash of the scripts of the card numbers in that game, checked in replay next to the card-data hash; `replayable` checks only the engine version. Scripting or changing an unrelated card leaves records live (planted test in `engine/test/records`). Goldens re-recorded: the version field changed, every game hash is identical. The golden guard is now strict for unrelated script changes; an engine change landing with a script change to a card in the same game can still slip, so the ENGINE_VERSION bump rule stays. Records made before this change open read-only once (their scripts field was the global hash). ENGINE_VERSION unchanged: no game plays out differently.

## 2026-11-23 — M6 fix round 1 (review docs/reviews/m6-review.md, FAIL on M6-1 and M6-2)

- **M6-1 (sev 3):** the deck store reads storage on every write (read-modify-write by id) and listens for other tabs at module load, so a stale tab can no longer overwrite another tab's deck; Setup and game start read a saved deck fresh from storage. After a failed write the store keeps building on its in-memory copy.
- **M6-2 (sev 3):** no builder action drops focus to the page. A tile's − uses `aria-disabled` at 0 copies; removing a card's last copy from the deck list focuses the list heading; choosing or changing the Leader focuses search; closing Import or Export returns focus to its button; Import opens on the paste box. A "Skip to the deck" link jumps past the grid.
- **Severity 2 fixed:** M6-4 (Setup waits for card data and checks unknown cards with the same message as the builder; an unknown set no longer fails the others), M6-5 (stored decks are repaired or dropped one by one), M6-6 (a failed save is shown), M6-7 ("Show more" inside the grid's scroll; short "Choose" label; the deck pane keeps 70vh below 960 px), M6-8 (each deck's problems under its picker in Setup, a summary by Start), M6-9 (lowercase IDs, headings such as "Characters (12)", a bare ID or "Leader: ID" line, trailing comments), M6-10 (a line count over 50 is refused), M6-12 (row wording from the format file: "Standard blocks 2–5", "list from 2026-10-12"; no file names shown), M6-13 (inline delete confirmation, not `window.confirm`), M6-16 (Esc keeps the pasted list for the next open).
- **Carried, with targets:** M6-3 (arrow keys in the grid, Tab between panes, F17's Enter/Backspace and `A` for art) and M6-14 (colour and set filters, DON!! deck size, group by category) → M10 accessibility and polish pass; none is in the M6 done criteria, and the skip link plus +/− keys cover the keyboard path. Severity 1 items M6-11, 15, 17 to 21 → M10.

## 2026-11-23 — M6 deck builder: one validator, saved decks, formats

Context: M6 (PLAN row 17) needs a deck builder whose checks agree with Setup and game start (flows F17 step 7), lossless import and export, and saved decks any Standard card can enter.

- **One validator in the engine.** `deckIssues(deck, lookup, format)` (`engine/src/deck.ts`) returns typed issues with the rule and the card numbers, for `standard` (CR 5-1-2 plus `standard.json`: banned, banned pairs including the Leader, restricted limits, rotated), `extra` (CR plus bans and pairs) or `free` (CR only). `validateDeck`, which `createGame` calls, is now `deckIssues(..., 'free')` formatted as strings, so game start keeps accepting rotated cards (scenarios, old decks) while the builder and Setup apply the deck's format. `createGame` now loads every definition explicitly; the old validator did it as a side effect.
- **Unknown legality is a warning, not an error.** A card whose block no cited source gives (EB-05, some starter decks, most promos; `standard.json` `gaps`) is shown with the reason and does not block play. Blocking would make cards unplayable because of a gap in our sources, not a rule.
- **Parsing and export live in `cards/`** (`decklist.ts`): format handling, not rules. Formats `4xID`, `4 ID`, `4 Name ID`, `4 ID Name`, and the app's JSON; an alt-art suffix maps to the card number; a single copy of a Leader card is the Leader; an unknown ID whose name matches a card suggests it (same product first). Export groups by first appearance, so export(import(list)) equals the list, normalised. Every one of the 36 meta lists round-trips in every format (test).
- **Saved decks in `localStorage`** (`optcg.decks.v1`, one entry for all decks), not IndexedDB: a deck is a few hundred bytes and ARCHITECTURE §5 lists IndexedDB for records, reviews and progress. **Every change saves at once**, which replaces F17 step 8's "Save changes?" dialog: there is no unsaved state, which fits G3 (few dialogs). Deleting asks for confirmation.
- **Each deck has a format** (Standard by default, or Extra Regulation). Setup blocks Start on that format's errors and lists them (F3a step 2). The meta lists are checked as Standard.
- **Setup shows saved decks** as a "Your decks" group in both deck pickers (`saved:<id>`), so "Play with this deck" and "Play against this deck" are links to Setup with the deck chosen.
- **The browser shows 60 cards at a time** with "Show more", ordered by cards in the deck, then meta-list cards, then the newest sets, instead of the card browser's virtualised grid: the builder's tiles carry controls, and the Leader's colours and the format cut the pool to a few hundred.
- **A deck with a non-meta Leader** plays against the AI with no prior (its hidden cards are guessed from what the AI has seen, padded from its own list) and, when the AI plays it, with no playbook. A test plays Pirate and Warlord games both ways. A default playbook for any Leader is M6b-7.

Alternatives rejected: validation in the app (breaks "rules only in engine/"); refusing over-limit adds (the deck shows why it is invalid instead, as the wireframe does); IndexedDB for decks (more code, no benefit at this size); an explicit Save button (the leave dialog it needs is the kind of interruption G3 avoids).

## 2026-11-23 — M5b closed under the time-box: Warlord vs Pirate target 65% not met, nightly asserts a 55% floor

Context: M5b (estimate 15 h, time-box 60 h) is far past its time-box. Five nightlies on the final code (2026-11-19 to 23) measured Warlord vs Pirate at 123/200 (61.5%, Wilson 54.6 to 68.0%) every night, as the games are seeded. To test changes quickly, a local runner played the gate's exact 200 games over 13 processes (about 13 minutes a run; it reproduced 123/200), and each variant was re-checked on 200 fresh seeds (530,000+). Measured, Warlord wins of 200 against Pirate: shipped 123 (fresh seeds 123); Pirate vs Pirate on the gate games 96 (the deck-pair baseline); leaf horizon 1 instead of 2: 130, at half the cost per game; horizon 1 + 2× turn budget 130; horizon 1 + choose by mean (visits ≥ 30% of the top) 132; horizon 1 + rollouts without Character plays 134; horizon 1 + the Pirate's defence 134; horizon 1 + 3× defence budget 122; horizon 1 + no rollout plays + Pirate's defence 127 (**fresh seeds 119**); no playbook bias in leaf values and 16 determinizations both tracked the baseline game for game (stopped at 76 to 95 games). Every variant lies inside about ±7 points of 61.5%, and the one that touched 65.0% on the gate seeds fell below the baseline on fresh seeds. As in M5b's own runs, strength does not move with budget. The limit is the leaf evaluation and the quick rollout policies, which is research beyond this milestone.

Decision: apply PROMPT's time-box rule. M5b is done with the strength target **not met**, stated in STATUS and benchmarks.md. The nightly test keeps the PLAN target in its report (`target: 0.65`, `meetsTarget`) and asserts a **regression floor of 55%** (a one-iteration Warlord scores 20.5%; the floor fails a crippled search), so the nightly turns green again and catches regressions. The shipped Warlord is unchanged; no knob was shipped, since none beat the noise on fresh seeds. "Warlord ≥ 65% vs Pirate" moves to `docs/BACKLOG.md` under AI depth (a tuned evaluation and a stronger opponent model in rollouts). Also: Kalgara seed 1 (M5b-14) takes the first option from "M5b fix round 2": a Warlord-only threshold of 0.55 (`warlordThreshold` in `OP08-098.json`; reason in the guide), because forcing the free play did not win more. The Emperor nightly report drops from 40 to 16 games (40 overran the 360-minute CI cap twice). The iteration-mode pacing bounds in the nightly e2e are hang checks and now fit CI (a591fb8).

Alternatives rejected: shipping horizon 1 (passes the gate seeds by zero games, not reproducible on fresh seeds); lowering the target to the measured rate (it hides that the PLAN goal is unmet); keeping a red nightly (it hides other regressions); spending more of the time-box on weight tuning, which M5a's and M5b's A/B runs showed insensitive at this sample size.

## 2026-11-17 — Full Standard card pool and live updates (M6b)

Context: only 180 of 2,799 cards have scripts (the 182 meta-list cards), the snapshot ends at OP-17, `cards:update` is manual, `meta:update` (planned for M9) does not exist, PLAN section 8 said CI never calls a card or tournament API, and every new script makes all saved records read-only because `SCRIPTS_VERSION` is one global hash. The owner decided, and approved the PROMPT.md wording (amendment A1):

- **Pool:** every Standard-legal card is fully scripted, each with a test (about 1,815 more). Rotated cards stay browsable and play for their stats with the badge.
- **Updates:** a scheduled weekly `update.yml` opens PRs for card data and the meta; scripting new cards is agent-run locally from `docs/runbooks/new-set.md`, with review per batch. Nothing reaches `main` without review. This reverses PLAN section 8 (ADR 0014; addenda to ADR 0007 and ADR 0011).
- **Order:** M5b, then M6, then the new M6b (card pool and live updates), then M7 to M10. `meta:update` moves from M9 to M6b-0 because scripting order needs its play-frequency data.
- **Scripting order:** block 5, then 4, then 3; within each set, leaders first, then cards by Limitless play frequency. **Block 2 is held** until the rotation after 2027-03-31 is announced (it may rotate out), and M6b's "100% of Standard" excludes it while held.
- **OP-18 and EB-05:** imported now, separately and stats-only (a data commit, no scripts); they become the first scripting batch. Result 2026-11-17: EB-05 imported (61 cards, Standard legality unknown until its block is cited); OP-18 not yet in optcgapi (release 2026-11-20).
- **`script-set.yml` is deferred:** the runbook runs locally only; running it in CI with an API key is a later decision.
- **A1 approved and applied** to PROMPT.md (Done-when bullets, M6b in the Phase 5 table, `meta:update` out of M9, pipeline step 6). PLAN gains row 17b (1,000–1,100 agent-hours, reconciled from the plan's first 650–1,000 because its itemised steps alone came to 1,005–1,115; total 1,718–1,818; re-baseline after M6b-0), risks 9 and 10, and the M6b section.

Open check before building: the Limitless API terms (key or attribution) and both sites' robots.txt (ADR 0014). Alternatives rejected: scripting meta decks only; auto-merging update PRs; scripting in scheduled CI now; scripting block 2 before the rotation is announced; hand-run updates only.

## 2026-11-17 — M5b parked with the Warlord-vs-Pirate gate failing

The owner asked to finish the current round and stop. Fix round 2 is merged (116542c); M5b is **not** marked done because the first nightly on the fixed code measured Warlord vs Pirate at 123/200 (61.5%, Wilson 54.6–68.0%), below PLAN §2's 65%. The gate is unchanged: lowering it needs its own decision with evidence, not a parking note. Kalgara seed 1 (M5b-14) stays failing, documented, with its two options below for whoever resumes. STATUS "M5b … PICK UP HERE" lists the steps to finish.

## 2026-11-17 — M5b fix round 2 (M5b-14, M5b-15, M5b-16, nightly timeouts)

Evidence: the first nightly (run 44074243109) at 64 and 40 games, plus local diagnosis. Warlord vs Pirate there was 123/200 (61.5%, interval 54.6 to 68.0%), below the 65% gate; **not addressed in this round by instruction, the gate is unchanged and stays red.**

- **M5b-15 (Kaido seed 2, 82/129 = 0.64 against 0.8 at 40 games) was a search defect, fixed by a bigger bias.** Diagnosis: in every miss the AI accepted the -2000 and then answered the "target" prompt with a Character that was not the attacker (Miss Buckingham Stussy or another), through the ordinary flat search (not a stale plan). The flat search values shrinking the attacker about 2 raw units below shrinking another Character (a likely cause, not proven: the quick defence policy defends more once an attack is cheaper to stop, and spends cards doing so), and the playbook's `useOnAttacker` bonus was 1.5, so the bias lost. Fix: `useOnAttacker` bonus 1.5 to 4.0 in `OP17-058.json` (a prior, in the same units as the search values). Measured, Warlord, 40 games of the same tally: seed 2 **105/131 = 0.80** (threshold 0.8; a pass by one hit), seeds 1, 3, 4, 5 unchanged and passing. **Not measured:** whether the larger bonus costs or gains strength (an A/B of Kaido against four decks was started and stopped when the round was parked). It is a marginal pass; the nightly decides.
- **M5b-14 (Kalgara seed 1, 55/83 = 0.66 locally, 0.58 over 64 games in the nightly, against 0.9): diagnosed, not fixed; the assertion is left failing and documented.** Of 28 misses in the 40-game local run, 23 are turns where the Leader attacked with no DON!! given (so no free-play prompt appeared) and 5 are turns where the free-play prompt was answered "done"; 22 of the 28 are at 2 or less Life, 3 have a full board. That is the Warlord choosing to hard-cast its Warriors and keep its Life rather than pay a Life card for the free play, the trade the guide itself warns about ("every free play costs a Life card"). Whether that is better play: an A/B with the playbook's Leader-DON!! and attack bonuses raised from 1.0 to 6.0 (the Warlord with Kalgara against four decks, 40 games each, same seeds) won 18 of 40 against 22 of 40 for the shipped size: forcing the free play did not help (within noise, about plus or minus 15 points). So it looks like better or equal play rather than a bias that is too small, but it is **not proven**, and the seed was not weakened. Options for the owner: accept a lower Warlord threshold with the reason in the guide (the measured rate is 0.58 to 0.66), or add a Life-and-slot condition to the seed (3 or more Life and a free Character slot), which I did not do because it changes what the guide claims.
- **Found on the way:** `warlord.ts` now drops a queued macro answer when the turn has changed (`mem.turn !== view.turn.number`), so a bundled answer its effect never asked for cannot answer a later prompt that offers the same handle. It did not cause M5b-14 or 15 (the misses were flat-search answers); it is a guard that the multi-prompt bundling makes more necessary.
- **M5b-16:** `avoidBasePower` test is not tier-tuned (want 10 and penalty 20 for both tiers); the OP13-001 forced position asserts both Characters were played, then the order; `withPrompts` trims to `MAX_MACROS` after expansion; the stale 133/200 STATUS text is marked superseded.
- **Nightly timeouts.** The first nightly's strength series printed their results and then timed out: the hosted runner took about 126 min for Warlord vs Rookie, 123 for Warlord vs Pirate, 142 for the two controls. Per-test timeouts are now 2.5 times those (315, 310, 250 and 100 minutes; Emperor 240, unmeasured) and every `strength-m5b` job has the hosted maximum of 360 minutes; the two controls are separate jobs.

## 2026-11-17 — M5b fix round (review m5b-review.md, M5b-1 to M5b-13)

Context: the adversarial review failed M5b on M5b-1 (the default tier did not follow the playbooks, because every assertion had only ever run against the Pirate). Decisions, in review order.

- **M5b-1: a macro's prior is the move's `mainBias` plus the `otherBias` of every answer bundled into it.** `withPrompts` (`ai/src/search/mcts.ts`) now bundles up to three prompts of a play or activation (a mode, then a target, ...), scores each answer with `otherBias` (targetHighestPower, preferPick, targetCanAttack, pickWithTrigger, avoidTarget on a pick, ...) and orders and caps the answers by it (the quick policy's pick breaks ties), at most 12 macros per move. Bundling the whole effect, not only its first prompt, also lets the tree plan on past an effect: a macro that ended on an open second prompt (Purple Enel's mode, then target) ended its branch of the tree, so the turn was re-searched from nothing at 25% of the budget after the pick and then preferred ending the turn. `discountPays` gained `turnStart` for the tree only: a Law discount that becomes "the only way" because the plan first gave DON!! away is not needed (Rosinante seed 1: the Warlord attacked with 2 DON!!, then discounted, a DON!! lost for nothing). Game-plan assertions are tier-parameterised: planted assertions run per push for the Pirate and the Warlord with the biases on, the Pirate also with search alone (the `BIAS_ONLY` runs, which are about what search alone finds); `scenario.ts` takes the tier from `setTier`, the self-play tallies take `PLAN_TIER`, and the Warlord's run nightly (`npm run test:plan-warlord`, biases on only). **Where the Warlord's search genuinely differs, the assertion was changed to what the seed claims, in the test and in the guide (never silently):** (a) Kalgara seed 1 reads the DON!! on the Leader at the attack, however it got there (the Warlord hard-casts Wyper and takes its gift); (b) Kalgara seed 2 checks the free play's pick is the highest-cost Warrior offered (the Warlord hard-casts OP15-101 first and trashes a card to dig, so one given card staying in hand is not the claim; the Pirate keeps the old field check); (c) OP13-001 seed 2: in the planted 9-DON!! position the Warlord's search prefers 7 DON!! on the Leader and a swing (it plays neither Sanji nor Luffy), so the test asserts the order when both are played and a second position with the Leader rested forces both plays; the guide says so (`OP08-098.md`, `OP13-001.md` "Warlord note"); (d) the group-2 `avoidBasePower` mechanics test sizes its "want" bonus per tier (4 for the Pirate, 1 for the Warlord, whose tree values a 9000 body about 2 units above not playing it), so the want minus the 5-point penalty is below search's own value either way; (e) the Kalgara "Why?" position drops OP15-114 from the hand, so the Warlord's give cites the guide in a position where it has no gift source. Result: all 12 playbooks' planted assertions pass for the Warlord (204 passed, 25 expected-fail, per run 15 s). **Open, found by an 8-game Warlord self-play tally (not a gate run; nightly will measure 40 and 64 games):** Kalgara seed 1 reads 9 of 13 against its 0.9 threshold: in the four misses the Warlord gave DON!! and attacked, then answered the free-play prompt with "done" (no Warrior) in late turns at 10 DON!!, which is what a search that counts the Life card the free play costs would do at low Life; and Kaido seed 2 (the leader's -2000 cutting the Counter needed) reads 17 of 24 against 0.8. Neither is fixed here: they need the 40- and 64-game numbers before a bias is sized, and a bias sized to 8 games would be tuning to noise. If the nightly confirms them, the choices are a `preferPick` bias on the free play, or a documented lower threshold for the Warlord with the reason in the guide.
- **M5b-2: the gates keep iteration budgets** (PLAN requires reproducible games), and say what they measure. The wrong "about 2.5 s" comment in `session.ts` is fixed, and the strength test header, ADR 0008 and benchmarks.md say the gates are a lower bound of the shipped Warlord: in ms mode the same time buys about 2,000 to 78,000 iterations (median 31,000) for Warlord and 5,000 to 170,000 for Emperor (review probe). The Emperor result describes the CI proxy, not the shipped Emperor.
- **M5b-3: the PLAN gate stays a point estimate (at least 65% over 200) on fixed seeds**, so it is reproducible. Every report prints the Wilson interval (133 of 200 is 59.7 to 72.7%: a pass by 3 games is not proof of 65%). The game-to-pair mapping changed so that 12 games cover all 12 ordered pairs (`g % 12`, seat `(g + floor(g / 12)) % 2`); the seeds are the same, so the 200 games are a different set from the 133/200 run and must be re-measured (nightly; not run here).
- **M5b-4: nightly timeouts fit what runs.** Each strength series has its own per-test timeout, twice the estimate from the measured cost per game (Pirate vs Rookie 0.75 s, Warlord vs Rookie 10.4 s, Warlord vs Pirate 25.5 s; 200 games) and its own CI job (`strength-m5b` is a matrix, 200-minute cap each; `plan-warlord` is a matrix of the four tally files, 150 minutes); the `property` job's cap is 150 minutes (estimate about 85: it was 15 to 30 before M5b and now also runs the Pirate tallies and four Playwright specs). Not dispatched; the caps are estimates until the first run.
- **M5b-5: `budget.test.ts` is broader and states its allowance.** Plan allowance stays (turn budget + lethal cap) x 1.2 (27% over the search budget alone for Warlord), said in the header; it also times a first Main decision with no plan, a re-search (a plan made at one position, a later position in the turn: 25% of the plan plus the lethal cap, x 1.2), two deck pairs, and Rookie (12 ms). The 120 ms wall-clock test in `warlord.test.ts` is replaced by an iteration count (a first Main decision spends exactly the turn budget; others at most the defence budget).
- **M5b-6: the Emperor copy** says "more than twice the thinking time; not measured to play stronger", not "for hard practice".
- **M5b-7: pacing.** 11 Warlord turns on ms budgets (three games, 2 to 11 moves): 3.5 to 10.8 s, median 6.2 s; 6 moves or fewer 3.5 to 6.2 s; 9 to 11 moves 10.2 to 10.8 s; the worker wait 1.8 to 4.1 s. No 15-move turn was produced (the late scenario ends in one turn). Setup copy: Warlord "typically 4-8 s a turn at 1x, up to about 11 s for a long one". board.md §5.2's 1.8 s of visible thinking and its estimated 9.2 s typical turn are replaced by the measured figures. **Emperor exceeds the 8 s turn target by design** (10 to 15 s, 3 turns measured): it is the opt-in slow tier, accepted as such and documented in ADR 0008.
- **M5b-8: guides and vetoes.** The OP12-061 guide no longer attaches `[SH-ROS]` to a line it deviates from: the T2 row and seed 1 say the playbook deliberately does not follow [SH-ROS] step 3. The 25-point penalties (`keepActiveDon` OP13-001, `playOnlyWhen` OP17-079) are intentional vetoes (`tanh(v / 12)` makes 25 about 0.97 of the range; only a win, worth 1000, overrides), documented in the `playbook.ts` header and on `keepActiveDon`. Seven seeds have no self-play cases (Robin 5, Kalgara 4 and 5, ST30-001 5 and 6, OP13-001 2, ST30-001 4 vacuous): carried to M10 in STATUS. No new A/B runs.
- **M5b-9: the per-push strength smoke is Pirate and Warlord vs Rookie, 12 games each, one per ordered pair** (at least 75% and 10 of 12); Warlord vs Pirate and the one-iteration controls are nightly. Measured locally: the Warlord vs Rookie test takes about 2 minutes for 12 games.
- **M5b-10:** `warlord.test.ts` plays a position (4 DON!!, a second Kyo in hand) where giving fewer than all is right: with the card the AI gives at most 1 DON!! per attack and plays it, with an empty hand it gives all 4. Closes the carried item.
- **M5b-11:** `SessionOptions.onCannotDeterminize` is called for every `CannotDeterminize` fallback; the strength gate counts them in `Series.cannotDeterminize`, reports them and fails on any.
