- **M5b-12:** the 40% re-search text is fixed (code is 25%). **PROMPT.md's tier table (10, 100, 1,500, 5,000 ms per decision) does not match ADR 0008's per-turn budgets (Pirate 500/250 ms, Warlord 2,500/400, Emperor 6,000/600); the ADR is right and is the one the code follows, PROMPT.md is not edited (owner's file).** The ADR addendum now says so.
- **M5b-13:** a declined effect's "Why?" now says what it keeps (its DON!! and hand) instead of "The effect is not used", end of turn says what the opponent now faces (its DON!! and hand to answer with), and the rail no longer shows "Last: X is thinking..." beside the thinking status. Layout nits (the long Warlord line widening the Opponent column; empty space at 1920) go to M10.
- Rejected: a new bias kind to keep the best card from Kalgara OP15-101's discard (the guide gives no list of "cards you cannot use"); raising bias sizes for the Warlord (would be tuning to the tests); dispatching the nightly here (the owner does it).

## 2026-11-17 — M5b Warlord and Emperor: determinized MCTS over macro moves, per-determinization statistics, a lethal check first

Context: ADR 0008 asked for information-set MCTS over turn plans. Decisions, each with what it was measured against (96-game same-archetype duels against Pirate, about ±9 points of noise; 200-game gates in docs/benchmarks.md). (1) **Nodes are macro moves**: a play or activation with its first prompt answered (every count of "give up to N DON!!", so the search can give fewer and keep DON!! for a later cost; three targets otherwise), or an attack after giving it 0..N DON!!, so the tree has no chance nodes and the AI's own moves are the only branching; progressive widening orders moves by the playbook's bias (`mainBias`, so every bias kind that is a move prior applies) then a structural rule. The merged playbook's `activateBefore` composites and `holdDon` filter are in the candidate list. (2) **Statistics per determinization, values measured against the root's mean in the same determinization** (control variate): without it a move visited under kind determinizations looked better. (3) **Reuse by consistency**: after the AI acts, determinizations whose projected view differs from the real one are dropped and the search continues from the node reached; nothing from a dropped determinization is used. (4) **Lethal first**: `lethal` in `public` mode before the search; a proven line is queued and played with no search; an error in the check counts as unknown (a position it could not analyse crashed a game: fixed in `analyse.ts`). (5) **Rollouts play Characters for both seats** (`SimContext.quickPlays`, Warlord only): 71.9% against 61.5% at 1,500 iterations. (6) Horizon is the end of the opponent's next turn (2); horizon 1 was no different. (7) **Emperor**: the same with 2.5 times the budget and iterative deepening as tree-depth caps over four stages of one budget. It showed no gain over Warlord (50%), as did 4,000 and 8,000 against 1,500 iterations for Warlord: **the limit is the heuristic and the rollout policies, not the budget**; Emperor is documented as longer thinking. (8) Re-search budget is 25% of the plan (was 40%; lowered for pacing, the 200-game runs used 40%). (9) The gates' deck pairs are cross-archetype; same-archetype pairs separate the tiers better but are not what a player meets. Found on the way: Pirate's `usedThisTurn` bias threw for a card that had left play (fixed). Rejected: a transposition table (3% net, `ai/bench/profile.ts`); expectimax fallback (not needed); a UCB constant or K sweep as a lever (inside the noise).

## 2026-11-17 — M5b group 3 playbooks (Sabo OP13-004, Purple Enel OP15-058, Rosinante OP12-061): seven additive bias kinds, a composite "activate, then play" move, measured thresholds

Context: three of the ten missing playbooks, written from the guides' "Game plan by turn" and "Playbook seeds" (`ai/playbooks/OP13-004.json`, `OP15-058.json`, `OP12-061.json`, registered in `ai/src/playbook.ts`). Every number is an inference (the guides give a direction, as for Rocks and Kaido); each bias carries the guide's line and citation field. Decisions.
- **Existing kinds first.** `play` (Saul and Loki by T4; Loki as the cost-18 enabler; the 1-cost openers; the Laws by T3), `acceptOptional` (Ohm, Shura and Satori's DON!! -1 draw), `attackWith` (Sabo's six 2-cost bodies, Enel OP15-118). Seats: Sabo goes first (S17 "aggressive curve-out"), Enel and Rosinante second (S17 default; the guides give no seat advice).
- **New bias kinds, each additive in `ai/src/playbook.ts`, `search/pirate.ts` and `search/why.ts`, with a score-difference unit test in `ai/test/bias-kinds-g3.test.ts`.** `activate` (use an ability from own turn N: Enel's leader surge, seed 1); `targetCanAttack` (a pick prefers an own Character that can attack: the surge's DON!!, seed 2); `giveDonLeader` (DON!! on Sabo once a cost-12 Character and 3 attackers are out, seed 2); `activateBefore` (Rosinante's discount before a Law of cost 4+, seed 1); `avoidTarget` (hold removal for real threats: Gum-Gum Lightning, Divine Departure, Sakazuki; `maxCost` 2 and, for Gum-Gum, `reach` 6000 from its card text, seed 4); and three optional fields on existing kinds: `acceptOptional.minLife` (the shield only at 2+ Life, seed 3), `attackWith.minDon` and `attackWith.card: '*'` (any Character carrying 3+ given DON!!: the loaded Character swings, Enel seed 2). `Why?` cites them from public facts only: `activateBefore` is not cited when the discount is activated (its reason, a Law in hand, is hidden) but is cited on the Law played after it (the used-this-turn list is public); `ai/test/why-differential-g3.test.ts` repeats the M5a differential with the three decks as the AI.
- **A composite candidate.** One rollout cannot value the discount alone (it is worth nothing until the Law is played, and the Law is often unplayable before it: 3 DON!! for a 4-cost). `mainCandidates` now also offers `[activate, playCard Law]` as one move for every Law in hand (only when the playbook has an `activateBefore` bias; moves that turn out illegal are already dropped by `score`). Search alone never activates the discount (0 of 86 plays), so the planted seed 1 and seed 5 tests are in the biases-only set. The `debug` hook now also fires for non-Main decisions (kind `'other'`), which the unit tests use to read a bias as the difference between two rankings on the same seed.
- **Thresholds are the guides', with three qualifications, each in the assertion text.** (1) Rosinante seed 3 (the shield at 2+ Life): a shield that loses the game in a quarter of the AI's own samples (accept's mean below −200) is not held to the plan, like a finishing turn; the bonus is 5.5 because a Life card at 2 to 3 Life is worth more than a Law in the heuristic (raw accept scores 2 to 6 points below declining). (2) Rosinante seed 5 (P-093 and EB04-038 with their DON!! condition met): judged when the leader's DON!! -1 can level the count, because a DON!!-cycling opponent (Enel, 1 to 4 DON!!) leaves the condition out of reach whatever the AI does; the guide's "going first helps" is wrong (each side has one more DON!! than the other's last turn left it, whatever the seat), so Rosinante goes second and only the discount closes the gap. (3) Rosinante seed 4 counts removal on cost-3+ targets; against Sabo's all-2-cost board the AI now holds the Event instead of firing it (penalty 2.0). Measured over Pirate-vs-Pirate games (deterministic seeds, three pairings Sabo-Enel, Enel-Rosinante, Rosinante-Sabo, both seats). Nightly config (`PLAN_GAMES=40`, passes; `npm run test:strength` now also runs `game-plan-g3.test.ts`), with biases (search alone in brackets): Sabo 1 25/27 (0.93), 2 22/25 (0.88), 3 8/8, 4 19/24 (0.79); Enel 1 113/113, 2 40/46 (0.87), 3 26/26, 4 69/69, 5 5/5; Rosinante 1 31/31 (relaxed wording below), 2 24/26, 3 32/35 (0.91), 4 3/3, 5 37/37 [18/47]. Thresholds: Sabo .7/.8/.8/.7, Enel .95/.8/.9/.8/.8, Rosinante .9/.6/.9/.8/.7 (the guides').
- **Seed 1 relaxed (lead's ruling), after a strength cost.** The first version asked for the discount before every Law of cost 4+ (the guide's literal [SH-ROS] step 3). Over 40 games against Rocks and Kaido (both Pirates), the playbook's biases on against off gave Rosinante 4 against 12 wins (Sabo 32 against 28, Enel 19 against 11); ablating one kind at a time showed only `activateBefore` mattered (13 wins without it; 6 with the composite move alone). The discount costs a DON!! on the field for several turns (it returns to the DON!! deck, the DON!! Phase still gives 2), which a Law's refund covers only when the count condition holds. New seed 1 (guide and playbook updated together): use the discount before a Law of cost 4+ when it refunds itself (P-093, EB04-038: own DON!! minus 1 at or below the opponent's) or is the only way to play that Law this turn; otherwise play it without. `activateBefore` gained `refundCards`; `discountPays` gates the composite move, the activation bonus and the penalty for playing first; and the bare discount is no longer offered as a move when no hand card needs it (rollouts scored it up to +2.3 over ending the turn, as a free extra turn, so a −4 penalty did not stop it). Measured: seed 1 31/31 at the nightly config (the tally counts a needless discount as a miss), other seeds unchanged within noise; Rosinante biases on 17/60 (0.28, Wilson 0.19–0.41) against off 15/60 (0.25, 0.16–0.37) vs Rocks and Kaido, so no longer worse. Rejected: following the dated source literally (unconditional discount), and dropping seed 1.
- Rejected: one `play` bias per seed for Rosinante (no seed-1 behaviour without the composite move), a per-seat DON!! rule for seed 5 (the card text makes it seat-independent), raising the shield bonus until every Life-2 shield is taken (it would give up a game-losing Life card for a Law, against the heuristic's own lethal check), and moving the "every seed has an assertion" check out of `game-plan.test.ts` (smallest change: seeds are numbered 1..n and there are at least four; the guide-seed count check for group 3 is in `game-plan-g3.test.ts`).
- **Merge with groups 1 and 2.** All kinds sit side by side. Overlaps left unmerged because merging would change measured behaviour: `giveDonLeader` (Sabo: DON!! on the Leader only with a cost-12 Character and 3 attackers) against group 2's `leaderDon` (every turn from N) and group 1's `giveDon`; `avoidTarget` against group 2's `avoidBasePower`/`preferPick`; `acceptOptional` carries group 1's `donDeckAtLeast`/`triggerInHand` and group 3's `minLife`. `mainCandidates(setup)` (the composite activate-then-play move) is wrapped by group 2's `holdDon` unchanged. Enel OP15-058 is imported as `purpleEnel` (OP05-098 is `enel`).

## 2026-11-17 — M5b group 1 playbooks: Nico Robin, Yellow Enel, Kalgara, Boa Hancock

Context: four of the ten missing playbooks (`ai/playbooks/OP09-062.json`, `OP05-098.json`, `OP08-098.json`, `OP14-041.json`), each written from its guide's "Game plan by turn" and "Playbook seeds" (docs/content/archetypes/), registered in `ai/src/playbook.ts`. Every number in them (weights, card values, bias sizes) is an inference, as for Rocks and Kaido; the guides give the direction. Game-plan assertions: `ai/test/game-plan-g1.test.ts` (planted positions, each run with the biases and with search alone, plus four "Why?" citation tests) and a Pirate-vs-Pirate run among the four decks (`npm run test:plan-g1`, 64 games, every ordered pairing, seeds 50,000 to 50,063, wired into the nightly workflow; `plan-tally-g1.ts` is the tally).

- **Existing bias kinds first.** Robin 1 to 4, Enel 1 and 3, Kalgara 3 use `attackWith`, `play` and `acceptOptional`. Planted tests that pass on search alone (so the bias only makes the line steadier in self-play): Robin 2 to 4, Enel 1 and 3, Kalgara 3 to 5, Hancock 2 and 3 (and the Life Trigger seed has no bias at all).
- **New bias kinds (small, additive, in `playbook.ts`, `pirate.ts`, `why.ts`, one condition helper in `search/conditions.ts`).** `giveDon` (a bonus for getting a DON!! onto the Leader, while it holds fewer than `upTo`; Hancock 1, Kalgara 1; the first version paid per step and gave all ten DON!!, so the bonus stops at `upTo`), `playOnlyIf` (a penalty unless the AI's own board or the opponent's Life meets a public condition; Enel 2 Linlin on a full board, Hancock 3 Shakuyaku at 3 or less Life, Kalgara 5 Kingdom Come with `never`: the penalty is paid on every move that does not win, and a win scores 1000), `playBeforeLeaderAttack` (Kalgara 4, Sweet 3 Generals before the Leader's attack drops Wyper), `attackWithPower` (Robin 5, a Trigger Character with 8000 power or more attacks; the power is derived, so it holds only while Linlin is out), `pickWithTrigger` (Hancock 4, Hiyori's pick from hand). Two options on existing kinds: `acceptOptional.donDeckAtLeast` (Robin's pitch adds nothing with an empty DON!! deck, RL-50) and `acceptOptional.triggerInHand` (Hiyori), and `targetHighestPower.by: 'cost'` (Kalgara 2, the highest-cost Shandian Warrior). Each has a planted test that fails with the bias off (the `BIAS_ONLY` list), and a citation rule in `why.ts`.
- **Citations stay public-only.** A bias whose condition is a card in hand or a face-down pick (`avoidPlay`, `pickWithTrigger`, `acceptOptional` with `triggerInHand`, Kingdom Come's `never`) is never cited; `playOnlyIf` cites only a play that meets its condition, and `donDeckAtLeast` is public. `game-plan-g1.test.ts` checks the cites render and that Hiyori's steps carry none.
- **Bias sizes were set by the planted and Pirate-vs-Pirate runs, and are large where the heuristic cannot see the line.** Robin's Leader attack 2.0, pitch 2.5, Yamato 3.0 (at 1.0 the pitch was declined in 17% of attacks; at 2.0 Pudding was played on T2 ahead of Yamato, which the guide puts on T2 with Pudding on T3), Linlin 2.0 and 8000-power attackers 2.0; Enel's Linlin penalty 3.0 and Linlin's card value cut to 0.3 (on an empty board search ranked her 2.6 above the next play); Hancock's `giveDon` 2.5 (the heuristic scores a DON!! left on the Leader about 1.9 below ending the turn, because the leader's effect fires on the opponent's turn, past the rollout); Kalgara's Upper Yard 2.0.
- **Measured over 64 Pirate-vs-Pirate games with the biases (without them).** Robin: 1 leader attack and pitch 71/73 (0/100), 2 Yamato 24/26 (12/28), 3 Pudding paid by T3 19/19 (19/19), 4 Linlin by T5 18/31 = 58% (9/31), 5 two 8000-power attackers 0/1 (not enforced; too few cases). Enel: 1 Pudding cost 45/45 (45/45), 2 Linlin with two Trigger bodies 22/22 (17/26), 3 rest-2 cost 50/50 (30/31). Kalgara: 1 attack with a DON!! and a free play 78/86 = 91% (59/88), 2 highest-cost free play 94/101 (42/75), 3 Upper Yard by T2 20/20 (18/20), 4 and 5 no cases with the biases (Sweet 3 Generals and Wyper never met in a turn; Kingdom Come was never played), so those two are judged only by their planted tests. Hancock: 1 DON!! on the Leader at the end of the turn 115/138 = 83% (65/139), 2 Life Triggers 48/48 (50/50), 3 Shakuyaku 4/4 (3/3), 4 Hiyori 11/12 (9/12). Every enforced seed (10 or more cases) meets its threshold with the biases. Robin 4 is the closest (58% against 50%; Shonen's own simulation, with perfect pitching, is 56 to 60%).
- **Unverified.** The guides' seed 5 for Robin has one case in 64 games, so only the planted test checks it. Win rates between the four decks (Robin 18, Enel 22, Kalgara 12, Hancock 12 of 64 with biases) are not a balance claim: the decks are the first sourced list of each, and the pairings include mirrors. No A/B of biases on against off was run for these four (the Rocks and Kaido A/B showed no strength cost; the per-push strength smoke still passes).
- **Shared-file edits.** `ai/test/game-plan.test.ts`: the "every seed has an assertion" check compared seeds with `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]`; it now compares them with `1..n` (the new playbooks have 3 to 5 seeds). `package.json` (`test:plan-g1`) and `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` (one step).
- **Rejected.** A bias on every Character attack of the Big Mom bodies (it would fire without Linlin; `attackWithPower` reads the derived power instead), and a per-seat DON!! check in `playBeforeLeaderAttack` for Sweet 3 Generals' rest-2 (search sees what the DON!! allow; the guide's 9-DON!! stack is a T5 line, not a rule the bias can state).

## 2026-11-17 — M5b group 2 playbooks: Luffy & Ace (ST30-001), Monkey.D.Luffy Red/Green (OP13-001), Monkey.D.Luffy Black (OP17-079)

Context: M5b needs a playbook and game-plan assertions for every archetype (ARCHITECTURE §4.6). These three were written from their guides' "Game plan by turn" and "Playbook seeds" (6, 5 and 4 seeds), in `ai/playbooks/<leader>.json`, registered in `ai/src/playbook.ts`. Decisions.

- **Six new bias kinds, all additive** (`ai/src/playbook.ts`, `ai/src/search/pirate.ts`, citations in `ai/src/search/why.ts`): `playOnlyWhen` (a [Main] play only while a public condition holds: an opposing rested Character of cost 7 or less, an opposing active Character or DON!!, or an own Character of cost 12 or more; optionally before the Leader attacks), `avoidBasePower` (never a Character of 7000+ base power), `leaderDon` (a DON!! on the Leader before passing), `keepActiveDon` (pass with 1 to 5 active DON!!), `spareDon` (a Leader attack takes every DON!! no Counter Event in hand needs), `preferPick` (an effect's choice goes to named cards), and no change to the existing kinds. Each has a mechanics test on a synthetic playbook (`game-plan-g2.test.ts`, "bias kinds, mechanics") with a control showing the move the bias changes.
- **`keepActiveDon` also prunes candidates** (`holdDon` in `pirate.ts`): a give of DON!! that would leave fewer than `min` active is not offered. Without it the search poured all 9 DON!! into one OP13-001 Leader swing (value 13 against 3 for the alternatives): the heuristic cannot see that this Leader defends with the DON!! it keeps. The play penalty is a rule, not a preference: 25 (past the search's noise; 0.8, 1.5 and 3 did not bind, measured 0.75 to 0.80 on seed 3 against 0.84 at 25). The same for `playOnlyWhen` on Luffy OP17-093 (25): at 3 and 8 the search still played him without a Giant in about a quarter of cases.
- **Searcher biases are 2.0 (Characters) and 3.0 (Thousand Sunny)**: the heuristic ranks a cheap searcher, and a Stage, below passing the turn on T1, so 1.0 did not move it. Sanji on T3 is 3.0 for the same reason (OP12-015 was played instead in 2 of 7 cases at 1.0). `attackWith` Leader from T2 is in ST30-001 (the guide's T2 row: "the leader attacks at 6000 even with no DON!!"); filed under seed 3.
- **Not done, kept honest.** OP13-001 seed 4 (Charlestone) has no bias: search alone already holds it (with 2 Life; at 5 Life taking the hit and its Life card in hand scores about level, which is the heuristic, not a plan failure). OP13-001 seed 3 misses are mostly Nami OP14-031's own "set up to 5 DON!! active" with 1 already active (6 or 7 active): the choice of how many is not biased. ST30-001 seeds 5 and 6 and OP13-001 seed 2 never came up in 40 games (no cases to measure); their planted tests and mechanics tests pass. ST30-001 seed 4 never triggers in the sourced lists (nothing has 7000 base power), so its self-play rate is 100% by construction; the planted test (ST31-004, 9000) is the real check.
- **Measurement.** `ai/test/game-plan-g2.test.ts`: every planted seed runs with biases and on search alone (`BIAS_ONLY` lists those that hold only with a bias), and a Pirate-vs-Pirate run over `PLAN_GAMES` games (the three decks against Rocks, Kaido and each other, seats swapped), wired into `npm run test:strength` beside `strength.test.ts`. The tally is `ai/test/support/plan-tally-g2.ts`. An assertion is enforced with 10 or more cases. The "every seed has an assertion" check reads each guide's numbered seeds, so a guide edit that adds a seed fails it. `game-plan.test.ts` only had its seed-count check narrowed to Rocks and Kaido.
- **Why?** Citations are public-only (opposing board, DON!! counts, a played card): `why-g2.test.ts` checks that each archetype's guide line reaches "Why?", obeys the word rule, names no hidden card, and that the explanation of every give, attack and end of turn reads the same whatever the AI holds.
- **Every number is an inference** (review M5a-09), as for Rocks and Kaido: weights (`don` 1.2 for OP13-001, `board` 1.15 for OP17-079), faceBias, counterAggression and cardValue come from the guides' framing, not from tuning.

## 2026-11-17 — M5b plan: playbooks in parallel with the Warlord search

Context: M4b is done (re-review 1 PASS). M5b's done criterion (PLAN §2): Warlord ≥95% vs Rookie, Pirate ≥75% vs Rookie, Warlord ≥65% vs Pirate over 200 games per pairing (nightly, iteration budgets, fixed deck pairs, swapped seats); every tier within budget +20%; game-plan assertions for all 12 playbooks. Decision: two kinds of work in parallel worktrees, because they touch different files. (1) Three playbook agents write the 10 missing playbooks (`ai/playbooks/<leader>.json`) from the sourced guides in `docs/content/archetypes/`, each with game-plan assertions on planted positions, played by Pirate. (2) One search agent builds Warlord and Emperor per ADR 0008 (information-set MCTS over turn plans, per-turn budgets, tree reuse, defence budget, lethal first), the tier gates and nightly strength runs, and takes the carried items M5a-10 (gates catch a too-small search), L-03 (search throughput) and the AI's choice of how many DON!! to give (M4b re-review). Then an adversarial review. Rejected: building Warlord first and playbooks after (playbooks are data and Pirate already consumes them, so they need not wait), and one agent per archetype (ten agents for data files is more coordination than the work needs).

## 2026-11-17 — M4b-19: face-up Life cards drawn with art, fanned, and inspectable like any public card

Context: re-review M4b-19 (severity 2) found the face-up Life tile (a cream name block) clashing with the art-first board, clipping long names, hiding all but the top name, being covered by the "N Life" plate, and being impossible to inspect. Decisions. (1) Each face-up Life card is its own `BoardCard` (new zone `life`, new `turned` prop that draws it landscape like the backs, without the "Rested" chip) placed over the Life button, so it gets the board's card art or the text-card fallback, the cost and Trigger plates, hover and focus preview, "I", Enter (card menu with Inspect), and for both seats. The Life button stays one item with the face-down count; face-down cards are not items, carry no handle in the DOM and never name themselves. A nested button would be invalid, so the face-up cards are siblings with their own roving indexes. (2) Fan: when any card is face-up the tiles are card-sized and the face-up cards fan on their own step (up to 0.34 card widths, among themselves, so a face-down card never eats the room) while the backs stay in the tight stack beneath. The card lower on the screen is higher in the stack, so every card shows its top strip with a name caption beside the cost plate; hover or focus raises one to the front. No layout shift: the stack stays inside the Life zone at 1280x720 and 1920x1080 (tested). Long names still end in an ellipsis in the caption (the strip is narrow at 720p); the full name is one hover or focus away in the preview, and is in the accessible name. (3) M4b-20: the "N Life" plate moved to the bottom-right, away from the captions; the "N face-up" plate is gone (the cards say so); the accessible name is "Your Nami, face-up Life card, Character, cost 1, Counter +1000, Trigger" (the board's pattern, with the zone words in place of active/rested); the cannotDraw log line now names the player ("You cannot draw with effects"), not the Leader. Rejected: a cropped art strip (a second art treatment to maintain; the rotated card face already reads like a rested card), and making the whole Life button inspect the top card (it would blur face-up and face-down). Unverified: real Bandai art over the new tiles (the e2e environment shows the text-card fallback).

## 2026-11-16 — M4b fix round 1 (engine and cards): the review's rules errors fixed, the owner orders their own bottom cards, empty picks skipped only where nothing is hidden

Context: the M4b review (`docs/reviews/m4b-review.md`) failed the milestone with rules errors M4b-01, -02, -03 and the engine half of M4b-05, plus severity 2 items. The AI and app findings are fixed in a parallel round. ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.4 (goldens re-recorded). Decisions:

- **`giveDon` "up to N" gives 0 to N (M4b-03).** ST01-011 Q&A ("Can I use this [On Play] effect to give only 1 rested DON!! card? Yes"), QA-GR and CR 4-8-1. Only existing decision kinds are used, so the app needs no new code: with more than one DON!! available a `choose` with `role: 'mode'` and options N down to 0 (the app already draws option choices; most first, so the first legal pick and the AI default give all), then the target `choose`; with one DON!! the target `choose` is `upTo 1`, so `done` gives none. A pick of `exactly 1` target in the script means "to 1 of your ..." and stays exactly 1 once a count above 0 is chosen. `each` keeps its M4b-wave-1 meaning (OP14-105). Rejected: a numeric decision kind (new UI), and a count asked after the target (a "none" then needs the target first for nothing).
- **E-Q11 (add or set active "up to N" DON!!) stays as it is.** The CR allows fewer, but more is never worse for the player and no meta card needs fewer; a count decision per add would only cost prompts. Recorded in OPEN-QUESTIONS.
- **Mandatory means mandatory (M4b-01).** The deck-top to Life `move` honours `exactly` (or no `pick`) like the other branches; OP05-098 is scripted `exactly: 1`. Every other deck-top to Life move says "up to 1" and is unchanged.
- **ST10-010 (M4b-02).** The player who activated the effect chooses the 2 cards face-down, as OP17-075: `chooser: 'you', blind: true` (the engine picks at random, so nobody is shown the hand). E-Q5 is closed for both cards in OPEN-QUESTIONS and effect-dsl.md.
- **Empty picks (M4b-05 engine half, M4b-15).** A pick over a hidden zone with 0 candidates asks no decision only when the whole zone is empty (a hand or deck size is public): OP17-091 and OP17-106 no longer show "trash 0 cards". With a filter, or looked-at cards none of which is eligible, the choice is still asked (max 0), because skipping it would tell the other player that no matching card exists (CR 8-4-4-2, ADR 0005): the review asked to skip a look-at-top with nothing eligible, but that is a small leak, so the UI answers `done` at `max 0` instead (the app half of M4b-05); a look at nothing (empty deck) is skipped. Unpayable costs (M4b-15) follow one rule already: unpayable on public information means the effect is not offered, an Event's optional cost is offered with "use" unavailable so the play is visible (B-1), and a hand-dependent cost is asked with "use" unavailable (ADR 0005). The one inconsistent case was the empty "trash 0 cards" choice, closed by the item above; nothing else changed.
- **OP08-115 (M4b-06) and OP15-119 (M4b-07).** The play of [Upper Yard] is inside the "If" (a "Then" after a failed "If" does not resolve; official OP14-111/112, OP15-116 Q&As; OPEN-QUESTIONS M4b-5 closed, all other cards were consistent). OP15-119's activation is `optional` (asked before the reveal) with a Life card needed to be offered.
- **Who orders cards placed at a deck bottom (M4b-08).** Official OP06 Q&A: the owner decides the order and the other player may not know it. When several of one player's cards go to the bottom of their deck by an effect (OP06-058), their owner gets an `order` decision whoever chose the cards. The cards still leave the field in a fixed order (by id), so the event stream is identical for every answer, and each is slotted above the ones owed below it (`Frame.rank/placed`, `MoveOptions.above`): the order cannot leak through event order. Tested for both answers (views and projected events identical for the other player). Cards from the two players (one each) have no order to choose. Before, the order was the canonical id order, chosen by nobody.
- **Leak property (M4b-09).** `engine/test/support/link-tracker.ts` holds the linkage check with the narrow exemption "a Life card revealed in place (a `CardRevealed` from Life) keeps its handle when it is later revealed again or moves": exactly that handle, spent by the next sighting, never for a card revealed from a hand. `engine/test/property/link-tracker.test.ts` plants each kind of real relink and proves it still fails (and a planted engine relink, `HIDDEN` cards keeping their key, fails the property at seed 1). Deck pairing: one game in three keeps a Rocks or Kaido side, the rest draw both decks from all lists, so M4b decks meet. The stub [Trigger] is added only to cards without a [Trigger] and no longer replaces real scripts.
- **Independent tests (M4b-13).** B-2 (OP15-032 rests an opponent's DON!!) and D-2 (OP17-095: one payment saves both Characters) were the engine being right; the `it.fails` tests were rewritten to assert that, with the Q&A cited, and the DISAGREEMENTS files record every ruling. The author tests that pinned "never fewer", "optional Enel" and "asks to trash 0" were updated.
- **M4b-17.** Notes in `golden.test.ts` and `record.ts`: a SCRIPTS_VERSION change alone allows a re-record, so bump ENGINE_VERSION by hand for every engine change.
- **Versions.** ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.4; goldens re-recorded.

## 2026-11-16 — M4b fix round 1 (AI and app)

Context: the M4b review (docs/reviews/m4b-review.md) failed on M4b-04 and M4b-05 (blocking) and listed M4b-10, -11, -12 and -16 for the AI and app. Decisions. (1) M4b-04: `battleNumbers` returns null when the battle names a card that is gone (a K.O.'d target whose [On K.O.] prompt arrives first); every caller already handles null. Any other error in `AiSession.decide` or `planTurn` now falls back to the rookie policy and calls `onFallback` (default `console.error`), so the worker no longer posts `error` for a search bug; the controller also plays the rookie policy on its own seat view when a worker request fails and the move is not forced, instead of throwing and stalling the game. A new test plays the review's repros (seeds 1, 2, 3) with `onFallback` failing the test, so a swallowed error cannot hide a regression; `PIRATE_SWEEP=<games>` runs a pairing sweep. (2) M4b-05 UI half: `needsDone(picked, max)` sends `done` while more picks are allowed and always when `max` is 0; the engine half (no 0-candidate choose) is the other agent's. The e2e uses `ask=1` because the default auto-skip hides a forced `done`. (3) M4b-10: face-up Life cards are drawn as themselves (name on a light tile, always above the backs, a "N face-up" plate) and named "Your Life, 3 cards, 1 face-up: Nami"; nothing changed in the projection, so the veil and the differential tests are untouched. (4) M4b-11: the look panel lists every looked-at card from `view.looking`, those the effect does not allow disabled with "can't be chosen for this effect". (5) M4b-12: `modifierWords` covers every modifier kind (its test is a `Record<ModKind, true>`, so a new kind fails typecheck, and a fallback string fails the test); deck to trash after a look is "Trash A, B from the top of your deck" (face up, public), hidden cards put back are counted and never named ("Put 2 cards back in your deck"; the event does not carry top or bottom, so the line does not either; an engine `position` field would let it say "bottom"), Life turned face-up names the card, and look to Life or add to Life show "Life 3 → 4". (6) M4b-16: a K.O. by an effect logs once (the following "trashed" move is consumed); with "Automated only" on, the readout adds "Automated only is on, so the list shows just those."; the stale EB04-007 "vanilla" comment is corrected (it is a scripted card with no [Trigger]; scenarios.ts had no such comment). Rejected: catching in `queries.card` (the engine's throw is a useful invariant), and a visible Life face with card art (the stack overlaps cards by about a tenth of their width, so only the top one could ever show it).

## 2026-11-16 — M4b perf: derive's permanent layer no longer recomputed per card on a draft; golden timeout restored

Context: replaying `meta-random-2.json` took 4.6 s in the golden test and the M4b archetype decks ran 333 (Sabo OP13-004), 648 (Luffy & Ace ST30-001), 1,101 (Enel OP15-058) and 1,104 (Hancock OP14-041) two-turn sims/s against ADR 0008's 1,000. Profile (`node --cpu-prof`, Sabo vs Rocks): 60% of the time in `derive`/`computePerms`, nearly all of it under `deriveLive` called by `fire` (the `negated` check of every card with an auto ability), which runs the whole five-stage permanent layer on a fresh memo per card because drafts are never memoised. Two separate findings. (1) The golden test's `measure` replayed from action 0 for every step (quadratic): a single replay of meta-random-2 was 46 ms (12.7 ms after the fix); 4.7 s was the test's own loop, not 12 ms per action. It now applies each action once to the previous state. (2) The engine cost was real. Decision: (a) negation depends only on stored `negate` modifiers (no permanent produces one), so `fire`, `collectReplacements` and the pending-effect negation check use `isNegated` instead of `deriveLive(...).negated`; (b) `computePerms` reads each definition's permanent abilities once (WeakMap, with a per-ability stage mask) and skips an ability, conditions included, at a stage it cannot contribute to (reading conditions has no effects); (c) `pool`, `matches`, `num` and `holds` on a draft share one layer memo for the duration of the outermost call (nothing is written while a selector or condition is read; `scoped` in `effects/eval.ts`). No behaviour changed: every golden replays to the same hashes without re-recording and `npm test` is green. Results in `docs/benchmarks.md`: 1,866 to 4,190 sims/s on all five matchups. Rejected: caching `deriveLive` across draft writes (needs a mutation counter on every `Draft` write path; error-prone), and keeping the 30 s golden timeout (the suite is now under 1 s).

## 2026-11-16 — M4b coverage in the browser: "plays fully" counts vanilla and keywords-only cards, "automated" does not

Context: M4b's done criterion needs coverage visible in the browser, and the Setup line counted by script only, so a Kalgara deck holding vanilla OP08-099 would have read as one card short. Decision: one definition, `coverage()`/`playsFully()` in `cards/src/coverage.ts`, used by the browser readout, the "Automated only" filter and the Setup copy: a card plays fully if it is scripted, or has no effect text, or only verified printed keywords. The readout and Setup say "play fully" and split the figure into "with a scripted effect" and "with no effect to script"; the word "automated" is kept for cards that have an effect and a script (the inspector chip "Effect automated", the filter name from flows.md, the tile's spoken name), and a vanilla card gets "No effect to automate", never "automated". The readout describes the scope as filtered but ignores "Automated only" itself, so the figure stays true while the filter is on; the meta-list line counts verified cards in that scope (all of them play fully). "Automated only" is a normal filter: `Filters.automatedOnly`, URL `auto=1`, a removable chip. Setup loads the two chosen decks' sets (`loadCards`, already used at game start) so it can classify cards; a card the data does not know counts as not automated and the line appears once the data is in. Also fixed: result tiles called `automation(card)` without the script set, so their spoken name could never say "scripted". Rejected: a generated set of no-script-needed ids in `cards/` (a second source of truth that drifts from the card data), and calling vanilla cards "scripted" to keep the old wording.

## 2026-11-16 — Golden records: a new SCRIPTS_VERSION also justifies a re-record

Context: the golden random games (`engine/test/golden/`) draw from the meta decks, so every newly scripted meta card can change how they play out. The re-record guard refused any changed result at an unchanged ENGINE_VERSION, which would have forced an engine bump per wave 2 merge for changes that are not engine changes. Records already carry `versions.scripts` (the script hash), and `isCurrent` (`engine/src/record.ts`) already treats a scripts change as a new version. Decision: the guard refuses a changed result only when both the engine version and the scripts version are unchanged. ENGINE_VERSION is still bumped for engine changes (the wave 2 Sabo merge does, for its `totalCost` fix). Rejected: bumping ENGINE_VERSION per merge (the version would stop meaning "the engine changed"), and dropping the random meta goldens (they are the only goldens that exercise scripts).

## 2026-11-16 — M4b wave 1: the effect runtime is complete for the 143 meta cards; permanent effects are a staged derive layer

Context: wave 1 of M4b (the entry below). The audit (`docs/research/m4b-runtime-audit.md`) found 34 shapes the 143 unscripted meta cards need that the M4a runtime did not run (it ran only what the 37 Rocks and Kaido scripts use). All 34 are now implemented, each with a planted test in `engine/test/cards/m4b/` on a vanilla card (not one of the 143), and each piece was reverted in turn (a one-line reversion per shape) and the new tests failed for every one except the Event-play branch of the `playCost` spend, which no test covers. Semantic choices, newest concerns first:

- **Permanent effects are computed, not stored (G1 to G6).** `derive` asks `engine/src/effects/layers.ts` for the modifiers every live permanent produces, in five stages (cost, base power, power, keyword grants, restrictions); a stage's conditions, targets and amounts read only the stages below it (`Ctx.lay`, `deriveAt`). This resolves the cycles the card pool creates without iteration: "if there is a Character with a cost of 12 or more, this gains +3000 power" sees +12 cost effects (OP17-080 and 12 more Elbaph cards), "base power of Characters with a [Trigger] and 4000 base power becomes 8000" (OP17-112) sees the printed 4000, "if you have a Character with 7000 base power or more" (ST30-001) sees base-power effects but not power ones. Several base-power effects: the highest applies (CR 4-9-2-1, as before). `tiers` entries are cumulative (OP15-092). A source with negated effects produces nothing (CR 8-2); hand-zone permanents apply to the card itself (OP07-064). Games whose cards have no such permanent skip the layer (`layered()`), so none of the 37 scripts changed. Rejected: a fixed-point iteration (unbounded, hard to cache), and writing permanents into `state.mods` (they would need invalidation on every board change and would appear in the hash).
- **Restrictions on a player, and one-shot discounts, are modifiers on that player's Leader** (`cannotDraw`, `cannotPlay` with a filter, `playCost` with `uses`). The Leader never leaves the field, so they expire with the ordinary durations and need no new state field. "Cannot draw cards using your own effects" (OP12-099) stops only that player's effect draws; the Draw Phase is a rule draw. "Cannot play Character cards with a base cost of 5 or more" (OP13-118) bars hand plays and effect plays. A `playCost` change applies to every pending matching change at once and is spent by the play it changed (OP12-061; E-Q2: an effect play pays no cost, so it neither uses nor spends it).
- **"Cannot activate [Blocker]" (ST21-003, OP07-057) is its own modifier (`noBlocker`), not [Unblockable].** The wording differs and a later card may read the keyword. `checkBlock` refuses the block with the CR 1-3-3 reason.
- **Removal protection (OP15-118)** is `Derived.removalProof`, read by `runKo` and `runRemove` when the cause is the opponent's effect. It does not stop battle K.O., rule trashing or costs; it stops K.O., bounce, deck placement and trash-from-field by an opponent's effect alike (E-D3).
- **"Will not become active in your next Refresh Phase" (G26)** is a `skipRefresh` modifier with a new duration `afterRefresh`: it is checked by `untapAll` and expires right after that Refresh's untap (6-2-4), after `untilStartOfNextTurn` modifiers (6-2-1) would already be gone. The duration belongs to the card's owner, whoever's effect it was (EB02-021 targets your own Character, OP13-040 an opponent's).
- **New event timings (G8 to G12).** `cardPlayed`, `eventActivated` and `blockerActivated` take `by` relative to the watching ability's controller; the turn player's watchers queue first (CR 8-6-1). `koed` watches the controller's own Characters and reads the K.O.'d card **as it now is in the trash, with printed values** (no last-known snapshot: nothing in the 143 depends on a power set by an effect; OPEN-QUESTIONS M4b-2). `lifeRemoved` and `lifeBecameZero` fire from the one place cards leave a Life area (`moveCard`), so damage, Banish, effects and costs all count; `lifeBecameZero` fires when the removal left 0.
- **Costs (G13 to G17).** `lifeToHand` (only n = 1, as every card is) is not damage, so no [Trigger] (QA-GR); "top or bottom" asks the owner; it is payable with at least one Life card. `turnLifeFaceUp` turns the top card, which must be face-down (payable only then: the CR does not say what turning a face-up card does; OPEN-QUESTIONS M4b-3); the card gets a fresh projection key and is public from then on. `mill N` needs N cards in the deck (deck-out is not a way to pay). `rest` costs rest only **active** cards (CR 1-3-2-1), may name the paying card itself, and include DON!! in the cost area when the selector lists `donCostArea` (chosen by class, E-D4). `move` as a cost places cards from a hand or trash.
- **Life moves (G18).** "Add up to 1 card from the top of your opponent's Life to its owner's hand" is asked as a yes/no (it reveals nothing, and "up to" may be declined); with `exactly` it is not. A card moves from a hand or trash to the top of Life face-down, or face-up when the script says (OP14-104).
- **Cards placed at the bottom of a deck "in any order"** (OP06-058, OP17-095's payload) go in the order picked: no `order` decision. The order is hidden afterwards and the only effect is on much later draws. `topOrBottom` (OP11-054) is one choice for all the cards, then they go in the order picked, as for `lookAtTop`. Rejected: an `order` decision per placement (extra prompts and AI branching for a hidden detail).
- **`giveDon` gives as many rested DON!! as the cost area holds, up to n, and never asks for fewer** (the M4a `addDon` precedent). With `each`, every target gets up to n; with fewer DON!! than targets the player chooses which targets (OP14-105).
- **`rest` of DON!! and cards mixed (G29)** asks one decision with the cards and one DON!! class (`counts`); an opponent's DON!! must be active to be rested. A DON!! count can be bound (`bind`) and read by `refCount` ("for every DON!! card rested this way", OP13-001; Q-T2 stays: one recipient).
- **`forEach` (G22)** counts once when it starts and runs its body that many times, each with its own choices. **`do` with `label` (G32)** records the label when the action did something (`Frame.acted`: a play, K.O., trash, move, rest or set-active of at least one card), so "If you do" after "Play up to 1 ..." (OP08-098) is false when nothing was played; after a "you may" the `may` label still means "accepted".
- **`activate` conditions are enforced (G34).** An [Activate: Main] with `conditions` is offered only while they hold (CR 8-3-2-3). Before, only autos checked them. Trigger and Event conditions are `if` steps, as before.
- **`rules` in a script (G7).** `alsoNamed` becomes `Def.alsoNamed`, which every `{ name }` filter honours (EB04-038); `donDeckSize` is read from the script as well as from the setup table (they agree for OP15-058).
- **Versions.** ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.2 and the goldens are unchanged (suite green, 1,216 tests that existed pass unchanged): none of the 37 scripts uses any new shape, the layer is skipped for them, and the one behavioural tightening (activate conditions) matches no existing script. Adding scripts changes `SCRIPTS_VERSION` only.
- **hidden.ts** needed no change: no new node kind was added to the DSL, only implementations and validator rules for existing ones. The projection tests (`engine/test/cards/m4b/projection.test.ts`) check that a card moving between secret areas (deck to Life, hand to Life, Life to hand) is not named in the opponent's events, and that one turned face-up or trashed is.
- **Not done, on purpose:** the app's log text for the new modifier kinds (the log omits kinds it does not know; M10 polish), and the Event-activation path of `playCost` (an Event with a pending discount spends it, but that branch has no test).

## 2026-11-16 — M4b starts without waiting for S1; the meta refresh moves after OP18; the runtime is built first, then the archetypes in parallel

Context: on resume, the next items are owner session S1 (the owner's, not schedulable by the run) and M4b. PLAN §1 makes M4b depend on S1, and risk 6 says "refresh before M4b". 143 of the 182 meta-list cards still need scripts (10 archetypes; Rocks and Kaido are done). Decisions:

- **M4b goes ahead before S1.** S1's findings are about the board and learning layer, which M4b does not touch (it adds scripts and runtime shapes). Findings are folded in when S1 happens. Rejected: idling until the owner is available.
- **The meta refresh is moved to after OP18 (released 2026-11-20) has data, and is done at M9 as planned.** The 36 sourced lists are 19 days old (the 60-day staleness flag is not near), EB05 has under three weeks of results, and OP18 lands in four days, so a refresh now would be redone within a month. M4b's done criterion is defined by the 182 gated cards. Rejected: refreshing now (wasted twice) and chasing OP18 mid-milestone (the plan forbids it).
- **Order: one runtime wave, then archetype waves.** A single agent audits all 143 cards against `IMPLEMENTED` (`cards/src/dsl-validate.ts`) and effect-dsl.md Appendix A and §5.1, then implements every missing shape in the engine with planted tests. Then archetype agents script cards in parallel worktrees, touching only `cards/effects/` and their own tests, with each card assigned to exactly one agent (first archetype by rank that lists it). Black-box tests come from separate agents, as in M4a. Rejected: archetype agents extending the runtime in parallel (every one of them would edit `engine/src/effects/run.ts`, and conflicting runtime semantics are worse than merge conflicts).

## 2026-11-09 — L screen-reader fix round 3: L stays on the board until the line, polite lines are spaced, the optional effect is described, and a mute NVDA invalidates the run

Context: the L NVDA pass r3 (`docs/design/a11y-passes/l-nvda-chrome-r3.md`, `-firefox-r3.md`). Chrome passed by the rule with three severity 2 findings (L-SR16, L-SR20, L-SR21). Firefox heard nothing at all: the speech tap joined, delivered no phrase, nothing flagged it, and step 6 failed on a harness key.

- **L-SR16, `L`, `L`, `L` could not be pressed in a row.** Every `L` moved focus into the coach (`Coach.tsx`), where NVDA owns `L` ("next list"). Decision: `coach.press()` no longer bumps the focus signal for tiers 1 and 2. Those are spoken politely (they already were) and focus stays on the board; the third press (and any `L` after it) moves focus to the tier 3 line, the one result worth reading with the virtual cursor. This amends learning-ui §2.1's "focus goes to that section's result" for `L` only, in the same sentence, and accessibility §3.12 (`L`, `C` rows) and §11.4 step 3. `H` and `W` are unchanged. The Lethal button (a click is a press of `L`) behaves the same, so focus stays on the button on tiers 1 and 2. Rejected: keeping focus on the board at tier 3 too (the ordered line is only reachable by browse mode or Tab, and a user who asked for the line should land on it); Tab or `C` to reach the result (a second key where one did it before); an `aria-live` copy of the whole coach (the tier lines already are the live text, and the full read is long).
- **L-SR20, polite lines lost when written within ~100 ms.** The controller wrote every polite line straight into one region (`say`), so the second replaced the first (the puzzle's "Solved" line 2 ms before "Rematch, or New game.", "You need 2 hits" 49 ms before "Playing from turn 9"). Decision: `PoliteQueue` (`app/src/play/game/politeQueue.ts`): lines go out at least 300 ms apart, in order, immediately after a quiet spell; more than three waiting lines are merged into the last (a fast AI turn must not make every line late); a reveal, rewind or dispose clears it. Assertive lines are not queued (a decision owed must not wait). accessibility §9.3 says so. Existing tests that read `ctl.polite` at an instant now wait for the queue to drain (`ai-controller`, `fixround`). Rejected: a longer settle delay before each write (delays every line, still races); merging every close pair into one sentence at the call sites (many call sites, and the coach and controller write independently).
- **L-SR21, the optional-effect decision was silent.** Focus lands on "Use" and NVDA spoke neither the prompt nor the cost. Decision: the prompt paragraph gets an id and is the accessible description of both buttons ("Use" also gets "Use it, or skip."), the same pattern as round 2's confirm. The names are unchanged, so the M3 script's optional step (it finds `#use-btn` and `#skip-btn` by id) is unaffected.
- **Severity 1.** L-SR13b: the coach's wrong-count reason pluralises ("1 hit", `ai/src/search/lethal/explain.ts`, wording only). L-SR13c: the DON!! More, Fewer and Move buttons in the guided count already had names but changed a number nobody heard; each now announces politely ("Kaido: 3 DON!!, attack 2."). L-SR22: `C` and the chevron announce "Coach open." or "Coach closed.". L-SR23: Confirm attack's sub-label ("5000 vs 5000") is `aria-hidden` (the description has the maths), which also removes the stray " ," in its name, and the brackets in "[When Attacking]" are dropped from the spoken sentence (NVDA paused on them). L-SR7 leftover: the review's "←" is `aria-hidden`. Not done: L-SR17, L-SR18, L-SR19, L-SR6 (need a manual NVDA check or a browse-mode probe), and the "Not built" list.
- **Harness.** (1) The speech tap marks itself dead on `close`, `end` or `error`; Guidepup's per-key capture is turned off only for a tap that passed the probe and is still up, so a mute tap can no longer turn the only other speech source off. (2) `Pass.speechProbe()` runs before step 1 of both NVDA scripts: a phrase the app never says is written to a live region and must be heard; after three tries the record carries "INVALID RUN" and "SPEECH TAP DEAD" (header and JSON `meta.invalid`) and the run stops (`A11Y_CONTINUE_WITHOUT_SPEECH=1` carries on for page evidence). A tap that dies mid-run is noted in the step where it was noticed. (3) The Enter-on-heading probe records that a real keydown committed the attack (Firefox without NVDA's click conversion) and skips the Tab probe and the user path instead of cascading into the Use step. (4) Step 6 uses Shift+Tab from "Keep playing" to "Concede". (5) F3 requires the skip link to be spoken before Enter (one retry; else "not reached" and no Enter). (6) F15's "STILL OPEN" now means focus on the waiting heading and `.` doing nothing; a working `.` is "does not reproduce". (7) Step 3 no longer needs the board-return assist for tiers 1 and 2 and records that focus stayed out of the coach. Unit tests (`app/test/a11y-harness.test.ts`) cover the probe and the capture rule.

## 2026-11-09 — L screen-reader fix round 2: the attack confirm no longer depends on a heading click

Context: the L NVDA pass r2 (`docs/design/a11y-passes/l-nvda-chrome-r2.md`, `-firefox-r2.md`) heard that round 1's L-SR1 fix did not work: Enter on the attack panel's heading did nothing in Chrome (focus stayed on the heading) and in Firefox focus went to `<body>`, so the click never reached `onClick` as a click on that element, and the `pointerdown` guess was not shown. Round 1's DECISIONS verdict ("fixed in code") was wrong for L-SR1; it is corrected in place. Decisions:

- **L-SR1 / FF-L1, structural.** When the attack confirm opens (from a target's Enter or a click) focus moves to "Confirm attack" (30 ms later, so it lands after the panel renders), and `M` during the confirm focuses it too (otherwise the heading, as before). The button carries `aria-describedby` to the sr-only sentence that was the live "confirm spoken" line, now richer: attacker and defender power, the gap ("Gap: ahead by 2000", "Level: ties go to the attacker"), what they need to survive and cards in hand, and "Enter to attack, Escape to go back". NVDA speaks name and description on focus, and Enter is a native press in browse and focus mode, so nothing depends on how a screen reader clicks a heading. The live announcement of that sentence is dropped (it would say everything twice). The heading's `onClick` and the `pointerdown` heuristic are removed: a click only focuses the heading. "Take the hit" is untouched and never the Enter target on defence (`primaryEnterId`). The panel text now says "Enter attacks (focus is on Confirm attack)", which is true where focus is. Escape from the button returns focus to the target (it was on the target before, and the button vanishes). Spec text changed to match: accessibility.md §3.5 item 3, §3.12 `M` row, §11.4 step 1. `M` still focuses the heading outside the confirm, so §3.12's "focus the battle panel" holds. Tests: `app/e2e/l-sr-fixes.spec.ts` (focus on the button, its description, Enter commits, `M`, heading click only focuses, Escape) and `app/test/hits-text.test.ts` (the sentence).
- **L-SR11 (Tab from the heading skipped Cancel and Confirm in Chrome).** Not reproduced: in Playwright's Chromium, Tab from the focused heading goes to Cancel, then Confirm attack (e2e `L-SR11` in `l-sr-fixes.spec.ts`, a regression guard, not a fix). The heading's Tab handler is correct; the cause is something NVDA does before or with the Tab that the page cannot see. With the fix above the path matters less (focus is on the button). The harness now records the event trace (below) after that Tab, so the next run shows what arrives. Left open at severity 2 until a run shows it.
- **Severity 1.** L-SR7: `→` is rendered as an `aria-hidden` glyph plus an sr-only "to" (`app/src/play/arrows.tsx`) in panel titles, buttons' sub-labels, battle results, the DON!! count line, the log and its Rewind and Why labels, and the live announcements are spoken with "to" (`spokenArrows`). L-SR12: the coach buttons no longer say `aria-keyshortcuts` L and C (NVDA takes both once focus is in the coach); each is described "The L key works from the board. Here, press Enter." and the visible "L" hints are `aria-hidden`. L-SR13: "1 hit" in the Lethal badge; "Check my count" grades and says only the question being asked. **Not done:** the reason text "0 plus 1 equals 1 hits" lives in `ai/` (`explain.ts`), which this round did not touch. L-SR6, L-SR8, L-SR9 left.
- **Harness.** (1) An event trace: a capture-phase logger (`addInitScript`) on the decision panel records pointerdown, mousedown, mouseup, click (with `isTrusted`, `detail`, `pointerType`), keydown, focusin and focusout, dumped into the record after Enter on the heading, after the Tab from it, and after Enter on Confirm attack. (2) The Firefox OS-focus guard is a keyboard canary: a window keydown counter and a harmless Shift press; a Tab that moves nothing asks it, and the loop stops (with an assist) when keys are not arriving. `focusPage` checks the canary and presses F6 until it fires, instead of a Playwright click, which does not give OS keyboard focus. (3) The Tab loops of `toCoachControl` and the board returns are short walks and then recorded assists. (4) 2b is seeded from the live-region history, so F15's defence no longer makes it a false "did not reach". F3 presses Enter on the skip link after Control+Home and checks focus lands in the decision panel (the dry run, with no virtual cursor, Tabs to the link first). `L` and `C` are pressed from the board. New steps: 5b (C and L from the board while the battle panel is up: L-SR2), 10 (Play from here), and an L after tier 3 in step 3 (L-SR10). (5) **F15's counterfactual is not available:** the AI answers an attack on timers that start at once, and K (pause) then `.` does not advance a paused AI (`nextMove` acts at Step pace only, although accessibility.md §3.13 says `.` advances "while paused or at Step pace"). That gap is recorded as an open question, not changed here.

## 2026-11-09 — L screen-reader fix round 1: Enter on the attack heading, coach reasons, review focus and keys, and a harness that says when it did not arrive

Context: the L NVDA pass r1 (`docs/design/a11y-passes/l-nvda-chrome.md`, `-firefox.md`) passed by the rule with a large gap: step 2's Enter on the battle panel's heading was NVDA's click, so the attack was never confirmed and 2, 2b, F15, 4b, 4c and the detector's "on" half were never heard; in Firefox focus left the page after a reload and 6 to 9 never ran. Decisions (all **fixed in code, not yet heard**: the r2 run on `a11y-nvda.yml` is what verifies them; app checks are `app/e2e/l-sr-fixes.spec.ts`, harness checks are the dry run):

- **L-SR1 / FF-L1, the heading click commits the attack confirm, and nothing else. (Corrected 2026-11-09, r2: this did NOT work. Enter on the heading still did nothing in Chrome and Firefox, and Firefox still left focus on `<body>`; "fixed in code" was wrong for it, a test with `el.click()` proved only that a script click is handled. Superseded by "L screen-reader fix round 2" above.)** The sentence "Enter to attack" is kept; Enter on the decision heading now does that. NVDA's Enter on a heading is a script click with no pointer event, so `Decision.tsx`'s heading `onClick` commits `#confirm-attack` when the click had no `pointerdown` in the last second and the panel is the attack confirm (`kind` main, `ui.kind` confirm). A mouse or touch click on the heading (it has a `pointerdown`) only focuses it, as before. Defence ("Take the hit"), picks and everything else are untouched: Enter alone still never commits a hit (§11.3 step 9, C8). Focus is put on the heading before the commit, and the focus rescue covers the rest, so it is never left on `<body>`. Rejected: rewording the panel to "Tab to Confirm attack" (a screen-reader user's Enter on the target already works, and the heading is where `M` puts them); committing on any heading click (a stray mouse click would attack).
- **L-SR2.** While the decision panel is tall the coach is a strip. `C` now says why (polite: "The coach opens when this decision is done. Finish or cancel it, then press C.") instead of nothing, and the third `L` appends "The numbered line is in the coach, which opens when this decision is done." L-SR10 rides along: an `L` past tier 3 says "That is everything the coach has for this turn." ahead of the line, so Chrome is no longer silent. Rejected: letting the tall panel carry the ordered line (the panel is already the tallest thing in the rail).
- **L-SR3.** The review timeline is wrapped in `role="application"` (roledescription "turn timeline", label naming Left, Right and N), the decision footer's pattern from the M3 fixes (F2), so NVDA's browse mode hands the arrows and `N` to the page. Every action also works by Tab and Enter (the first turn and every flagged turn are tab stops; the Flags table's buttons select a flag). Choosing a flag by `N`, Shift+N, the timeline or the table is said in a polite live region ("Flag 1 of 1 selected. Turn 9, your turn: missed lethal, ...") as a fresh child node each time, so `N` with one flag is no longer silent. Not done: the timeline as an ARIA toolbar (still a list of turn buttons).
- **L-SR4.** The review screen focuses its heading (`#rv-title`, `tabIndex -1`) when it mounts, so "Review this game" and "Back to the review" never leave focus on `<body>`. "Practice this" and "Play from here" focus the new board's decision heading after it mounts and announce ("Practice: turn 9 as it stood. Nothing is timed. Find the win you missed." / "Playing from turn 9, as it stood."). The cause was a whole-screen swap: `useFocusRescue` only helps when an element leaves a mounted game, and a new `<Game key=...>` starts with nothing tracked.
- **L-SR5.** The board's label ends ", read-only" when the game is a restored Study position, and `Q` appends "Read-only: nothing here is played."
- **Severity 1.** Done: the "←" in Study's "Overview" button is `aria-hidden` (L-SR7 in part), and L-SR10 as above. Not done: L-SR6 (Chrome's run-on cells), the rest of L-SR7 (the panel title "→" is a plain string tests and the live log use), L-SR8, L-SR9, F3's skip-link polish (the harness now presses Enter on the link and checks focus lands in the decision panel).
- **Harness.** `Pass.reached()` records "HARNESS: did not reach X" and the dry spec fails on any. Step 2 presses Enter on the heading, and if the attack is not confirmed it records the app finding and Tabs to "Confirm attack". The dry reader now clicks (not presses) Enter on a heading, as NVDA's browse mode does. `Pass.until` stops when `document.hasFocus()` is false (Firefox's toolbar) and `Pass.focusPage()` clicks the page's corner after every goto (steps 6 to 9). The F3 step presses Enter on the skip link. Steps 3, 4b, 4c and 5 check that they are in an idle Main Phase, that the coach went tall, that the count was answered and that the detector select reads "2".

## 2026-11-09 — M4b entry items: typed hidden-zone walk, optional-cost check, M4a-19 guards, M4a-10 engine gaps

Context: STATUS "Next" item 3 lists what must be done before M4b scripts can reach latent gaps. Decisions:

- **M4a-17, the veiled Use check is a typed walk (`cards/src/hidden.ts`, `abilityReadsHiddenZone`).** It replaces the regex over the script's JSON in `veiled.tsx`. Every Action, Step, Cost, Condition, Num and Continuous shape is classified in an exhaustive switch (`assertNever`), so a new DSL shape is a compile error until it is classified. A selector counts when any zone of `from` (a string or an array) is `hand`, `deckTop`, `lifeTop`, `lifeTopOrBottom` or `looked`; `lookAtTop`, `reveal` and moves into a hand, deck or Life count too. Numeric counts (`{ hand: 'you' }`) stay public. Run over the 37 scripts it agrees with the old regex on every ability, so no behaviour moves. The engine's own `secret` flag was rejected as the source: it is set only for cost shapes (`costIsSecret`), not for effect reads, and the veiled panel needs the public script shape before any decision exists. `readsHiddenZone` takes an optional script map so a test can plant a shape.
- **The "you may" cost check (`cards/src/optional-cost.ts`, `cards/test/optional-cost.test.ts`).** Per ability, from the card text: Event `costOptional` equals "you may" before the colon; an auto with a cost needs "you may" in the text; an auto with no cost has no "you may ... :" clause; a replacement's `optional` equals "you may" in its sentence; a cost made only of DON!! −X is not judged (always optional, RL-52). **No mismatch was found in the 37 scripts.** The four non-Event [Activate: Main] abilities worded "You may" (EB04-032, OP17-044, OP17-054, OP17-061) carry no `costOptional`, and the check requires that: on those it would make the cost declinable and an unpayable activation legal (`optionalCost`, `actions.ts`), contradicting the 2026-10-29 B-1 decision that choosing to activate is the consent. The check rejects `costOptional` on a non-Event activate for that reason.
- **M4a-19, four fix-with-no-test items.** Tests now fail on revert: `runtime-shapes.test.ts` (deck-top to trash is an `Anomaly`, no Life card; deck-top to Life still works), `OP17-049.test.ts` (neutral `ModeChosen` labels), `records.test.ts` (keys and batch counters out of the hash, batch grouping and `known` in, by position), `app/test/setup-copy.test.ts` (`automationLine`: counts different card numbers, names the seat, never says "automated"). Each fix was reverted in turn and the new test failed.
- **M4a-10a, one removal event, one replacement offer.** Removals made by one effect over several Characters (`ko`, a move off the field) carry a shared `group` on their `ko` and `remove` frames. Accepting a replacement applies its payload once and cancels the replaced removal of every other Character of the group it covers (one `ReplacementApplied` per Character, one DON!! return); declining marks it considered for all of them, so it is not offered again. QA-OP15 OP15-069 ("one DON!! return keeps both") is now tested. `group` is a counter, so the hash maps it to the position of the first frame in its group. Rejected: grouping by effect frame (a removal batch can also come from several steps of one effect, which CR treats as separate events).
- **M4a-10b, one End Phase batch per player.** `endTriggers` allocates one batch and fires, for the turn player then the non-turn player, that player's [End of Your Turn] or [End of Your Opponent's Turn] autos and then that player's delayed "at the end of this turn" effects. The turn player therefore gets an `order` decision across their own, and all of theirs come before the opponent's (CR 6-6-1-1, 8-6-1). Before, the three groups were separate batches, so the order was fixed by code. `fireDelayed` now takes a controller and the batch.
- **M4a-10c, delayed effects skip the source-negation check.** `drainPending` applies the CR 8-2-1-1 check only to entries without `start` (those are queued autos, not created delayed bodies). A delayed effect is created by an earlier resolution, so negating or losing its source afterwards does not stop it.
- **Versions.** ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.2, and the golden records and hashes are unchanged (the engine suite and goldens pass): none of the 37 scripts has two End Phase effects for one player, a multi-removal against a replacement, or a negated delayed source, so no recorded game plays out differently. The first M4b script that does will need the bump and a re-record, by the convention in `record.ts`.

## 2026-11-09 — The 320 px rail wins over bigger cards at 1280 wide; S1 scenarios planted

Context: preparing owner session S1, the lethal task's position at 1280×800 (a common laptop window) showed no coach at all: the board's cards grow with the window's height, so the board took width and the rail fell to 280 px, under the coach's 320 px minimum (`Game.tsx` `coachRoom`). 1280×720, 1366×768 and 1440×900 were fine; 1280×800 and 1280×900 were not (264 px at 900). Every test viewport is 1280×720 or 1920×1080, so nothing caught it. Decision: in `computeLayout`, for a window at least 1280 wide and taller than 720 (not the roomy or peek profiles), the cards shrink back, never below their 1280×720 size, until the normal fit picks the first (320 px) rail. At 1280×800 the cards are 93×130 instead of 95×133. Test: `app/test/layout.test.ts` (1280 by 721 to 1024, and 1366/1440/1536 by 900: rail 320, cards at least the 720p size). Rejected: lowering the coach's minimum to 280 (board.md sizes the coach at 320 and its longest strings were tested at that width), and a coach that overlays the board (learning-ui §2: the coach never covers the playmat). S1 scenarios: `ub-defend-1`, `ub-lethal-1`, `ub-keyboard-1` in `app/src/play/setup/usability-scenarios.ts`, every board card a scripted Rocks or Kaido card, the lethal one proven against any hand by test; the kit is `docs/design/usability/S1-kit.md`.

## 2026-11-09 — L screen-reader script built; F3 and F15 re-checked, not changed

Context: the L pass (accessibility.md §11.4) needed a deterministic harness, and the M3 NVDA r4 records left F3 and F15 open at severity 2. Decision: `app/a11y/src/l.ts` (with the dry, NVDA and VoiceOver specs) runs the nine §11.4 steps plus re-checks of F3 and F15, steps 4b and 4c for "Count it with me", and the workflow gains a `script` input (l, m3, both). Steps 1 to 5 run on a new scenario `a11y-l`, with Rookie as the AI: it always counters a lethal attack, while the Pirate AI sees the game is lost and does not, so step 2 would have had no defence to read. Steps 6 to 9 reload `lethalDemo`, the planted missed-lethal game of `learning.spec.ts`, concede it by keyboard and open its review; there is no URL for a finished game. Where §11.4 expects something not built, the step records "Note: not built" (the AI Counter sentence with totals and the tie rule, a "Coach alerts" setting and "Lethal available" line, the timeline as a toolbar, focus moving to the explanation when Study opens, "Declare no lethal"). **F3 and F15 were looked at and not changed in code.** F3: the app focuses nothing on load (Chromium's first Tab from the top reaches the skip link, which `a11y-focus.spec.ts` checks), so the NVDA-only result (Control+Home puts the browse cursor on the skip link, then Tab goes to the heading) has no cause visible from the page. F15: Step pace holds the AI, and enables "Next move", only after the AI's own Main moves (`stepWaiting`, set in the controller after a `main` action). While the AI answers the player's own attack it moves on timers, so a `.` there has nothing to do, and the decision panel has no "Next move" button, so the focus rescue lands on the heading. Showing a disabled "Next move" there was tried and dropped: it would only move focus onto a button that does nothing. A real fix needs a choice (hold the AI's defence at Step pace, or give the player something to do there) that the NVDA run should inform; the L script re-checks both findings. Alternatives rejected: making steps 6 to 9 depend on the game played in steps 1 to 5 (it differs from the planted record, so the flag and the line would too); adding the missing §11.4 features here (out of scope for a harness task).

Context: L re-review 2 (`docs/reviews/l-review.md`, "## Re-review 2") passed L only because the reviewer re-graded L-03 from 3 to 2: Unknown is 34.8% of searched positions (target about 30%) and 20.7% of all Main decisions (was 72% and 26%), 58% at a turn's first decision (was 90%), and on Unknown the coach now states the public defence bound. Decision: accept the re-grade and mark L built-and-reviewed, because every answer is sound (0 broken, 0 bound-agreement violations, 0 leak differences in independent harnesses) and Unknown is honest and informative; the remaining gap is search speed on late-game Kaido boards. Carried: L-03 (sev 2) to M5b (search throughput: an incremental state key instead of `summaryHash`), L-16 (sev 2: 9 of 13 bound mutations and the reduced pass survive the suite; add planted tests, including the reviewer's `addDon` case, and make the L-12 test assert `proven`) to be fixed before the screen-reader pass, L-17 (sev 1) and L-15 to M10. Rejected: a fifth fix round on L-03 now (four rounds moved it 72% → 35%; the next lever is engine-side and belongs with M5b's search work).

## 2026-11-06 — L fix round 2: a search-free refutation, draws in the attackers bound, honest Trigger wording

Context: the L re-review (`docs/reviews/l-review.md`, Re-review 1) failed on L-03 (72% Unknown among searched positions), L-12 (the attackers bound refuted a turn that wins by a draw) and L-13 (the Trigger caveat said "any hand" on a hidden-info proof). Not decided here: a **proof** without search (see "Still open").

- **The refutation bound (`ai/src/search/lethal/bound.ts`, `refutedByBound`).** Reduce the defender to its hidden hand only, every card a plain +P Counter (a mode the placeholder always has, at no cost); ignore Blockers, board effects and the cards each hit gives it. That only weakens it, so a lethal that fails against it fails against the real defender. With `R` attacks landing out of `m` possible and `L` Life, `need = L+1` (`ceil(L/2)+1` with Double Attack) attacks must land, so `k = m - need + 1` must be stopped. The reduced defender is an online strategy: it stops an attack whenever stopping costs at most `t = floor(H/k)` cards, so `k` stops fit in `H` cards. What is left is `need` attackers each facing at least `Leader + P*t`. The attackers' power comes from `attackerPotential`, an over-estimate read from the typed scripts of every card they can reach (never text): attackers and their power, DON!! (with `addDon`, `giveDon`), a permanent per-attack gain (`gain`), and the power the used abilities add in all over the turn (`gainTotal`, shared out over attackers: sum of max(0, a_i - g_i) >= max(0, sum a_i - sum g_i)). If the shortfall in DON!! exceeds the DON!! there is, no lethal. Any op, form or `kind` the scan does not classify returns `null` and the search runs.
- **Defects of the first draft, fixed.** (1) Opponent discards were "neutral": X.Drake (OP17-075) and Linlin's trash mode shrink the defender's hand, so the bound could refute a real lethal. `trashFromHand` by anyone but its owner now adds `pick x multiplicity` to `handLoss`, and `H - handLoss` is used. `mill`, `trashLifeUntil` and `skipRefresh` left the neutral list (no script uses them; unknown means search), a `move` is classified by where it takes from, and a `restrict` is neutral only for the attack-side kinds (a barred Counter or Event play is not). Audited against all 37 scripts. (2) Copies were counted once (a `Set` of card ids): two Torches add twice, two Drakes take two cards; the scan is per instance. (3) An unknown ability `kind` bails. (4) `ai/bench/scratch-*.ts` deleted. (5) Round 3: "an [On Play] counts once per instance" was false: OP17-056 returns a Character to hand (X.Drake can then be played again), OP17-052 returns an Event from the trash to hand. `bouncesOf` counts, per card, the moves off the board or out of the trash into the hand, K.O.s not aimed only at the opponent and `trashSelf` costs (with the same multiplicity as the scan); with `B` of them every ability's multiplicity is multiplied by `(B+1)^2` (a bouncing Character is itself replayed), `B > 4` or a bouncer in the trash gives no bound, and `B` extra attacks are added to the attackers' reach (a Rush Character can attack again). Planted test: Drake alone refuted, Drake plus OP17-056 not; fails with the multiplier removed.
- **L-12.** `attackReach` has a `deckKnown` flag. In `actual` mode (review, the full state) a hand or board card that draws or searches counts the whole deck as reachable, and the search plays draw and look-at-top cards (`Cap.deckKnown`; they are idle only in `public`, A3). In `public` the deck stays hidden and a draw finds a blank (A3, as before), so a refutation by count while a card the seat holds draws is labelled `drawsIgnored`: certainty `needed-hidden-info`, never `any-hand`, and the text says "from what you hold" and "Cards you draw are not counted". Rejected: turning the bound off whenever a draw card is in reach. It was the first draft and it made the bound answer almost nothing (Rocks's own Leader draws), which is the opposite of the milestone.
- **The reduced-defender pass.** After the bound and a short try at the cheap path (15% of the cap), `public` searches the same position against the reduced defender (every hidden card only its plain mode, no Counter that rests or draws, no type-for-cost) for 30% of the cap. A refutation there is a refutation against the real defender (it has strictly more answers); a proof or a cap hit is thrown away. Reason `reduced`. The short cheap-path try keeps E01 and E20 at 11 ms and 100 ms (without it E20 went from 100 ms to 700 ms).
- **L-13, L-14.** The Trigger note follows the certainty: `any-hand` "Works against any hand, unless a Life card's Trigger stops it"; otherwise "Depends on their hand, and a Life card's Trigger could stop it". The polite line is `tier1.sr`; tests cover both certainties, the reviewer's Sabo case and the app's polite line. The `lethal-games` gate is now (proven + refuted) / searched over searched positions only, over 6 games. At that test's 8,000-node cap the measured rates are 28% (actual) and 13% (public, 7 of 52), so the floors are 22% and 10%: regression floors, not the L-03 measure (`rate.ts` is). An earlier 2-game sample (6 public positions, 67% decided) was luck.
- **Measured** (`npx tsx ai/bench/rate.ts`: Rookie v Rookie, Rocks and Kaido in all four pairings, 3 seeds, 1,181 Main decisions, `public`, 1,500 ms, Node). After round 2 (before the replay accounting): attackers bound 706, bound 104, reduced pass 126, search 6, proven 26, Unknown 213 of 475 (45%; was 72%), 18% of all decisions. **After round 3 (replay accounting, final):** answered by the attackers bound 480; refuted by the bound 31; by the reduced pass 322; by the search 12; proven 26; **Unknown 310 of 701 positions that needed more than the attackers bound (44%)**: Rocks 106 of 313 (34%), Kaido 204 of 388 (53%); first decision of a turn 105 of 152; median 449 ms. Of all 1,181 decisions Unknown is 26% (the baseline's 26%). The replay accounting (`bouncesOf`) is the cost of soundness: the attackers bound answers fewer positions (706 to 480) because a script that can return a Character or an Event to hand now adds attacks (only when a Rush source exists) and repeats. **Target (under about 30% of searched) not met.**
- **Round 4, throughput and cap split (no new proof path).** A CPU profile of a hard Kaido position showed 62% of the time in `summaryHash` (the engine's semantic hash, taken at every node for the transposition table). (1) The search now memoises only the attacker's own decisions (`Searcher.solve`): states inside a defender's answer chain rarely repeat (interchangeable cards are one answer), and hashing one costs more than expanding it. Node throughput went from about 27k to 57k a second; results are unchanged (memo is only a cache). (2) `engine/src/hash.ts`: `hashString` computes both cyrb53 digests in one pass (identical output; the engine suite passes) and `canon` skips empty arrays. (3) Cap split from measured times: cheap path 10%, reduced-defender pass 85% (refutations there need 25k to 130k nodes; the full search after it almost never decided anything the pass did not), then the rest. Measured at each step (Unknown, of 701 searched): 310 (44%) before; 280 (40%) with the memo change; 267 (38%) with a 60% share; 248 (35%) at 80%; **242 (35%) final**, with the hash change. Final table: answered by the attackers bound 480; refuted by the bound 31; by the reduced pass and the search 402; proven 26; Unknown 242 of 701 (**35% of searched; target under about 30% missed**): Rocks 87 of 313 (28%), Kaido 155 of 388 (40%); **20.5% of all 1,181 decisions** (was 26%); median 238 ms; E01 12 ms, E20 95 ms. Tried and not useful: counting their active Blockers in the bound (free stops; only 2 of the 310 Unknowns refuted; it is kept, argued in `bound.ts`: an attacker needs `need + free` attacks that each get past `t` cards), a fixed-order proof plan and a closed-form proof (see round 3). What is left are positions the reduced search refutes only after 250k to 700k nodes (4 to 12 s), or never; more throughput (the hash is still about 45% of the time) is the next lever, or a state key that is not a JSON of the whole summary.
- **Soundness evidence.** `lethal-sound.test.ts` at 160 positions x 4 hands: 85 proven, 316 replays, 0 broken (this round adds no proof path). `lethal-bound.test.ts`: the bound-agreement property (wherever the bound or the reduced pass refutes, `bound: 'off'` at 120,000 nodes must not prove) and a hidden-information differential over re-rolled hidden cards of both seats, including bound refutations.
