Mutations: a bound that doubles the Counter fails the property and two planted tests; dropping `handLoss` fails the planted Drake test (the random property did not hit it: the planted case is what guards it); counting copies once fails two planted tests.
- **Round 3, a sound proof path, tried and dropped.** Two ideas were measured on the 213 Unknown positions. (a) A fixed order (the strongest `Life + 1` attackers, weakest first, the fewest DON!! per attack found by bisection, each attack checked against every defender answer by `expand`, like `greedy`): the check of one attack against a Kaido hand of 5 to 8 cards exceeds 60,000 nodes, so it proved nothing and cost 15% of the cap; removed. (b) A closed-form proof (every attack above their Leader by `maxPerCard x (hand + hits so far)`, Blockers counted, no Counter that rests or draws): Kaido's prior has rests and draws, so all 135 Kaido Unknowns are ineligible; of the 78 Rocks Unknowns 44 have fewer real attackers than hits needed (the lethal, if any, plays or untaps first) and 33 need more DON!! than there is; one fits. Not worth a proof path whose argument has to cover every defender board effect. L-03 stays open at 45%.

## 2026-11-05 — L re-review 1 (app part): Unknown states the public defence bound; two fixes get tests

Context: `docs/reviews/l-review.md` Re-review 1 failed L (L-03, L-12, L-13 open; the ai/ builder owns those). App-side:
- **L-03 (what Unknown can say).** When the check doesn't finish, the coach adds one line built from the result's public facts: their hand size times `maxPerCard` (the `defenceBound` the search uses, DON!!-aware), active Blockers, and whether their Counters can rest or draw. Public facts only, so no leak. Rejected: a bigger cap on "Check again" (it costs the turn's pace and doesn't fix the rate).
- **L-14 (app mutations that passed).** A unit test asserts `theyCouldHave` itself carries the Counter extras for a Kaido defender. An e2e removes a focused coach control outside React, so only the `focus.ts` rescue can act (it fails 8 of 8 with the rescue off).
- **L-15 (sev 1, DON!! math tag ignores [When Attacking] boosts and Blockers):** carried to M10 polish.

## 2026-11-05 — L fix round (AI half): sound hand costs, a cheap proof path, a conservative attackers bound

Context: the L review (`docs/reviews/l-review.md`) failed the milestone on L-01 and L-02 (false claims) and L-03 (the detector rarely answers). This entry records the AI-side fixes.

- **L-01, the attackers bound is decided from keywords and scripts, never text.** `attackersPossible` counted a hand Character only if its text had "[Rush", so EB04-030 ("gains Rush", printed without brackets) was missed and a lethal turn read "No lethal, any hand". It now counts legal attackers, hand Characters with the printed Rush keyword, and for every card the seat can reach (hand, Leader, Characters, Stage) the most attacks its script could add: cards it untaps (`setActive`), plays (`play`) or gives Rush (`grant`), each as many as its pick allows (a `forEach` counts 5); Double Attack comes from keywords or a granting script. So the bound can only err toward searching. The earlier A7 sentence ("errs only toward searching … a rare lethal could show as refuted") is replaced by this: it errs only toward searching. Planted test: Kaido Leader, EB04-030 in hand, 10 DON!!, Rocks at 1 Life with nothing: proven (`public` any-hand, `actual`), the line wins when played, and `reviewMissedLethal` flags the turn.
- **L-02, the placeholder pays whatever a real prior card could pay.** The hidden hand is now a placeholder Event carrying the union of every type and colour in the defender's prior, so "trash 1 card with a type including Rocks Pirates" (Shiki) can be paid and Shiki's −3000 fires. Costs and filters the model cannot meet with one card (a name, a category, a Counter value, distinct names, a total cost in a `from: hand` selection on a card of the defender's public board) are model gaps. A prior card with no data, or a prior Event with no script, is a gap too. A gap drops a proof from `any-hand` to `needed-hidden-info` (chip "Depends on their hand"); review then calls it hindsight, not severe. Derived from the prior's scripts, not by hand (`profile.ts`, `handCostGaps`).
- **L-03, DON!! costs are modelled, and proofs are found by a cheap path first.** The defender's DON!! is public, so a Counter that costs DON!! −N is a mode guarded by "N or more DON!! on your field" that returns N DON!! (the engine checks and spends the real DON!!). Plain +power modes are collapsed to the biggest one. A power boost aimed at a card not in the battle is pruned (one battle only). The seat's own plays and activations are searched only for cards that matter (Rush, a permanent or replacement ability, or an op other than drawing, looking at the deck, milling or revealing: its own deck draws blanks, A3). The search then tries, in order: (1) a plan in which **every attack lands** against every answer, found per order of attackers with the fewest DON!! for each attack (more DON!! never hurts an attack, so the first that lands is the least), choosing the plan with the fewest DON!! in all and then the smallest for the earliest attack (the same line the tidy order gave, E20's 2 / 5 / 0, in thousands of nodes); (2) the adaptive AND-OR search, which decides; (3) for an adaptive proof, a fixed plan with a bait attack within what is left of the cap.
- **A soundness harness.** `ai/test/lethal-sound.test.ts` samples positions (Rocks with Shiki, Newgate or nothing on its board, and Kaido; 1 to 2 Life, 0 to 3 real cards from the sourced lists, 0 to 10 DON!!), takes the `public` proof, and replays it against **every** defender answer with the real cards of the prior in hand, Life and deck, on the position's own hand and on re-rolled hands. A broken proof fails the test. Result: 0 of 180 checks broken with the corrected model (120 positions); 22 of 168 broken with the superseded model (`model: 'power-only'`, which has no types and no rest or draw), so the harness has teeth. `lethal-games.test.ts` now replays `actual` proofs against every answer, replays oblivious `public` proofs against the real hand, and fails if fewer than 30% of the positions it searches are decided.
- **L-05.** A turn where the seat played the proof's attacks (same attackers, same order, each at least as strong as the line made it, or the next needed attacker could no longer attack) and still did not win is not flagged: something outside the model (a Life Trigger, a card not in the lists) stopped it. Planted record test in both directions.
- **L-04, L-08.** The polite text carries the Trigger caveat; copy says "their usual lists run …" (the prior is not their deck). `eventCounterOf` reads `when` as a list too (`hasCounterTiming`). Additive exports: `FALLBACK_COUNTER`, `defenceBound(priorCardIds, cards, defenderDon, fallback)` (the most one hidden card adds, given the DON!! on their field, and whether their Counters can rest or draw) and `counterProfile`. `facts.maxPerCard` is now DON!!-aware, so an Unknown can still say "hits needed, attackers, the public defence bound".
- Alternatives rejected: dropping DON!! costs only for modes with a trash cost (the trash cost is dropped and errs toward the defender, but DON!! is public and was inflating the space); a per-hand-composition search; trusting the text of a card for any rules fact.

- **Measured the reviewer's way** (`.cache/l-review/rate.test.ts`: Rookie-versus-Rookie, all four Rocks and Kaido pairings, `public` mode at the 1.5 s app cap, in Node on the same machine; this run gave 829 Main-phase decisions). E20: proven, any-hand, in about 120 ms and 3,066 nodes (it was unknown at 3 s; E01 proven in about 20 ms). Unknown rate: **353 of the 580 positions that need a search (61%; reviewer: 86%)**, which is 43% of all decisions because 249 are answered at once by the attackers bound. Against a Rocks defender 78 of 235 searched are unknown (33%; reviewer 65%); against Kaido 275 of 345 (80%; reviewer 99.5%). The target (under about 30%) is **not reached**, and mostly for Kaido: with DON!! on their field its +4000 cards, rest and draw modes multiply the defender's answers, and the sound model cannot drop them. The levers left: a fast upper bound on total defence that proves or refutes without search (it needs a complete, sound list of what a defender's board can add, which the scripts do not yet give), and a cap on each attack's DON!! at the point where it is unstoppable. What the UI can show on Unknown without a search: `facts.hitsNeeded`, `facts.attackers`, and `defenceBound(...)` (the most one hidden card adds given their DON!!, and whether it can rest or draw). Differential: 276 re-rolls of the opponent's hidden cards, no difference.
## 2026-11-05 — L fix round (app): puzzle judging, coach focus, DON!! math on the board, and owners for what is not built

Context: `docs/reviews/l-review.md` failed L; these are the app findings.

- **L-06.** A puzzle is "solved" only by a win on the turn it was cut from; a win on a later turn reads "not this time". A Rewind starts a new attempt (the result is announced again and the old toast goes). Tests in `app/test/fixround.test.ts`, each checked by reverting its fix.
- **L-07.** Count it with me, each step change and Close send focus to the current question or the coach result, and the M3 focus rescue hands a coach control's focus to the coach, never the decision heading. Keyboard e2e walks every step and checks `activeElement` (it fails without the fix).
- **`C` and focus (L-11).** learning-ui §2.1 wins: `C` toggles the coach and focus does not move; only the keys that ask for a result (L, W, H) send focus to it. accessibility.md §3.12 is corrected.
- **L-04 (app part).** The live coach's `any-hand` tooltip speaks of the lists we know and what you can see now (the review's "every hand they could have had" stays in review), carries the Trigger caveat when `tier1` supplies one, and the polite tier 1 line appends the caveat when the `ai/` sr text lacks it.
- **L-08 (app part).** "What they could have" adds one line, "Their Counters can also rest one of your Characters or draw a card", when a [Counter] Event in the archetype prior does (read from the public scripts). `FALLBACK_COUNTER` and this reading are interim: switch to the `ai/` exports (one fallback, one defence-bound function) when they land.
- **L-09, DON!! math on the board.** Built, smallest honest form: the setting "DON!! math on the board" (a checkbox in the coach's Lethal section, default off, kept per browser). With it on, while an attacker is chosen each legal target shows "+X to stop" (or "fails alone"), computed from the engine's `preview` (a [When Attacking] decision that leaves the battle undecided falls back to the power difference); while giving DON!!, their Leader shows "+X to stop your best" for your strongest attacker, and it updates as DON!! go on. Against the AI only. Not built: tags on Characters other than legal targets, and drag-time tags.
- **L-10.** M9 was a real bug (setting always-on inside a puzzle checked at once): fixed, test added. M16 (the hand count) now has a unit test. Both checked by reverting.
- **L-11 copy.** The guided count says the attack that has to beat the most cards goes last, because every hit gives them a card (curriculum L16 has no smallest-first rule).
- **Owners for the unscheduled items (PLAN row 13 split).** The opponent's-turn threat check: **M7**, with the engine change it needs (a search skeleton that accepts a foreign decision, undoing M5a-02's refusal safely). The "Practised: solved" mark on the flag: **M8** (it is per-flag state that History owns). The "Their real hand: one way to see it" label on a hindsight puzzle and the any-hand puzzle's harder hand: **M9** (the drill bank builds puzzles; it can choose real strongest hands from the prior). "Lethal given away": **M8**. The board sandbox for the guided count: after owner usability session 1 (flows decide whether it is worth it).

## 2026-11-05 — L UI: the missed-lethal review is a minimal Overview, a read-only Study and a puzzle, run in its own worker

Context: PLAN row 13 needs the missed-lethal flag "end to end": run after a game, shown with its Explanation, restorable, and openable as a puzzle. Full Game Review is M8, and `ai/` is frozen for this task (another builder is fixing A1 there), so the worker protocol cannot grow a `review` request.

- **Where it runs.** `app/src/play/review/review-worker.ts` is a second worker that imports `reviewMissedLethal` from `@optcg/ai` and is given the finished game's record and every card in it (the game is over, so nothing is hidden). It checks the human seats only. `actual` mode (inside `reviewMissedLethal`) therefore appears nowhere but `review/`; a unit test walks `app/src` and fails if `'actual'` or `reviewMissedLethal` appears elsewhere. Rejected: a `review` request in the AI worker (needs an `ai/` change now, and would put the human's cards into the AI's worker).
- **Entry.** The game-over panel offers "Review this game" in games against the AI (not hotseat, where the viewer is ambiguous). The finished controller stays alive behind it; "Back to the board" returns.
- **Overview** (`ReviewFlow.tsx`): header with the result, the analysis status ("Analysing your turns…", "1 flag · 1 severe", "Analysed: no missed lethal found."), a turn timeline (one column per game turn, "T9 · You", flags sized by severity with the word "Severe", hatched cells while analysing, Left/Right between turns), a flag table, and the selected flag: "What you did" (from the game's own log), the `Explanation` at Learner verbosity, and the pinned actions Study this moment, Play from here, Practice this. N and Shift+N step flags. Each flag's accessible name is "Turn 9, your turn: missed lethal, severe, holds whatever they held." A search that hit its cap is a "Not fully checked" marker on the timeline, never a flag, with "Analyse deeper" (the whole review again at ten times the node cap, 1.5 million per search).
- **Study** restores the position **exactly** (the record's first `actionIndex` actions, hidden cards included, replayed by `GameSession.fromRecord`) on the real board at native size, read-only (`GameController` with `restore.readOnly`: no decision is ever "mine", no legal actions, no AI). The rail holds ← Overview (Esc), "Flag n of N" with previous and next (N, Shift+N), the explanation with **Show** buttons (they light the step's cards with a "Step n" chip, from the `public` proof's handles, which are this board's handles because both derive from the same state) and the pinned footer. The coach strip, the log and the decision panel are not rendered.
- **Practice this and Play from here** start a normal game controller from the same record prefix against the same AI tier and decks, with the flagged player as the human. A puzzle (`restore.puzzle`) is judged on the turn it was cut from: solved when the player wins on it, failed once that turn ends without a win (a toast and a polite line; Home goes back to the review); its rail chip reads "Practice", "Practice · solved" or "Practice · not this time"; the background detector is off in it. Play from here shows "From review" (it is not counted in any win rate; there is no win-rate store yet).
- **Deviation from §4.5.** An `any-hand` puzzle should give the defender "the strongest hand consistent with what the player could see". The `public` search proves a worst-case abstraction (placeholder cards), not a hand, and building a real strongest hand means choosing real cards from the archetype prior, so the puzzle keeps the **actual** hand from that game. The proof still beats any hand; the puzzle only cannot present a harder one. Recorded for M8, which owns the drill bank.
- **Built for extension, not built:** the opponent's notable-moves row and the Life sparkline under the timeline, the text-free thumbnail of the restored position, sort and filter (F, kinds), History, the other flag kinds, "Analyse deeper" for one turn only (it reruns the whole review), "Practiced: solved" on the original flag, and stale records (the record has the current versions by construction here). `ReviewFlag` is the type M8 widens; the timeline, flag list and rail take any flag.

## 2026-11-05 — L UI: the coach strip, the tiered detector and its worker

Context: learning-ui §2.1–2.2 and §3.1 specify the coach strip, the interruption contract and three tiers; the AI worker is the Pirate's and holds only the AI seat's data (ADR 0005).

- **A second worker, the player's own.** The detector runs in a second `AiClient` (the same worker script, started on first use) given the player's own data: their view's cards, the public archetype prior for their opponent's Leader and the card data it shows. It receives `{ type: 'lethal', mode: 'public', view }` with `session.searchView(me)` only. The AI worker never sees the human's cards, and a long search never delays the AI's turn. Rejected: one worker (the human's hand would enter the AI worker's card map, which `ai-controller.test.ts` forbids, and a 1.5 to 3 s search could delay `planTurn`).
- **Hidden information (the L done criterion).** Whether a check runs (the player's idle Main Phase, a function of the player's own view), when it runs (at the start of that Main Phase and after each of the player's moves, or on a press) and what it says (the result and the text built from it) are functions of the player's `SearchView`, which has every hidden card stripped. `app/test/coach.test.ts` runs two games that differ only in the opponent's hidden hand, deck and Life card and requires identical requests, identical tier 1 and identical polite text; `app/e2e/learning.spec.ts` does the same through the browser. `actual` mode is never requested in a live game. The detector is **not offered in hotseat** (a second person may be looking, and there is no AI worker).
- **Tiers.** The Lethal button or `L` reveals tier 1, then 2, then 3 (`C` opens or closes the coach; both keys work wherever focus is, except in a text field). The first press opens the coach on Lethal and sends focus to its result, because the player pressed a coach key; nothing else ever moves focus. Statuses: Checking… (a neutral sweeping bar, no verdict), Proven, Refuted, Unknown ("Couldn't finish checking." with Check again; never "no lethal"). The Trigger note shows under tier 1 when `tier1` supplies it. Tier 3 and the guided count open the coach **tall** (the decision panel keeps its header and footer; End turn's sub-label still carries the summary); an owed decision (an attack confirm, a defence) drops it to its strip.
- **Always-on** ("Ask me", "Tier 1 always on", "Tier 2 always on", a select in the Lethal section, kept per browser in `localStorage`, default Ask me): a check at priority 4 at the start of each Main Phase and after each move, the Lethal button badge ("◆ Lethal", "◆ 2 hits", "? Unchecked"; a refuted tier 1 shows nothing) and one polite line, spoken only when the text changes. Tier 3 still needs a press. A puzzle never checks by itself.
- **Wall-clock caps:** 1.5 s in the background, 3 s on a press, 8 s on Check again. A cap is the only thing that makes a check "Unknown", and it depends on the public worst-case state, never on hidden cards.
- **A result belongs to its position.** Any action of the player's clears it (and with an always-on level the new position is checked at once), so the coach never describes a board that has changed. The spec's "line kept" badge while a decision is owed is not built: the position the line was for is gone as soon as the player acts, and a fresh check finds the rest of the line. Rejected: carrying a line across moves (it would need a replay of the player's moves against the line).
- **Skipped on purpose:** the **opponent's-turn threat check** ("Their board threatens lethal", learning-ui §3.1 "Both players"): `lethal` answers only for the seat that owes the Main decision, because the search skeleton refuses a foreign decision (M5a-02). The Hint, DON!! math and Tempo sections are not built here (M7 owns Hint and Tempo; the DON!! math is the battle panel's own, below); so is the "Do step 1" button on the line, which shares its staging with Hint. "Show" is built. The strip holds Why and Lethal, and the chevron. Narrow rails (under 320 px) and the hand-peek layout have no room for a strip, so there `L` and `C` do nothing visible; the tablet layout is not changed. A Settings page does not exist yet, so coach verbosity is Standard, stored with the profile and ready for Settings.
- **Progress:** the furthest step used on each lethal opportunity (tier 1, tier 2, count, tier 3) is kept per game and turn in `localStorage` for the Progress page; "found it alone" is not measured (it needs the player's own attack before the first press).

## 2026-11-05 — L UI: "Count it with me" without the board sandbox

Context: learning-ui §3.2 puts step 4 ("Split your DON!!") on the board in a labelled sandbox mode that uses the normal inputs (DON!! mode, `+`, drag) with a "Sandbox · nothing is played" midline, a numbered order chip per attacker and an Exit sandbox button.

- **Decision.** Steps 1 to 4 live in the tall coach panel (the questionnaire the spec already puts there). Step 4 is a table: one row per attacker (the cards the player picked in step 2, or all the search's attackers if none), DON!! − and + buttons, and ↑ ↓ to order the attacks; each row shows "needs X · they can have Y" for its position, computed with the same arithmetic `checkGuidedCount` uses. "Check my count" marks hits, attackers and split with the reason for any that is wrong; "Show the line" reveals tier 3; Close leaves. Nothing reaches the engine's game state, by construction (the panel never calls `act`), and the status line says "nothing is played".
- **Why.** A true sandbox needs a second board state that the real inputs (DON!! mode, drag, Count) can mutate and discard, plus rendering every stage of the real board's UI against it. That is a large change to `Game.tsx`'s mode model for one lesson step, and the guided count's value is the questions and the checks. The board's own DON!! inputs are unchanged and still available to the player outside the count.
- **Rejected:** building the sandbox as a copy of `GameController` (a restore controller could do it, but its inputs would be live and need their own commit-guard); a keyboard-only DON!! split on the real board (it would change the real game).
- Step 3 ("How much can they stop?") is the spec's build-up: active Blockers, the listed defence effects from the search's facts, their hand with the deck's biggest Counter, and "Each hit gives them a card" with the growth per attack. It asks nothing; the answers checked are the hits, the attackers and the split.

## 2026-11-05 — L UI: the battle panel's coach additions use the public bound

Context: learning-ui §2.7 puts "What they could have" on the player's attack and "What you have" on defence, from public information, with the rule footnotes shown three times.

- **On your attack**, against their Leader: their active Blockers by name, their visible on-attack effects (the script's own label, for every card on their side whose script has an [On Your Opponent's Attack] or "when attacked" ability), and their hand as "N cards: up to +T if each is one of their deck's +P Counters" where P is `biggestCounter(leader, cards)` from the archetype prior, the same public bound the `public` lethal search uses (the fallback of +4000 when their Leader has no sourced list, said as "a typical deck's"). If A1's fix changes how `public` bounds a hidden card (every [Counter] effect in the prior), this number should follow it: read it from a result's `facts.maxPerCard` then. **On defence**: Counter cards in hand and their worth, Event Counters and the active DON!! to pay for them, and how many are staged.
- **Rule footnotes** (ties go to the attacker, CR 7-1-4-1; DON!! add power only on their owner's turn, CR 6-5-5-2; boosts last until the end of the battle, CR 7-1-5) show the first three times each is relevant to a profile (a count in `localStorage`).
- **Where.** Against the AI only (hotseat is unchanged, so the veiled screens stay a pure function of public information and the differential test keeps passing: a count-based footnote would differ between two views of one screen). Where the tall body is under 400 px (a 600 px window, tablets) the additions shrink to one line and the footnotes are left out, so a tall body never scrolls (board.md §3.9; `board-states.spec.ts` "adapted sizes").
- "DON!! math on the board" (the "+4000 to stop" tag on each legal target) is a setting that does not exist yet and is not built.

## 2026-11-05 — L: carried items M4a-16, M4a-18, M5a-18, M5a-21

- **M4a-16.** The veiled hint ("This effect uses your hand, so Use shows your hand first") is now decided from the decision's own `source` (card and ability) by the same `readsHiddenZone` that `veiledUse` applies, so it can never describe another effect or the other player's last activation. The source is a card on the table or an Event just played, so the answer is the same for any hidden cards; a decision with no source reads as "uses the hand", as `veiledUse` already treated it. The `hidden-differential` e2e (generated pairs) still passes. Rejected: adding a `source` to `PublicView.decision` (the allow-list view stays as it is, review M3-37).
- **M4a-18.** `GameSession.optionalCostPayable(player, action)` tries the Event's play or activation on a scratch copy and asks the engine whether the optional cost can be paid (the next decision is `optional` with a "use" answer). In the player's own revealed hand, an Event [Counter] in the defence panel, and an Event's Play (action menu and the selected-card panel), whose cost cannot be paid, shows "can't pay its cost now · plays for no effect" and needs a second press within 6 s (the first press says why in the board's popover and the polite line). Playing a non-Event card is never held up: its unusable [On Play] costs waste nothing.
- **M5a-18.** `app/test/ai-guards.test.ts` (a slow stand-in worker): "thinking" shows on the AI's own turn and never on the player's (reverting the gate fails it), and a rewind cancels the turn plan in flight and calls `reset` (removing either call fails it). Checked by reverting each.
- **M5a-21.** Setup's turn-time lines are exported constants checked against `docs/benchmarks.md` ("Setup copy"): Pirate "typically 1–8 s a turn at 1×, up to about 10 s for a long one", Rookie "typically 1–8 s a turn at 1×, up to about 12 s for a long one" (first measured today: 12 turns, 1.0 to 4.0 s up to six moves, 4.8 to 12.1 s beyond).
## 2026-11-05 — L: A1 corrected, `public` lethal is sound against every [Counter] effect in the prior

Context: review found A1 ("each hidden defender card is worth at most the biggest Counter and nothing else, so a proof is at least as strong as one against the real deck") false for the slice decks. Kaido's lists run OP07-076 (+2000, then rest up to 1 of your opponent's Characters), OP15-078 (+1000, then draw 1 if the defender has 6 or fewer DON!! on its field) and others. A defender holding the rest card can remove an attacker that has not attacked, so a line labelled "works against any hand" could lose to a real hand.

Decision: the hidden defender cards are now **placeholder Events** whose single [Counter] ability is a "choose one" that the defender makes, with a mode for each distinct effect found in the `when: 'counter'` abilities of the cards of the archetype prior (`profile.ts`): plain +power (the biggest of any [Counter] card), and each Event's own effect list with its boost aimed at "your Leader or a Character" (filters dropped), its rest aimed at "up to 1 of the opponent's Characters", and its draw kept with its own condition (the engine still checks it). DON!! and trash costs are dropped, which errs toward the defender. So each hidden card is whichever card of the prior is best for the defender at that moment, and a proof holds against every hand the prior allows. A draw pulls the next deck card, so draw-capable placeholders are the hidden hand, the hidden Life, and only the top N cards of the deck, where N is the most copies of drawing cards in any sourced list; the rest of the deck is the same placeholder without the drawing modes, which keeps a chain of draws finite as a real one is. Anything the model cannot represent (an op other than power, rest or draw; a leader with no sourced list) goes in `facts.modelGaps`, the proof's certainty drops from `any-hand` to `needed-hidden-info`, and the explanation says so; `lethal.test.ts` checks that every [Counter] ability in the Rocks and Kaido priors is modelled, so a future card fails that test rather than silently making `public` unsound. When a prior's Counter cards rest or draw, the explanation says "Their Counter cards could also rest one of your Characters or draw a card, and the line allows for it". Rocks's priors only add power, so E20 and E01 are unchanged.

Alternatives rejected: one placeholder doing everything at once (+power and rest and draw each time: with a draw every use refunds the card, so the defence is unbounded and every Kaido lethal would read as refuted); a choice of hand compositions per search (exponential in the hand and the Life that joins it); a downgrade without a model (it would mark every Kaido proof as unsure). The superseded model stays reachable as `lethal({ mode: 'public', view, model: 'power-only' })` only so the planted test can show a line it proved and the corrected model does not (Kaido at 2 Life, Luffy 5000, Sanji 7000, Zoro 9000, 6 DON!!: the rest card on the second attack removes the third attacker). Cost: E20 (Rocks, 10 DON!!) went from about 78,000 nodes and 2.4 s to about 128,700 nodes and 4.0 s (the placeholder is an Event whose boost has a target to choose); with 6 DON!! the refutation takes about 102,500 nodes and 3.2 s. Against Kaido the search hits its cap (`unknown`) much more often, because rest targets, draw chains and mode choices multiply the defender's answers; that is the honest answer to "any hand" there. The first A1 paragraph below is superseded where it says a hidden card is "only" +power.

## 2026-11-05 — L: the lethal detector's output, the missed-lethal flag, and the worker request

Context: PLAN row 13 and learning-ui §3.1/§4.2 need three tiers of answer, a guided count with data to check, and a review flag; ARCHITECTURE §4.1 names only `lethal({ view, mode, capMs })`.

- **Answers.** `tier1` (status, certainty chip, the Trigger note), `tier2` (hits, attackers), `explainLethal` (tier 3: E20 for a proof in the live coach, E01 for a `public` proof in review, E02 for an `actual`-only proof, E03 for a cap) and `guidedCount` + `checkGuidedCount` (the four questions' data: Life + 1 with a Double Attack note; the attackers that can hit the Leader; blockers, visible on-attack effects read from public scripts, hand size, the deck's biggest Counter and the cards they hold at each attack; the DON!! split with each attack's `need` and `maxDefence`). Every figure is recomputed from the engine, not from the search's bookkeeping: `maxDefence` replays the line with the defender taking each hit, then explores every defender answer at that battle. E20 is a snapshot test against the doc's own object. `checkGuidedCount` accepts any split that meets each attack's need, not only the search's.
- **Certainty.** Proven in `public` = `any-hand`; proven only in `actual` = `needed-hidden-info` (hindsight, never severe); cap = `search-incomplete`. A **refutation** is `needed-hidden-info` (it rests on a worst-case or real hand), except "too few attackers", which holds whatever they hold and is `any-hand`. The Trigger chance is a label on a proof, not a certainty change, as learning-ui §3.1 words it.
- **Request.** `{ type: 'lethal', id, mode: 'public', view, capMs?, nodes?, priority? }` or `mode: 'actual', state`. Priorities: the turn's own search 1, a pressed Lethal 2, review 3, the background detector 4; the queue is ordered by priority then arrival. A running search cannot be interrupted in a single worker thread, so its wall-clock cap (default 1,500 ms) is what bounds the delay of anything queued behind it; `cancel` drops a queued request. `actual` posts a full `GameState`, so it is used only in review and open-hands games, never in a live hidden game, and the worker comment says so. The code lives in `ai/src/search/lethal/` because the lint boundary allows full-state engine calls only there.
- **Missed lethal.** `reviewMissedLethal(record, lookup, cards, opts)` replays a record, checks each seat's first Main decision of each turn that has an action after it (the record's own end is not a miss), and flags it if the seat did not win that turn: `public` proven → severe, `any-hand`, the proof's line (E01); else `actual` proven → hindsight (E02); else a cap → a "not fully checked" marker, not a flag. Turns that cannot reach Life + 1 hits are skipped (see A7).
- **Not done here.** The opponent's-turn threat check ("their board threatens lethal") needs `determinize` on a state where the opponent owes the decision, which the skeleton refuses by design (M5a-02: a foreign decision makes it opaque), so `lethal` answers only for the seat that owes the Main decision. It is a skeleton change (it needs the public part of the decision), left for the UI slice. The "lethal given away" flag kind is also not built.

## 2026-11-05 — L: what `public` lethal assumes, and how the search is built

Context: ARCHITECTURE §4.4 says the `public` defender's hand is "bounded by public information" and a hidden Life [Trigger] is a chance node; nothing says how to drive the real engine with cards nobody can name.

- **Method.** The search runs on a real `GameState` built with `determinize` from the requesting seat's `SearchView` alone (it cannot read a game: `publicState(view, cards)`), so Blockers, DON!! allocation, on-attack effects (Kaido's −2000, Newgate's +3000), Counters, Triggers and "each hit gives the defender a card" (CR 7-1-4-1-1-2) all come from `reduce`, `legalActions` and `queries`. Hidden defender cards become a **placeholder Character** that costs nothing, has no effect and carries the biggest Counter in the defender's archetype prior (a Character's printed Counter or a [Counter] Event's +power: `biggestCounter`); every other hidden card is a blank. `public` therefore assumes: **(A1)** each hidden defender card is worth at most that one number in defence and nothing else. A Counter Event's DON!! cost and any other effect on it (a K.O., a draw) are ignored, so a proof is at least as strong as one against the real deck, and a refutation may be pessimistic about a real hand. **(A2)** Hidden Life cards are never [Trigger]s inside the search; the chance is a label (`triggerRisk`: C(50−T, k) / C(50, k) averaged over the prior's lists by recency weight, with k the face-down Life cards, all of which a winning line takes). A refutation ignores it (a Trigger can only help the defender). **(A3)** The seat's own deck draws blanks, so an effect that draws is searched but finds nothing. **(A4)** With no sourced list for their Leader the biggest Counter is assumed to be +4000 and the facts say so (`noPrior`). The leak property: `publicState` takes the view and public card data and nothing else, a differential test changes every hidden card of both sides and demands an identical answer (up to handle names), and no hidden card id may appear in the answer.
- **Search.** AND-OR over the engine: the seat's decisions are OR nodes, the defender's every legal answer an AND node, with a transposition table on `summaryHash`, interchangeable cards (same id and state) as one choice, moves largest swing first, and node caps (tests) or wall-clock caps (the app). The Main Phase moves are **macros**, "give k DON!! to X, then attack T with X": **(A5)** DON!! is never given to a card that does not attack next, so a DON!! condition on a Character that helps another card is not searched; `playCard` (only cards with a script or [Rush]: a card the engine knows nothing about does nothing but spend DON!!) and `activate` stay single steps; attacks on Characters are searched only for a Blocker or a scripted Character (**A6**). A refutation is exhaustive under A5 and A6; a cap is `unknown`, never "no lethal".
- **The line.** First a **fixed plan** that wins against every defender answer (a person can follow it without adapting): the search carries the set of states the defender's answers lead to, over simple moves (Leader attacks, "may" effects declined), tightening the DON!! budget until no plan is left and then taking the first plan in order of fewest DON!!, each attack's DON!! from none upward and smaller attacks first. That is why E20's split is 2 / 5 / 0 and E01's is "all 4 on the Leader". If no fixed plan exists the adaptive AND-OR search decides, and a proof there is labelled `oblivious: false` (the line depends on their answers). Alternatives rejected: the fully adaptive search alone (its first proof gave the Leader 2 DON!! after the defender had spent its cards, which is true but cannot be followed or checked as "split 2 / 5 / 0"); a model of the defender's hand as a number (it would restate the rules the engine already knows, and miss Blockers, Newgate and Kaido).
- **A7.** `attackersPossible` (a bound on attackers against Life, counting [Rush] Characters in hand and [Double Attack] anywhere) answers "no lethal, too few attackers" at once and lets review skip hopeless turns. It does not count effects that untap a Character or add an attack, so such a card could make a rare lethal show as refuted; the bound errs only toward searching otherwise.
- **Cost.** About 30 µs a node (the hash and `reduce`). E20 (a proof with 10 DON!!, 3 attackers, a Newgate) takes about 78,000 nodes, 2.3 s, for the plan search; positions with a full hand of scripted playable cards usually hit the cap (`unknown`) at 8,000 to 25,000 nodes. The per-push test caps are node counts; `npm run test:lethal` runs more games and a bigger cap.
- **Engine.** No engine change. Additive only on the AI package: `lethal`, `explainLethal`, `tier1`, `tier2`, `guidedCount`, `checkGuidedCount`, `reviewMissedLethal`, `answerLethal` and the request type.
## 2026-11-05 — M3 NVDA pass fixes: one focus rescue, a child-node assertive region, and eight design choices

Context: the scripted NVDA pass (`docs/design/a11y-passes/m3-nvda-chrome.md`, `-firefox.md`) failed M3 on F1 (focus lost to `<body>`) and FF1 (Firefox/NVDA speaks only the first `role="alert"`). Decisions, one per finding where a choice was offered:

- **F1, one rule.** `app/src/play/game/focus.ts` remembers where focus last was as a zone and an index (or "the decision panel"), and a `MutationObserver` puts focus back when the focused element left the page and focus fell to `<body>`: same index in that zone (clamped), else the decision heading. It runs for every unmount, so the action menu, Done, the order choice, "Next move" and later modes are covered without each remembering to refocus. A focus the player moved to `<body>` with a click is left alone, because the element they left is still in the page. Rejected: a refocus call in each handler (the four findings were four missing calls; a fifth would be the next one); refocusing in a layout effect on every render (it cannot tell a click on empty board from a lost element).
- **FF1/F6b.** `#announce-assertive` is now `aria-live="assertive"` (no `role="alert"`), each message a fresh keyed `<p>`, emptied one second after the write. Rejected: clear-then-write in one node (Firefox coalesces the two mutations).
- **F2 (browse-mode keys).** The decision panel's footer strip (buttons only) is `role="application"`, so NVDA is in focus mode there and `.`, `E`, `S` reach the page; its buttons carry `aria-keyshortcuts`. Browse mode is not lost where there is nothing to read: the body, with its headings and lists, stays a document. Rejected: the whole panel as an application (R1 in accessibility.md); a note on the sheet only ("press Enter on the button"), which leaves the sheet's own claim false. Unverified until the NVDA re-run.
- **F3.** Not reproduced: in Chromium the first Tab from load reaches "Skip to decision" and nothing focuses the heading on load (new e2e). The Guidepup harness loads the page twice and its virtual cursor may have set the focus start. The heading now also hands Tab to the panel's first control and re-focuses itself on a click (FF2). Left for the NVDA re-run to confirm.
- **F4.** The engine's `Preview` gained `battleUndecided` (an attack whose Damage Step was not reached because the attacker owes a decision first, such as [When Attacking]). The panel then says "Result depends on [When Attacking]" and no verdict. No battle maths in `app/`. The confirm is spoken politely when it opens (focus stays on the target, so Enter still confirms). Rejected: moving focus to the confirm heading (Enter on the target stops confirming).
- **F5.** An AI turn's end is spoken with a one-sentence summary built from that turn's public log lines (plays, attacks, damage to you, your K.O.s).
- **F6a.** `EffectActivated` and `EffectDeclined` events now carry `player` (the controller), and their log phrases start "Your"/"Their" before the card name; in a mirror the opponent's speech label is "Opponent", never a name that could be yours.
- **F9.** Pick mode lists the board cards of the candidates' zone that the effect does not allow as unavailable, with the card's cost and power and "can't be chosen for this effect" as the reason. The engine does not expose per-card reasons for a selector (and the prompt omits the cost test), so the app does not invent one; "King, cost 6, 7000: can't be chosen" lets the player see why. A per-candidate reason from the engine is carried to M10.
- **F10.** `order` stays "choose what resolves first" (the engine's decision is "which first", answered one at a time), and same-named sources are told apart: "Kaido (your Leader)", "Kaido (Character slot 2)". accessibility.md §3.9's grab/move/drop is dropped for effect orders; step 14 of §11.3 changed. Rejected: a reorder control that would send an `order` per item anyway.
- **F11.** Won't fix in v1. Staged Counters go to the defender (the attacked card or its Blocker); no other target changes a battle's result. Step 10's "change one counter's target" is removed from §11.3.
- **F12.** The rewind list is one native radio group (arrows select, Tab reaches the buttons), not a listbox of buttons.
- **F14.** Won't fix: `E` ends the turn at once, as flows D1 decided (undo, not confirm; Strict mode's second press covers the careful case). Step 8 of §11.3 changed to "E; U undoes it".
- **FF2.** Hardened, not reproduced: see F3. **F7:** the pinned inspector's `aria-describedby` points at the card text. **F8/F13:** the played-card sentence is "Played X, rested N DON!!", glyphs are `aria-hidden`, a pause separates a button's label and sub-label, "1 card", "X. Drake", and "before Basil Hawkins".

Not marked verified: nothing here was heard in NVDA; M3 stays open until the owner or CI re-runs `a11y-nvda.yml`.

## 2026-11-05 — AI dwell compression is decided from elapsed board time, not the plan; the pacing check follows board.md §5.2

Context: on resume, the per-push pacing e2e failed every run: the late-game AI turn (15 moves) took 11.5 s at 1×. The M5a fix round's new holds after the AI's own picks and DON!! groups (M5a-12) added about 4.7 s, and the fix round carried dwell compression to M10 on a measurement taken before those holds existed. The check it wrote also asked 8 s of **every** turn, while board.md §5.2 promises 8 s for a typical turn and budgets heavy turns above it (its own heavy example compresses from 13.2 to 12.3 s). Decision: build compression now, as part of M5a. Non-attack dwells on the AI's turn drop from 400 to 240 ms once the turn has spent 6 s of board time, with human decisions and the worker wait left out (`aiDwell` in `controller.ts`). Never in Step mode or below 1×; attacks and battle results keep 600 ms. The late turn now takes 10.0 s. The check asserts §5.2 as written: a typical turn (at most 6 moves) within 8 s; the fixed-line late turn within 10.6 s, which fails without compression; the nightly game's heavy turns within 12.3 s; the worker wait within 1 s on every turn. board.md §5.2 is updated to match. Rejected: board.md's up-front rule, which compresses from the plan's queued length. That length depends on the AI's hidden hand, so fast dwells on the first move would tell the player a long turn is coming, a timing channel like M5a-01. Also rejected: cutting attack holds, or dropping the new pick and DON!! holds, to fit 8 s (principle 10; review M5a-12 asked for those holds). Also rejected: keeping "every turn within 8 s", which no 15-move turn can meet with §5.2's holds.

## 2026-10-29 — M5a fix round 1: "Why?" from public facts only, blind skeletons, gates that can fail

Context: the M5a review (`docs/reviews/m5a-review.md`) failed the milestone: the "Why?" chip's presence leaked the AI's hidden option count (M5a-01, severity 4), the search skeleton copied another player's decision verbatim (M5a-02), and the AI's follow-up picks were never announced (M5a-03), plus eleven severity-2 findings.

- **"Why?" is a function of public information (M5a-01).** The rule: an AI explanation depends only on the position as the player sees it, the AI's move (public once made) and its playbook (public). So every AI decision goes to the worker and stores an explanation, forced or not, and one builder (`ai/src/search/why.ts`) explains every decision the same way. "Only legal move." is gone (forcedness can be private), and so are `search-incomplete` labels (whether the budget ran out depends on how many moves the hidden hand allows). Reasons never cite the heuristic term a move gained on, because the search that ranked moves played out the AI's hidden cards. Wording stays true for any hidden hand ("It let the hit through", "It added its Life card to its hand", "Its Leader already holds"). The "thinking" state shows only on the AI's own turn; on the player's turn the uniform considering beat covers the search. Rejected: hiding the chip on forced windows (its absence leaks), and keeping term-based reasons with a filter (no filter can make a reason computed from hidden cards public). Guards: `ai/test/why-differential.test.ts` (reachable pairs that differ only in the AI's hand, Life or deck; 2,258 action explanations and 526 decisions compared) and the controller twin tests in `app/test/ai-controller.test.ts`. Both fail on the M5a code.
- **Playbook citations replace heuristic reasons (M5a-04).** When a move follows a playbook line whose condition is public, "Why?" cites the line and its guide source; otherwise a main move says "Its search ranked this move first, playing out guesses at your hidden hand." `avoidPlay` is never cited (its condition is a card in hand). Attacks carry `needed-hidden-info` (their result depends on the player's hand), and their outcome names Blockers and effects (M5a-07).
- **The skeleton is blind to other players' decisions (M5a-02).** When another player owes the decision, the skeleton holds only its public shape (kind, player, generic prompt) and is opaque; a record about a hidden card has its `cardId` and `label` blanked too. A reachable-pair differential (`engine/test/property/skeleton-diff.test.ts`) replaces the permutation property's blind spot; it fails on the M5a code in under a second.
- **Merged lines are announced (M5a-03).** An action that joins an earlier move (an effect's pick, the next DON!! of a group) re-announces that move's line, whole. A live-region test checks every final AI line was heard.
- **Defence (M5a-05, M5a-11).** At 0 Life a lethal hit is always defended when a defence exists, even when a later attack may still win; the take template has separate wordings for 0, 1–2 and 3+ Life; a defence of several cards is explained card by card, never previewing a card still in hand. The chosen defence survives an Event's target pick, and the worker forgets its plan and battle memory on rewind (`reset`).
- **DON!! steps (M5a-06)** explain only themselves, never the attack that may follow.
- **Scoring (M5a-15).** Candidates are compared over complete rounds of samples only, and the first round always completes once started, so no candidate goes unscored (at most one round past the budget per plan). Unchosen alternatives are no longer posted to the page. The fallback when a skeleton is opaque speaks as Pirate, not Rookie.
- **Weights (M5a-09).** Every weight now says what is sourced (the direction, with its passage) and what is inference (the number). The two changed during M5a say why; their A/B over 140 Pirate-vs-Pirate games each (benchmarks.md "Heuristic A/B") shows no measurable strength difference either way, so they are kept for the reasons they were changed, not as strength gains. The AI's Life table is concave from 0: 1 → 0 costs 4.2, 2 → 1 3.1. The quick policies' thresholds are named (`QUICK`), Unblockable gets a weight (§6). Kaido's mulligan counts each card once and needs two Counters, as its guide says; the explanation states the rule it applied.
- **Game-plan assertions (M5a-08)** run twice per push: with the biases, and on search alone. Search alone finds 7 of the 10 seeds plus the Leader-attack hold; two Kaido lines (holding the −2000 when it changes nothing; the Ulti set-up) pass only by their bias and are marked `it.fails`, so the test turns red if search alone ever finds them. The planted positions now offer real alternatives, and no assertion can pass vacuously. Nightly measures the tallies with and without biases, and counts the exempted finishing turns.
- **Strength gates that can fail (M5a-10).** Since the scoring fix, a "1-rollout" Pirate still scores every candidate once, so it is no longer crippled (it beat Rookie and the baselines). The gates use real baselines instead: Pirate vs Rookie at 90% per push and 95% nightly; Pirate vs a shallow Pirate (no rollouts: it scores moves by the position right after them) at 60% (per push 14 of 20; a broken rollout makes Pirate into that player, and the nightly shallow-vs-shallow control shows the gate failing at about 50%). Nightly adds a row where the Pirate's prior leaves out the opponent's actual list, and A/B rows. Rejected: a higher floor against Rookie alone (Rookie cannot tell settings apart).
- **Pacing (M5a-12).** The per-push pacing e2e uses iteration budgets (`?aibudget=iterations`), checks every turn (the whole turn, the board's own pacing, and the worker's visible time, separately), measures four turns, and runs without CI's retry. The AI's own-turn picks hold `dwell`; a DON!! group dwells at its end unless that card's attack follows. The plan request is tracked and cancelled on rewind; requests queue in the worker so a cancel arriving behind one is read before it runs. The considering beat covers every AI decision on the player's turn, as flows F21.2 says; board.md §5.2 now agrees.
- **"Why?" panel (M5a-13).** The dialog is described by its claim, the claim and evidence are announced politely when it opens, the log behind it is inert, and W with nothing to explain says so. Rejected for now: moving "Why?" into the coach panel (the learning slice builds it).
- **Carried:** board.md's dwell compression for long turns (every measured turn is within 8 s without it; it needs the plan's length on the main thread) and the pre-existing midline overlap at 1280×720, both to M10 polish.

## 2026-10-29 — Scripted NVDA pass for M3 (accessibility.md §11.2)

- **Harness in its own package.** `app/a11y/` holds the Guidepup script with its own `package.json` and lockfile; it is not an npm workspace, so `npm ci`, lint's type-free rules, typecheck, unit tests and e2e never install or compile it. The `a11y-nvda` workflow (manual `workflow_dispatch` only, `windows-2025`) installs it, NVDA 2026.2 through `npx @guidepup/setup install nvda` (the archived `guidepup/setup-action` says to use the CLI), and Playwright's Firefox. Versions are pinned exactly: `@guidepup/guidepup` 0.38.0, `@guidepup/playwright` 0.21.0, `@guidepup/setup` 0.29.3, `@playwright/test` 1.64.1. Rejected: the deployed Pages URL (it would test whatever deployed last, not the commit), and a root workspace (CI would install Guidepup on every push).
- **Speech from a tap, not Guidepup's capture.** Guidepup records speech only during its own actions and presses NVDA's stop-speech key before each one, so an announcement that arrives after a key is cut or lost, and its capture never returns while something keeps talking (the first run hung for 30 minutes). The harness joins the same NVDA Remote relay channel as a second client and timestamps every `speak` message; each key then waits for a second of NVDA silence (at most 10 s). Keys still go through NVDA, so browse and focus mode apply as for a user.
- **Assists are recorded.** When the keyboard path does not reach what a step needs, the harness moves focus with Playwright and writes a "harness assist" into the record. Pass verdicts are written by comparing the logs with the Expect column, not by the script.
- **`a11y-m3` scenario** (`app/src/play/setup/a11y-scenarios.ts`): Kaido against Rookie's Kaido, every card scripted (M4a), turn 3 rather than turn 1 (on turn 1 the "played this turn" reason in step 7 is hidden by "no battles on the first turn"), seed 11 so Rookie's first attack is King at 7000 against the 5000 Leader (step 10's tie, then survive). Two OP17-062 Kaido give step 14's order decision when a DON!! returns; Queen OP17-065 gives step 13's two-target pick.

## 2026-10-29 — M5a AI slice: search views, the Pirate tier, playbooks and explanations

Context: M5a (PLAN row 12) needs per-turn planning on views only (ADR 0005, 0008), the Pirate tier, the two slice playbooks with game-plan assertions, the `Explanation` builder, and the app's "Why?" chip (M3-23). Each decision lists what was rejected.

- **Search views (engine, additive).** `projectForSearch(state, p)` is `project` plus a *skeleton*: the state with every card `p` cannot see stripped of its identity and flags, every instance id replaced by a canonical id assigned from public structure (player, zone, position), the RNG removed and projection keys blanked. A reference to a hidden card outside the zones (a frame local, a modifier) would say which hidden card it is, so it becomes a constant and the skeleton is marked `opaque`; `determinize(view, fill, cards, seed)` refuses those, and the AI then answers from the view (lethal defence first). `determinize` names every hidden card from the `fill` (keyed by the view's positional handles), rebuilds definitions from card data and seeds a fresh RNG. The leak property now also requires identical skeletons under hidden permutations, and a mutation that leaves hidden card ids in the skeleton fails it. In 331 sampled positions none was opaque, and the true fill round-trips to the same cards, decision and legal actions. Rejected: rebuilding a state from the plain view (it loses modifiers, Once Per Turn marks, summoning sickness and the stack); giving the worker full state (cheating by construction); a `determinize` that takes state (the worker never has it).
- **Queries (additive).** `queries.battle`, `queries.usedThisTurn` and `CardView.played` (public facts the search and the explanations need). No rule, hash or record changed, so ENGINE_VERSION stays 0.5.2. `projectForSearch` and `determinize` join the lint list of full-state names: allowed only in `app/src/session/` and `ai/src/search/`.
- **Pirate is a greedy planner scored by rollouts.** Each candidate is applied in K = 4 determinized states, its follow-ups and the rest of the turn are played by quick policies, and the position is evaluated at the start of the next turn's Main Phase, so every candidate (including ending the turn) is judged at the same point. Candidates: every play and activation; every attack alone or after 1, 2, 3 or all active DON!!; one DON!! to a card that can still attack; ending the turn. Moves that cannot matter are left out (DON!! to a card that cannot attack, an attack that fails for certain from a card with no [When Attacking]), so a level score never shows a pointless move. The plan runs until the samples disagree about what the AI will see next (an attack's result, a draw); `decide` follows it while the view's fingerprint matches and plans again otherwise. Rejected: a static evaluation right after each move (it cannot value DON!! given before an attack, and ended turns early); every DON!! split (too many candidates for the budget); searching each decision without a plan (more work per turn, and nothing to explain as a turn).
- **Defence.** A Counter decision compares "take it" with *whole* defences that stop the attack (fewest cards; least Counter spent), never a partial one, and keeps to the chosen defence for the rest of the battle. Lethal is always defended where possible. Block, Trigger and effect choices are scored by rollouts to the start of the AI's next turn. Rejected: judging one Counter card at a time (it can strand a half-played defence, which is exactly the "thrown-away counter" the review flags).
- **Determinization prior.** The opponent's hidden cards come from one of their archetype's three sourced lists, weighted 3/2/1 by recency, minus every card of theirs the AI has seen; short or long pools are padded from the same list or trimmed at random. The AI's own deck order and Life come from its own list. Rejected: uniform legal cards of the Leader's colours (no meta signal; kept only as the fallback for a Leader with no sourced list, where seen cards and the AI's own list pad it); the human's actual list (cheating).
- **Heuristic.** Seven named terms (life, hand, counter, board, DON!!, lethal threat, deck), each weight documented with its concept in `ai/src/search/heuristic.ts`. strategy-concepts.md marks all its weights as inferences to tune, so two were tuned against tests: a rested Character's exposure 0.35 → 0.15 (it held attackers back), and the AI's own Life value from 2 to 1 raised to 3.2, so that at 2 Life a cheap Blocker beats a hit ("switch hard to blocking once you're at 2" [S31], "below two Life, block by default" [S40]).
- **Playbooks** (`ai/playbooks/OP17-039.json`, `OP17-058.json`): the guides' five playbook seeds each, as assertions with thresholds, plus weight multipliers, card values for ongoing effects, face-versus-board by stage, mulligan rules (strategy-concepts §9, the guides' keepable hands) and move biases. Both go second (§10 [S17]: "the general default is to go second"; go second with removal, late-game and counter-heavy decks). Two biases encode guide lines the greedy search cannot see on its own: Kaido holds its −2000 "for the turn it changes an outcome" [SH-KAI] (a penalty for using it when it changes nothing), and its Leader's [When Attacking] −2000 sets up Ulti & Page One's K.O. [SH-KAI] (keep its DON!!, shrink the target first). Rejected: hard-coded scripted lines (they would not survive a different hand).
- **Game-plan assertions** run in two ways. Per push: planted positions, one per seed, plus "holds the −2000" negatives (`ai/test/game-plan.test.ts`). Nightly: statistically over 40 Pirate-vs-Pirate games, because against Rookie most games are decided before the guides' key turns; a turn the AI's own search scores as a win in at least half its samples is a finishing turn and is not judged by the plan. Rejected: judging over the Rookie games alone (they measured how fast it wins, not whether it plays the plan).
- **Explanations.** `ai/src/explain.ts` builds every decision's `Explanation` from public facts only (flows D9): a template per decision kind (block, counter, take, the Leader's trick, play, attack, DON!!, activation, choices, mulligan, turn order), with the heuristic term the chosen move gained most on, against its runner-up, as the reason. E12, E13 and E16 are snapshot tests that read the expected objects from `docs/design/explanations.md` itself, recompute every figure from the position, and check the "AI Why" certainty label. A whole-game test checks every AI explanation for its concept, lesson, copy rules and that it names no card the player could not see. `certaintyLabel(certainty, context)` holds the labels table.
- **Worker protocol.** `init` now carries the game (tier, the AI's own list, both Leaders, budgets); `cards` adds public card data as cards become visible to the AI; `planTurn` plans and keeps the plan in the worker, answering only with a count; `decide` follows the plan while it holds. Rejected: sending the plan to the main thread and back (the UI has no use for it, and it would put the AI's intentions in the UI's memory).
- **Budgets.** Iterations (rollouts) in tests and CI: 900 per plan, 120 per defensive decision. Milliseconds in the app: 500 and 250. K = 4 samples.
- **App.** Pirate is selectable on setup and is the default opponent (it is the slice's tier; Rookie stays as the baseline). The e2e flows written against Rookie name it (`opp=rookie` in the shared start helper). The AI's turn plan starts with its 700 ms Refresh-Draw-DON!! segment, and each next decision is requested as soon as the previous move is applied, so the worker searches while moves animate. The considering beat now applies only to the AI's defensive windows on the player's turn (flows G5, board.md §5.2); before, it also delayed the AI's own effect choices on its turn. DON!! given to one card in a row is one move (one log line, one animation), as board.md §5.2 budgets it. Each AI move with a choice stores its `Explanation` by the move's first action index; follow-up picks of an effect join the move's intent. "Why?" is a 24 px chip on the move's first log line and W opens the last one; the panel docks over the log (the coach panel arrives with the learning slice) at Standard verbosity. Measured AI turns at 1×: 1.0 to 6.8 s (docs/benchmarks.md).

## 2026-10-29 — M4a fix round 1, Phase B (app) and CI time

- **[Activate: Main] in the UI (M4a-03).** A card with a scripted [Activate: Main] opens its action menu on click or Enter (with "⚔ Attack…" when it can also attack); each ability is an item ("✦ Activate: <label>", key E for the first), or disabled with `whyNot`'s reason. Rejected: a separate ability bar (more chrome; the menu is already the keyboard path).
- **Effect log lines (M4a-05)** come from projected events only, so a hidden card is never named: activations with the script's label, Events, declines, reveals (the engine now tags `CardRevealed.reason`: play, cost, effect, search), looks, modes (the `neutral` label), DON!! returns and adds, replacements, effect rests (`Rested.reason`), power and keyword changes (`PowerModified`/`ModifierAdded` carry `card` and `byEffect`), bounce, bottom-deck and trash. Hotseat keeps using the public log. Draws by an effect are a count.
- **Prompts (M4a-09).** The `use` prompt names its cost ("pay DON!! −1 to use this effect"); focus starts on Use and Enter uses it (flows F8d). Enter never commits "choose none" on a paid effect's target choice. DON!! choices show counts per location and warn when the DON!! sits on a card in the battle. The order panel names the timing ("Kaido [On Play]: …"), and card ordering has "Keep this order". Event [Counter]s list their cost (with the optional activation cost) and effect, and show in the Block Step as "available in the Counter Step". When the attack already fails, the defence footer says "Done · the attack fails". Rejected for now: staging Event [Counter]s with Character Counters (their costs and targets need decisions, which the one-commit sequence cannot carry).
- **Setup copy (M4a-15)** is per seat and per deck, counted by different card numbers, and claims only that scripted cards' effects happen.
- **Veiled hotseat (M3-41, M3-42, M3-43).** The veiled panel says whether Use will show the hand, judged from the last effect both seats saw activate in the public log and its public script, so it cannot vary with hidden cards. A differential e2e test presses Use under the veil for a payable and an unpayable Shiki cost and for Kaido's public DON!! −1; removing the `readsHiddenZone` check fails it. A `scenario=json:` spec without a valid mode returns to setup with a message.
- **CI time.** Playwright runs in 3 parallel shards, separate from lint, typecheck and unit tests. Per push, the full-game flows (vs Rookie, hotseat, keyboard) play to completion from a late-game Rocks-vs-Kaido position with every card scripted; the turn-1 full games run nightly (`E2E_FULL=1`). `E2E_PORT` lets several working copies run e2e on one machine without reusing another copy's preview server. Rejected: dropping the full games (coverage) or a single longer timeout (still flaky).

## 2026-10-29 — M4a fix round 1 (engine and cards)

Context: the M4a review (`docs/reviews/m4a-review.md`) failed the milestone. Phase A covers the engine and card findings.

- **M4a-04: cards played by one effect trigger as one event.** OP17-118 plays up to 2 cards; their play frames share a pending batch, so their [On Play]s queue together and the player orders them with the existing `order` decision (CR 8-6-1). The cards are still placed in canonical order, which only matters for the 5-Character rule. Rejected: asking the play order first (an extra decision that only stands in for the [On Play] order).
- **M4a-08: an Event's DON!! −X activation cost is optional**, like its "you may" costs (B-1). The engine treats any DON!! −X in an Event's [Main] or [Counter] cost as declinable, without a per-script flag: QA-ST01–04 ST04-016 (attribution checked by hand from the cached text, RL-52), CR 8-3-1-4 and the "You may return" reminder. Affected: OP09-077, OP15-078 [Main]; OP07-076, EB04-040, OP17-077 [Counter]. E-Q12 is closed. Unchanged: [Activate: Main] and [Trigger] costs (activating is the consent) and auto costs (already asked).
- **M4a-07: the schema rejects what the runtime would get wrong.** `validateScript` now rejects: a move from the deck top other than "up to 1 to the top of Life"; moves from hand or trash to the deck without a top or bottom position; moves into Life or from mixed zones; a restriction on a player; `play.rested`; plays from outside the hand; K.O. outside the Character area; modifiers on cards off the field; looking at the opponent's deck; trash-from-hand outside the hand. The runtime honours a declared deck position, and every fall-through emits an `Anomaly`, which fails any random game. Rejected: implementing each shape now (M4b will, with its cards).
- **M4a-06: the leak property knows what the viewer knows.** A card whose play is in progress was revealed at its pay step, so it is no longer treated as hidden. Card ids in projected events are now compared up to the first reveal, instead of being stripped everywhere, and the follow-up `autoSkip` check is skipped only when an event revealed a card.
- **M4a-11:** a "choose one" may give `neutral` labels for both players' logs; `ModeChosen.label` uses them (OP17-049).
- **M4a-12:** the summary hash drops projection keys and batch counters (batches become queue positions) and adds each player's `known` cards by position.

ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.2.

## 2026-10-29 — An Event's "you may" activation cost can be declined (B-1)

Context: the independent card tests (DISAGREEMENTS.md B-1) expected EB04-040's "[Main] You may rest 6 of your DON!! cards: …" to let the player decline the cost after playing the Event. The engine paid it automatically, and refused to play OP17-056 when its "you may" cost could not be paid. The coordinator ruled for the test author: under CR 8-3-1-4 a "can/may" cost is optional and skipping it means only that the effect does not activate; playing the Event (paying its printed cost and trashing it, CR 2-7-3, 8-4-2) is a separate step.

Decision: the DSL marks such abilities `costOptional: true` (EB04-040, OP17-055, OP17-056, OP17-077 [Main]; OP17-076 [Counter]). The Event is legal to play, or to activate in the Counter Step, whenever its printed cost can be paid. After it is trashed, the engine asks a `use` decision; "use" is unavailable when the activation cost cannot be paid (a forced no). Declining trashes the Event with no effect. Costs written as DON!! −X or with no "may", and costs of [Activate: Main] and [Trigger] abilities, are unchanged: choosing to activate those is the consent. ENGINE_VERSION 0.5.1.

Rejected: refusing the play when the optional cost cannot be paid (no rule supports it); asking only when payable (the AI and UI would see a different flow for the same card).

## 2026-10-29 — Veiled hotseat screens are a pure function of a branded public view

Context: three review rounds each found another veiled widget reading private data (M3-01 to M3-04, M3-35, M3-39/M4a-01). The last one, whether "Use" was enabled, revealed whether the hidden hand could pay a cost.

Decision:
- **One entry point:** veiled effect decisions render only through `veiledContent(VeiledProps)`. The props carry a branded `PublicView`, which only `GameSession.publicView()` returns, plus the seat name and callbacks.
- **Public decision:** it keeps only its kind, its player and constant wording.
- **No private answers under the veil:** `controller.actions()` returns nothing for veiled optional, choose and order decisions.
- **Answerability is public:** Use and Skip are always enabled. Whether Use needs a reveal comes from the public script (`readsHiddenZone`), never from the private answer list.
- **Guard:** a differential e2e test requires identical veiled HTML, aria and live-region text across 52 position pairs that differ only in hidden cards.

Rejected: fixing each widget as it was found, which had already failed three times; and disabling Use when the cost can't be paid, which leaks the hand.

## 2026-10-29 — Hotseat reveal: hold for mouse and R, sticky tap for touch, pen and keyboard

Context: M3 re-review 1 (M3-32) found that on touch the defender could hold to reveal their hand but could not stage a Counter. A second finger's tap fires no `click`, and lifting the holding finger hid the hand.

Decision:
- With a mouse, or the R key, the reveal is press-and-hold.
- With touch, a pen, or Enter/Space on the veil, a tap turns on a sticky reveal. It ends on "Hide my hand", the defence commit, the window losing focus, or a seat change.
- Single taps stage Counters while the hand is revealed. The commit always re-hides it.

Rejected: handling second-finger taps on `pointerup`. Browsers don't reliably fire a click for them, so it would be fragile, and it needs two hands.

## 2026-10-29 — M4a effects slice: runtime and API decisions

Context: M4a builds the effect DSL runtime and scripts the Rocks (OP17-039) and Kaido (OP17-058) lists, 37 cards. The independent card-test author reported API gaps (mode choices, Event [Counter] targets, decision sources, DON!! adds, looked-at cards, effect state in views, keyword strings). Each decision below lists what was rejected.

- **Programs, not trees.** Each ability compiles to a flat program (costs, then the effect; if/else, may/ifDid, choose-one modes and delayed bodies become jumps); an effect frame holds an instruction pointer and its locals. Rejected: walking the step tree with a path stack (more state per frame, harder to hash).
- **API additions (additive, frozen shapes unchanged):** `Decision.purpose` (`cost`, `target`, `mode`, `order`, `use`, `replacement`, `characterLimit`), `Decision.source` (card id, ability id, label; `instance` in queries, `handle` in views), `Decision.options` (named options with labels); `pick.choice` may be `{ option: id }` for modes and top/bottom; `order.first` is a listed option id (a card, or `<card>#<ability>` for pending effects); `CardView`/`ViewCard` gain `counter`, `cannotAttack`, `negated`; `ProjectedView` gains `looking`, `known` (now filled), `attackLock`, `delayed`; event types `CardsLooked`, `ModeChosen`, `EffectDeclined`. Granted keywords use the printed strings (`Rush`, `Unblockable`, `Blocker`). Rejected: a new decision kind for modes (breaks every consumer's switch); encoding modes in the prompt.
- **Event [Counter] is `activate` in the Counter Step.** `{ type: 'activate', card, ability }` with the card in hand; costs are paid at activation and targets are chosen while it resolves (CR 8-4-4). `counter` stays for Character Counters only. Rejected: `counter` with a dummy target.
- **Auto with a cost asks first.** If payability depends on the hand (a trash or reveal cost), the `optional` is asked even when the cost cannot be paid, with "use" unavailable and `secret` set, so the opponent learns nothing; a public cost that cannot be paid skips silently. Rejected: asking only when payable (leaks whether the hand holds a matching card).
- **Hidden-zone choices are always asked** (even with no match) and flagged `secret`; `publicCount` marks a pick over a whole hand of public size. With another human watching, `autoSkip` skips a forced pick only when every candidate is visible or the count forces it (M2-19).
- **DON!! by location with counts (E-D4).** A DON!! −X or return asks for classes with counts; it is not asked when one class can pay or every DON!! must go. "Add up to N DON!!" and "set up to N DON!! active" act on as many as possible without asking. Rejected: asking "how many" each time (adding fewer is never better in these decks; it would double the decisions for players and the AI). Logged as open question E-Q11.
- **Pending effects batch per event; a player orders their own.** `order` asks the controller when two or more of their effects from one event wait (Rule Manual "Effect Activation Order"). Two Characters played by one effect (OP17-118) trigger in play order, as separate batches. Rejected: FIFO with no choice.
- **Negation removes printed keywords too** (CR 10-1 calls them keyword effects; CR 8-2-1-1); granted keywords stay (CR 8-2-4). A negated card's [On K.O.] does not activate, and its replacements do not apply (E-Q1's best answer).
- **"If X, A. Then, B" puts B inside the guard** (CR 4-10-1; effect-dsl §4.10): EB04-030, OP17-060, OP08-074, OP07-077.
- **Blind picks are random.** "Trash 1 card from your opponent's hand" (OP17-075) is chosen face-down by its controller (QA-OP17); the engine picks uniformly with the seeded RNG. Rejected: a positional pick (the hand array order is draw order, so it would leak).
- **Replacement order.** Applicable replacements are offered one at a time in CR 8-1-3-4-2 rank order; each is optional, so declining one and taking the next gives the same choice as ordering them.
- **`derive` never memoizes a state that is being drafted.** A memo taken mid-`reduce` went stale and showed one viewer an old power; the leak property found it.
- **Instance ids start with `$` (M2-22)** so the hash and the leak tests recognise them anywhere, including compound keys.
- **Benchmark:** 5,588 two-turn simulations per second in a worker; ADR 0008 confirmed, no fallback (`docs/benchmarks.md`).

## 2026-10-29 — Target milestones for M3's carried items

Context: M3 re-review 1 (M3-33) found that the items carried out of M3 had no target milestone, so they could drift.

Decision:
- the "Why?" chip on AI log lines goes to **M5a**, with the `Explanation` builder;
- the hotseat "you look away" line goes to **M7**, with the coach copy;
- saving and resuming a game goes to **M8**, with local save;
- drag as a shortcut for click, and 44 px lane chips on tablets (M3-18, the product's own rule in board.md §6.2; the chips already meet WCAG 2.5.8 at 24 px), go to **M10** polish.

Rejected: building them in M3. None is in PLAN's M3 exit criteria, and each depends on later work (Explanations, the coach, local save) or competes with the tablet height margin that fixed the Linux scroll.

## 2026-10-29 — Hotseat renders a public view while veiled

Context: M3 review 1 found four severity-4 leaks in hotseat (M3-01 to M3-04). Behind the handoff curtain and during a veiled defence, the board, log, live regions, staged-Counter breakdown and rewind list all rendered from the private view of the incoming or defending seat.

Decision: the app session builds a **public view** from the two player views, keeping only cards both seats can see, with no actions and generic decision wording. It also builds a **public log**, keeping only event fields both projections share. The hotseat screen renders the public view unless a seat's reveal is active (holding R, or press-and-hold on touch). The hotseat log and live regions are always public, and they are cleared when a reveal ends. Everything behind the curtain is `inert`. Rewind is off while a seat is veiled, and a hotseat rewind needs the other player's agreement. The cost: a player's own draws no longer appear by name in the hotseat log.

Rejected:
- masking each widget separately, which is how the leaks happened;
- adding a public projection to the engine, which would change the frozen API for something the app can derive from two views.

Also decided in this round: with a Blocker chosen, Enter blocks without playing Counters that would be wasted, and "Play anyway" is a separate button (M3-05). Carried: 44 px lane chips on tablets (M3-18), because they would use up the cross-platform height margin; the chips pass WCAG 2.5.8 through the spacing exception.

## 2026-10-28 — Tablet defence panel compacts instead of scrolling

Context: at 1024×690, the defence panel body had only 19 px of free height on macOS. Linux fonts wrap more text, so the panel scrolled on CI, and it would scroll for Linux and Windows users too.

Decision: on rails 280 px and narrower:
- the Attacker and Defender labels sit above the names, not in a 62 px column;
- the defender breakdown reads "+N staged", with the card names listed only in the Counter lane;
- the lane labels shrink to 52 px;
- the staging hint drops "ties go to the attacker", which the result line already states.

The e2e check now also requires at least 24 px of free height in every tall state at the adapted sizes. Rejected:
- letting the body scroll, which `board.md` forbids for decision panels;
- relaxing the test, which would hide a cross-platform failure.

## 2026-10-28 — M3 board implementation decisions

Context: the M3 builder made these choices while building the playable board.
1. **No coach strip yet.** The coach arrives with the learning slice, and an empty strip would be dead UI. The decision panel keeps its `board.md` coordinates, and the strip's 48 px go to the log (and to the ticker when the panel is tall). Rejected: a placeholder strip of disabled buttons.
2. **Hotseat seat.** The screen always belongs to whoever owes the decision. A defence flips the board to the defender, whose hand stays hidden until they hold R. When play returns to the attacker, their hand stays hidden until they click or press Space. Rejected: keeping the attacker's view throughout, which leaves the attacker's hand on screen while the defender looks.
3. **Accessible names from content.** Cards and zones take their accessible name from visually hidden text instead of `aria-label`, because axe's label-in-name rule (WCAG 2.5.3) failed on the card art. Rejected: suppressing the rule.
4. **Tablet footer.** Rails of 280 px and narrower get an 80 px footer, so the primary button keeps its 136 px minimum and its labels fit (UX-V12). Rejected: shortening labels or dropping key hints.
